# PlusPlus — Decisions Log

The append-only architectural record. **Record new decisions here as they're
made** (same format as always: **Date — Decision — Reason**); root CLAUDE.md
keeps only the standing laws that every session needs. Read this file when
touching an area with history — the reason a thing is shaped the way it is
usually lives here.

> Record architectural and significant implementation decisions as they're made.
> Format: **Date — Decision — Reason**

**2026-08-05 (third) — Focusing the search input hides the bar's other content, reversing build 147** — Dave: focusing the field should hide the ++ key and the kit switcher so the input rises to the top of the view. That is the system's own `.automatic` `searchPresentationToolbarBehavior`, so the change is one word — but it undoes a call made deliberately at 147, and the reason it is now the right one is that **the bar it protected is not the bar we have**. On 147 the scope control ITSELF lived in that row, so letting search empty the row took the only way to change catalogs with it: a dead end, not a decluttering. Scoping moved to the system's scope bar earlier today, and that belongs to the search presentation rather than the toolbar. What clears now is two keys that each have a second door — the drawer also opens on a leading-edge drag from any tab root, and the kit is reachable from every catalog tab. It is written as an explicit `.automatic` rather than by deleting the modifier, so the next reader sees a decision instead of an omission.

⚠️ **This is in KNOWN TENSION with the scope bar, stated here because the device pass is about to resolve it either way.** The theory recorded this morning for why native `.searchScopes` might survive where it failed at 140–143 named two changes since those builds: the title going `.inline`, and `.avoidHidingContent` stopping the system from clearing the bar on activation. This reversal puts the second one back. So build 188 is a clean experiment: if the scope bar survives a focus/blur cycle, the content-keeping half was never load-bearing and the `.inline` title was; if it dies, the theory holds and the two asks are genuinely not co-satisfiable — the scope bar and the rising field cannot both have that row, and choosing between them is Dave's call, not a bug to fix. Either outcome is worth more than the guess it replaces, which is why it ships rather than being argued about first.

**2026-08-05 (later) — The search field moves to the TOP, and `Tab(role: .search)` is retired to let it** — Dave, after build 186: "is there a modifier we can pass to `searchable` to make the input be at the top of the view?" There is — `placement: .navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .always)` — but passing it alone would have been an argument the app could not win, which is the whole content of this decision.

**The morph IS the bottom placement.** `Tab(role: .search)` is a package: the system floats the tab apart as a separated circle AND morphs the tab bar into the search field at the bottom. Asking that role to put its field at the top asks it to be both things at once. The only recorded sighting of a top field on this surface is the morph FAILING (nav-diag 4e, an `.onGeometryChange` in the TabView subtree), and its symptom is precisely the double affordance: the circle stays in the bar and a field appears up top. So the choice was never "placement vs. role" — it was "top field" vs. "the morph", and Dave asked for the top field. `displayMode: .always` rather than the default: the automatic drawer collapses on scroll, and on the one surface where the field IS the point, a field that hides when the list moves is not a field.

**Build 135's homeless field is NOT what is being re-run.** That failure was a tab root that HID its navigation bar, leaving `.searchable` with nowhere to fall back to. Every root has worn the system bar since 2026-07-26, and a drawer is exactly what a navigation bar provides — the container the old failure lacked is now standing. Worth stating plainly because "plain tab + `.searchable` = invisible field" is the wrong lesson to carry out of 135, and it is the lesson that would stop anyone trying this.

**It also collapses the other half of the scope-bar problem, which is why this is the option worth trying first.** Native `.searchScopes` was retired at 140–143 as "renders exactly ONCE per app run, and at the TOP" — and "at the top" was only ever a complaint because the field was at the bottom. With the field in the drawer, the scope bar renders directly beneath the field it scopes, which is where a scope bar goes. Two open questions become one: whichever of the render-once theories is right, both now point the same way.

**What the role took with it.** The separated search circle in the tab bar, the bar→field morph, a system-supplied label and glyph (the app now states "Search" and `magnifyingglass` itself, and owns localizing them), and `.tabViewSearchActivation`, which only addresses a search-role tab — if the scope bar turns out to need search presented on arrival, the lever is `.searchFocused`, at the same cost 143 paid. ⚠️ Three laws were SCOPED, not repealed, and each is marked where it lives: the nav-diag 4e state-write hazard (the morph was the casualty that proved it, not its extent — `navigation.md`, `today-rail.md`, `TodayView`), the `Tab.hidden` reflow finding (139's motive died with the morph, its finding did not), and build 139's scroll-position retirement. A law whose exemplar dies needs re-pointing, not deleting — the 2026-08-02 fault.

⚠️ Both this and the scope-bar swap are UNVALIDATED and ride the same branch as separate commits, deliberately: build 187 ships from the branch so either can be reverted alone.

**2026-08-05 — Scoping goes back to the system: `.searchScopes` replaces the app-drawn segmented control, and the search surface's hand-laid bar row goes with it** — Dave asked for another shot at the native answer, doc in hand, knowing it was tried and retired at builds 140–143. `ScopeSegmentedControl.swift` is deleted; `.searchScopes($scope, activation: .onSearchPresentation)` rides the same `.searchable` that carries the field, inside `CatalogScopeView`'s `NavigationStack`, and the search surface's toolbar is the same leading-++/trailing-switcher arrangement as the other four roots.

**Why this is a re-try and not a re-run, which is the only thing that makes it worth Dave's device pass.** Native scopes failed 140–143 as "renders exactly ONCE per app run, and at the TOP", across four activation routes (`.onSearchPresentation`, `.searchable` moved inside the stack, a real navigation bar under it, and `.tabViewSearchActivation(.searchTabSelection)` so every arrival was a fresh presentation). Both modifiers the presentation carries TODAY landed AFTER those builds, and both are about the navigation bar the scope bar attaches to: the title went `.large` → `""` + `.inline` at 147 (it had been collapsing away as search presented), and `.searchPresentationToolbarBehavior(.avoidHidingContent)` arrived at 147 too — before it, the system's `.automatic` behaviour CLEARED the bar's content on activation, which is build 143's emptied top band, recorded at the time as a separate symptom. A scope bar attaching to a bar that is being emptied and re-laid out underneath it is the shape of a control that renders once and never again. That is a hypothesis, not a diagnosis: nobody re-ran 140–143 against the current bar, and the two facts could be unrelated. It is testable in one device pass, which is the whole point.

**What the swap buys, independent of whether the theory is right.** The segments carry WORDS again (`FindScope.label`) — the glyph-only rule was a consequence of the `.principal` slot, never of segmented controls: the platform limit is only that one segment cannot hold a title AND an image, and three words could not share a bar with the ++ key and a variable-width kit switcher. The hand-laid row's whole apparatus goes: the explicit `width - 32`, the `barGap`, the `.padding(.bottom, 4)` that seated the caps against `RaisedKeyStyle`'s travel, and the surface's own `GeometryReader`. That last one is the real gain — it was a pure read and legal, but it was a geometry read inside the TabView subtree (the documented morph-breaker's neighbourhood) and its closure re-ran the ranking pipeline on every size change. `RoutineDetailView.detailContent` is the live exemplar of a pure width read now, and navigation.md's citation moved with it; a law citing a deleted exemplar is the 2026-08-02 fault repeated.

**`activation: .onSearchPresentation`, not the iOS default.** The default is `.onTextEntry`, and an empty query is a first-class state on this surface — it browses the scope's whole list. A scope control that appears only once you type would be missing at exactly the moment you arrive wanting to change catalogs. ⚠️ The open question the device pass has to settle is whether ARRIVING on the search tab counts as a presentation at all, or whether the field must be tapped first; `.tabViewSearchActivation(.searchTabSelection)` is the lever if it doesn't, and it costs the arrive-without-the-keyboard behaviour Dave chose at 2026-07-26. The smoke helper tries the bar as it finds it and falls back to tapping the field, which keeps the flows running without pretending XCUITest can arbitrate this.

**The retirements that stand.** `tabViewBottomAccessory` (does not rise with the keyboard, 137–139/144; app-authored animation does not survive inside it, 138–139), a `.bottomBar` `ToolbarItem` (lands in the row the field expands into, 145), and a top `safeAreaInset` under the bar (one row too many, 147). And do not hand-roll a segmented control — iOS 26's interactive glass belongs to tab bars and segmented controls alone. ⚠️ **The fallback is `git revert`, not a rebuild**: the deleted control and its `.principal` row are one commit back, with their seven-build account in the diff.

**2026-08-02 — The catalog round's five follow-ups land together (#495–#499)** — every issue filed out of the 2026-07-31 expansion, done as one PR because they interlock: two of them needed the attribute enums to live somewhere new, and the file split had to come last or it would have churned under the other four.

1. **The three attribute enums moved into PlusPlusKit** (`ExerciseTaxonomy.swift`, beside `MuscleGroup`). They were app-side static data when only the app's catalog authored them; the swap ranker and the interchange both needed them, and they are pure value taxonomy, so Kit is where they belong. No behavior change — the app files already import Kit.
2. **#495, the swap ranker reads pattern AND mechanic.** The issue named movement pattern, but its own example (a Romanian Deadlift ranking a Leg Curl too high) is not separable by pattern: a Leg Curl carries none. Mechanic is what tells them apart, so both went in. Laterality stayed out — a unilateral variant is usually a fine swap for its bilateral twin, so it would be noise. ⚠️ **An absent attribute is UNAVAILABLE, not a mismatch**: it drops out of the weighted sum and its weight redistributes across the rest. Scoring nil as zero would push every cardio row, every stretch and every custom down the list for saying nothing; scoring it one would hand them free credit. ⚠️ **And the base three weights are the PRE-#495 ratios scaled by the 0.7 they now share**, so an attribute-less pair renormalizes back to exactly the old weights. The first cut of the numbers only looked like it held that — the Kit suite passed on margin, not on equality, while attribute-less pairs quietly reordered — so the invariant is now pinned by a test that recomputes the old formula rather than by `score(x, x) == 1`, which is true under any normalization (swift-reviewer).
3. **#496, customs carry their own attributes.** Three additive optional override columns on `Exercise`, resolved the way `muscleGroups` is (explicit wins, else catalog, else nothing), exported under the same explicit-state rule so an untouched built-in stays byte-identical. This closes a hole the filter row opened: a custom answered no attribute facet, so it silently vanished under three of the five chips. ⚠️ **nil means FOLLOW THE CATALOG, never "none"** — which is why the editor offers "Not set" only where the catalog is silent. On a built-in that already files as a hinge, that option would store nil, which resolves back to hinge; an option that cannot stick is worse than an absent one. The draft carries the RESOLVED value and stores only a genuine disagreement, so re-picking what the catalog says writes nothing.
4. **#497, Operator answers synonyms on READ paths only.** `find_items`, the two history resolvers and the did-you-mean suggestions reach the hidden terms; `ChangeEngine`'s write-target resolution stays exact-or-unique-substring. A hidden term should widen what the assistant finds and suggests, never what it silently edits. ⚠️ **A RESOLVER is not a ranker, and the first cut broke both halves of that** (swift-reviewer): it dropped `FuzzySearch.bestMatch`'s blank-query guard, so a question naming no exercise resolved to an arbitrary one and reported its number; and it scored a glued "name + hidden terms" candidate, which PENALIZES a row for having synonyms — `FuzzySearch`'s coverage term divides by the candidate's token count — so "bike" resolved to Cycling over Assault Bike and attributed the count to the wrong name. `CatalogSearchSynonyms.bestMatch` now guards blank and scores name and terms SEPARATELY, demoting a hidden-term hit to 0.75 so a literal hit always wins. The glued `searchKey` survives only where it is merely ordering a visible list.
5. **#498, facets multi-select through a tray.** Every facet with a real list of options holds a `Set` and opens `SheetPickList`; OR inside a facet, AND across them; an empty set is off. The two BINARY facets (mechanic, sides) keep their Menus deliberately — picking both `compound` and `isolation` says exactly what picking neither says, so a set there is ceremony around a toggle. This is the tray shape the single-select v1 named as its own successor, and `CatalogFilterState` moved to sets without the engine threading changing at all.
6. **#499, SeedData split three ways** — seeding + one-shot migrations (355 lines), `EquipmentCatalog.swift` (287), `ExerciseCatalog.swift` (690). Pure move, verified by diffing the declared-member list against the pre-split file. It forced exactly one visibility change: `builtInExerciseDefinitions` drops `private`, since `loadIfNeeded` tops up an existing store from it and that read now crosses a file.

Verified locally on Linux (621 Kit + 26 CLI, `DocsConformanceTests` included, both real compiles); the app target and the smoke suite are CI's. Device pass still owed on the facet trays and the editor's new MOVEMENT section.

**2026-08-02 (latest) — On the search surface the facet row starts in its pinned seat, and the hairline goes with the gap** — Dave, from a three-shot scroll sequence of the search tab's Kit scope: at rest "there's a gap above the filters", then scrolling slowly "a hairline line appears above them, but then goes away as they pin". They should sit in the pinned position always, and the line should never draw.

**Two faults, one cause, and the screenshots proved it before anything was changed** (pixel-measured at 3x, all three shots). The `Type` chip's top border sat at 142 pt at rest, 126 pt one shot later, and 120 pt in the third — while the list's own content moved 16 pt then 13.7 pt. The chip tracks the content exactly until the third shot, where the content moves 13.7 pt and the chip moves 6. **That is the row arriving at its pin**, at 120 pt, having started 22 pt below it. The navigation bar's scrolled-under hairline drew at 115.3 pt in the middle shot only — absent at rest (the bar is in its edge state at offset 0) and absent once pinned. 115.3 pt is 4 pt above the seated chip, which is `FacetChip`'s 44 pt hit frame around its 36 pt cap: the same 4 pt #521 measured off the previous mount's screenshots. So **the pinned row's opaque band lands exactly on the hairline and occludes it** — the line was only ever visible during the 22 pt in which the row had not yet arrived.

**The 22 pt is the `.plain` List's own top padding above its first section header, and it SCROLLS.** That is a fine thing on the four roots that wear the system large title, because the title travels through that space. The search surface deliberately carries NO title (2026-07-26), so the space holds nothing and the chips read as floating below the bar until you scroll them into place. Closed on the search surface only, via `listSectionSpacing(.custom(0))` + `contentMargins(.top, 0, for: .scrollContent)`; the other four pass `.default`/`nil` and are untouched. ⚠️ **Both knobs, deliberately**: the padding has been owned by either one across releases, a Linux session can observe neither, and zeroing an absent one is a no-op — cheaper than a device round trip to learn which.

**⚠️ The fix for the gap IS the fix for the hairline, and that is the part worth remembering.** No scroll-edge-effect modifier was added and none is wanted: seated from rest, the row covers the line at every offset above 0, and at 0 the bar is in its edge state and draws nothing. Suppressing the effect directly would have been the second answer to a symptom whose cause was 22 pt of padding — and the bottom edge's own history (`.hard` → hidden → `.soft`, 2026-07-26) is what that costs when you reach for the effect first. The third mount stands unchanged: the row is still the ONE section's header, still inside the list's own layout, still touching neither the safe area nor the nav bar.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; the change is pure layout and invisible to XCUITest. ⚠️ Device pass: docs/DEVICE-PASS.md — the search tab in all three scopes, at rest and scrolling slowly, plus the three catalog tabs to confirm their large titles still travel.

**2026-08-01 — The widget's tap starts the workout, by reusing a route rather than minting one; and two process calls from Dave** — #509's b25, plus answers to the two questions the round surfaced.

1. **b25 ships as a DEEP LINK, not an interactive button** (Dave's call, given the constraint below). The Due-today widget's tap now starts the scheduled routine instead of opening Today for you to find and tap the same card again. ⚠️ **The issue's premise was wrong in a way its checkbox hid**, which is why this needed a decision at all: "the intent already exists" is true — `StartRoutineIntent` is in `PlusPlus/Platform/PlusPlusIntents.swift`, the APP target — but `project.yml` gives the widget extension only `PlusPlusWidgets` + `PlusPlusShared`, so it cannot see the type, and `perform()` posts on `NotificationCenter`, which is in-process and would reach nobody from an extension. A `Button(intent:)` therefore means moving the intent and its `RoutineEntity` into a shared target and inventing a handoff — a cross-target change with App Intents registration consequences, for a tap that already has somewhere to go.
2. **What it reuses is the point.** `plusplus://start/<name>` already exists, is already built by `WorkoutCalendarLink.appURL(forRoutineNamed:)`, and is already handled in `RootTabView.onOpenURL`, which hands off to the same pathway Siri's intent uses (#333, the calendar event's start link). The widget inherits a route that is already exercised in production instead of a second one that isn't. A rest day keeps opening Today, and a name that fails to percent-encode falls back to Today rather than doing nothing — the tap always goes somewhere.
3. **`.claude/rules/interchange.md` is DEFERRED until after the TestFlight build** (Dave). The case for it is on the record and got stronger this week: #508's dupe bug was a hand-written target field list, and its review found the identical drift in `PlusPlus/Operator/ChangeInverse.swift`, already missing `heartRateTargetData` and unnoticed. The census law still lives only in `docs/PLATFORM.md`, which nothing auto-loads. Not now, with a branch in flight; revisit once the build ships.
4. **⚠️ The next batch of decisions gets written in SMALLER SLICES, closer to implementation** (Dave). This program was 26 decisions plus 28 bulk items, written 2026-07-31 and executed across a week of concurrent rounds — and the premise checks caught **four** items (b6, b10, b14, b25) whose specs no longer matched the code, each of which would have shipped a surface WORSE while closing a checkbox. The verify-before-implementing step worked, but it is a tax paid per item forever; writing a round's worth just before building it removes the cause instead. **The general lesson: a decision has a shelf life, and it expires against the code, not the calendar.**

**Validation.** Widget target, so CI is the verifier. ⚠️ Device pass in docs/DEVICE-PASS.md — the widget tap on a scheduled day, on a rest day, and with a punctuated routine name.

**2026-08-01 — Editor + kit round: a copy that dropped half of what it copied, and a rule the app only told VoiceOver (#508)** — the sixth decision-sheet round (b19, Q22-B, b13, b26, b27, b28, b1; b14 held back).

1. **DUPE dropped what it claimed to copy** (b19). `duplicateExercise` spelled its field list out by hand — weight, reps, repsUpper, duration, heart rate — and silently omitted `extraTargetsData` and the group's `restSecondsOverride`. Duplicating a configured cardio block lost its distance, pace and resistance plus whatever custom rest it ran, while the function's own doc comment said "with its targets". ⚠️ **Fixed at the cause, because the cause is a shape, not a typo:** the six fields now live in ONE `RoutineExercise.targets` accessor (get/set over the existing `Exercise.AddTimeTargets`, which already IS that list), and every site that fills an entry assigns it. A hand-written list drifts the moment a seventh field is added, and this one already had. Following #505's lesson I checked the other copies FIRST rather than after: `applyDefaultTargets` and `WorkoutSession`'s init both already carried the bag, and both fields are EXPORTED in the interchange, so the loss was confined to this one path. The mutation also moved to `Routine.duplicateExercise` beside `addExercise`/`replaceExercise`, with the synchronous save left at the interactive door (swiftdata.md: shared mutations also run in import/seed loops). **That move is the durable half of the fix** — the function was private to a `View`, which is exactly why nothing could test it and why the drift went unnoticed for as long as it did.
2. **Remove asks first** (Q22-B). One small tap in a compact pair permanently dropped the slot and everything configured on it, then auto-dismissed the sheet — so there was nothing left on screen to undo from. It confirms in the routine-delete shape now, naming the slot so the confirm can't be answered on muscle memory. ⚠️ An `alert`, not a `confirmationDialog`: this sheet is presented over a pushed screen, and a dialog raised from there adapts to a popover anchored to nothing (RoutineDetailView's own delete confirm carries that post-mortem).
3. **`setupMode` was two flags wearing one name** (b13): the guided Done-bar chrome, AND stripping equipment detail to add-and-configure by hiding its EXERCISES/ROUTINES graph. The drawer's "Edit your kit" passed it for the chrome and lost the graph as a side effect — most useful exactly when curating a kit you already have, which is the only time you reach that entry. Split via `CatalogScopeView.isFirstRunSetup` (`setupMode && !SetupState.equipmentDone`): once the equipment step is done, setup is over by definition. `EquipmentDetailScreen` stays a dumb flag; the caller decides, which is the right side of that line.
4. **The triad's eviction rule is visible now** (b26). Set a pace and the duration you entered quietly becomes the derived one — a real surprise, and the app told NOBODY: the `accessibilityHint` reads "Set it yourself instead", which describes the tap and never the eviction, so the rule lived only in a source comment. It is a caption under the triad now, once per CARD rather than per row (three copies under three rows would read as three rules), extracted with its chrome as `DerivedMetricCaption` so the two sheets that mount it can't drift. ⚠️ Two review corrections, both of which would have shipped the feature broken. It renders under the TRIAD and above the heart-rate row — placed after the `ForEach` it sat below heart rate, annotating a metric that is not part of the triad. And it is gated on the PROFILE, not on `derivedMetric != nil`: that gate hid the caption in exactly the state where the eviction fires, since a legacy entry with all three values stored has no derived metric yet and its next edit evicts one. **The rule is a property of the card, not of today's values.**
5. **Cancelling a browse-all swap returns to where it started** (b27). "Browse all exercises" is a detour inside the swap flow, not a new errand, but backing out of the catalog dropped you on routine detail with the sheet you were configuring gone. The slot is remembered when the swap picker opens and reopened on cancel; a pick clears it first, so only a genuine cancel returns. ⚠️ The reopen runs in the picker sheet's `onDismiss`, never under the live sheet — presenting while another presentation tears down is this codebase's documented drop class.
6. **b1 and b28**: `HistoryView.swift` → `SessionDetailView.swift` (deferred out of #482 to dodge a conflict; no Swift references existed, the types were already named for the record). And TodayView's `createRoutine` comment was stale twice over — `RoutineListView` was replaced by `CatalogScopeView`, and that create row LANDS on the list with the entrance flash rather than pushing. Blank creation pushing to detail is the deliberate other half of the landing law, so the comment now says which half it is.

**⚠️ b14 is HELD BACK, and the reasoning is recorded on the issue.** It asked the swap sheet's empty state to read "No kit-doable alternative found". `ExerciseFilterState.swapSuggestions` PARTITIONS kit-doable-first and hides nothing; the intro already says so; the empty state already names the true reason ("No similar moves in the catalog yet."); and Browse-all always renders. The proposed string would tell the user their kit filtered the list when it only sorted it — contradicting #113's flag-don't-hide law, and a worse fault than the quiet menu it was written against. Its cited file had also been rebuilt as `ExerciseSwapTray`.

**⚠️ The pattern, now that it is three (b6, b10, b14): the decision sheet was written 2026-07-31 against surfaces that later rounds then rebuilt.** Each held item was caught by verifying the premise before implementing rather than after, and each would otherwise have shipped a change that made a surface WORSE while closing a checkbox. #509 is the last round: re-verify its items against current code first.

**Review found six more, and three would have shipped broken.** Beyond b26's two above: b27 cleared its return target AFTER a guard that can return early, so a slot deleted while the picker was up reopened an EMPTY sheet dismissable only by drag (the clear moved ahead of the guard, and the sheet builder gained a real fallback — `.sheet(item:)` presents whether or not its builder yields content). b13 had decoupled ONE of `setupMode`'s three effects: the "adds land in ⟨kit⟩" switcher was still hidden by the same argument the graph was, and the Done bar called `markEquipmentDone()` unconditionally, moving an established user's setup-milestone DATE to today. `isFirstRunSetup` also read UserDefaults unobserved in a body — now `@AppStorage`, matching `RootTabView`. `duplicateExercise` took a `group` parameter nothing checked against the entry; it derives it now, because a model API with tests is a thing other callers will reach for. ⚠️ And Q22-B was HALF applied: the VoiceOver "Delete" custom action still destroyed a slot in one activation, which is the closest analogue there is to the one small tap the alert was added for. It routes through the same confirm; the SWIPE keeps its directness, since a two-stage drag is its own confirmation.

**⚠️ The same drift the round exists to kill was found one file away, already missing a field** (review): `EntryTargetsSnapshot` in the Operator's undo hand-wrote five of the six target fields and had **never carried `heartRateTargetData`**. Latent, not firing — no tracked mutation clears a heart-rate target today — but the first one that does would have had its undo silently keep the clear. It is backed by `Exercise.AddTimeTargets` now, so the list nobody can forget to extend covers the undo too. **Finding the second copy is the point: #505 taught that this codebase's copies drift, and looking for them is now part of fixing one.**

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; every changed file parse-checked locally. Four tests pin the dupe contract (every target field including the bag, the rest override, a nil override staying nil, and the accessor's round trip). ⚠️ Honest about them: they are GUARDS, not red-first proofs — none could have failed against the old code, because `Routine.duplicateExercise` did not exist, and that absence WAS the root cause. Moving the mutation out of the view is what the red-first law was really asking for here. ⚠️ Device pass: docs/DEVICE-PASS.md carries the checks.

**2026-08-01 — The week band sits above the WHOLE timeline, and moving it cost three invariants the old placement was providing silently** — Dave, on build 163 with a screenshot: the bar rendered between the week-ahead cards and the anytime entry. "It should always sit fully above the timeline, including future items."

**The cause is one sentence, and it was not a regression anyone introduced carelessly.** #530 (build 162) made the band the timeline's pinned `Section` header — the right mount, for the right reason (a top `safeAreaInset` costs the system large title, measured on a `List` and a `ScrollView` alike). But **a section header renders where its section BEGINS**, and the week-ahead block sat OUTSIDE that section, in an eager `VStack` above the `LazyVStack`, because #267 put it there: a `LazyVStack` sizes unrealized children approximately, and the opening `scrollTo` aims at an anchor below that block, so laziness above the anchor lands the landing off by the estimation error. Two correct decisions, taken months apart, composing into chrome wedged into the middle of the rail.

**The fix is to move the block inside the section, and the block stays eager** — as ONE `VStack` child, so it is a single realized `LazyVStack` child laid out in full with an exact height. Review confirmed that guarantee survives: laziness is per-CHILD, and a realized child reports its true height, so #267's hazard is not reintroduced.

**⚠️ What the move DID cost is the entry's real content: three invariants the old placement was providing by construction rather than by intent.** All three were found by review, none is visible to CI, and each would have reached Dave's device.

1. **The landing seated the anytime row BEHIND the band.** `ScrollViewProxy.scrollTo(anchor: .top)` aligns to the visible content top and knows nothing about a pinned header. The old placement escaped this only because the section BEGAN at the anchor, which made the band the first thing on screen by construction. Once the band pins above content that starts higher, a bottom-seated anchor is covered by exactly the band's height. The anchor is now a zero-LAYOUT overlay held one band-height above the block's bottom — an overlay reserves no space, so nothing moves at rest, and `scrollTo` aligning its top leaves exactly that gap. ⚠️ The height is DERIVED from `UIFont` metrics mirroring `weekStrip`'s own gating and constants, never probed: a state-writing geometry probe anywhere in the TabView subtree is the documented iOS 26 search-morph trigger. Change `weekStrip`'s constants, change `bandHeight` — the same "change one, change the other" rule the shelf overlay already carries.
2. **The below-anchor `minHeight` stopped bounding the below-anchor region.** `.frame(minHeight: viewport)` sat on the `LazyVStack`, which used to hold ONLY below-anchor content. With the band header and the week ahead inside that same stack, the min-height is consumed by content ABOVE the anchor and guarantees nothing below it — so on a short timeline (one scheduled routine, a young history: exactly Dave's TestFlight state) today can no longer scroll to the top and the week ahead sits in the upper third at rest. The frame moved onto a NESTED `LazyVStack` wrapping the below-anchor content. Nested and lazy, deliberately: an eager stack there would reinstate the O(sessions) render the bug hunt killed.
3. **The opening `scrollTo` could silently skip, permanently.** Both entry points set the one-shot `hasAnchoredToday` and THEN scroll. `scrollTo` against an id that does not currently exist in the hierarchy is a no-op, and the anchor now lives in a lazy container rather than the eager outer stack — so if the container had not created its first child yet, the landing was skipped for that appearance with no retry, because both guards read the flag already set. Both are deferred a runloop now, matching the three re-anchors that already were.

**A note on how this was recorded, because the doc was briefly wrong.** The first pass filed the landing overlap in navigation.md as a "⚠️ device-only consequence to watch". Review's traced geometry showed it is deterministic, not a risk — and **a law that records a known-broken behaviour as a device check is worse than no law**, because the next reader takes it as a shipped shape. It reads as the rule now, with the overlay as its answer. The pending checks that ARE genuinely device-only moved to docs/DEVICE-PASS.md, which is what that file is for.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; parse-checked locally. ⚠️ Device pass, all three of the above: the anytime row landing flush below the band on a cold open (a wrong `bandHeight` shows exactly here), a SHORT timeline still scrolling today to the top, and the pull — the band now starts at content y = 0 for the first time, so build 152's ghost has a new adjacency.

**2026-08-01 — The agent docs get a drain, not another compression pass: the device-pass queue moves to its own file and navigation.md is audited claim-by-claim** — Dave, asked whether to split reference files out of CLAUDE.md: "Do whatever you think is best." And on navigation.md: "Audit it and trim the fat."

**The diagnosis came before the fix, and it changed the fix.** CLAUDE.md had been compressed three times (#488, #501, #522) and was back at 25,460 of 25,600 bytes within seven rounds each time. Measuring it rather than assuming showed why: the **device-pass queue was 8,367 bytes across 18 lines — a third of the whole file** — and it is the one section with no drain. Every round appends a line; nothing is deleted until Dave passes a build on device, and the one-build-after-the-rounds decision meant nothing had drained since 159. The reference material everyone kept reaching for was already gone. ⚠️ **The lesson, recorded in the Hygiene section: when a budget binds, look for the section that GROWS, not the one that is biggest. Compression is what you do to a section that is finished; relocation is what you do to one that isn't.**

**The queue is now `docs/DEVICE-PASS.md`** — same one-item-per-line format (load-bearing: git merges line by line, and every round appends from a different branch), newest first, each with its ⚠️ rider. CLAUDE.md keeps a pointer, the count, and the instruction to read it before touching a surface it names. The test that decided it: **would a session need this BEFORE knowing what it was about to touch?** The queue fails that test twice over — it is read by a different person (Dave) at a different moment (device-pass time) than the standing laws around it. CLAUDE.md went 25,460 → 17,854 bytes.

**Not converted to skills or agents, and the reason is structural.** `.claude/rules/` already IS on-demand path-scoped loading, and it is where path-scoped laws belong. Skills are procedures invoked by name — the one plausible candidate was the Claude Code Setup catalog, but **an index of capabilities cannot sit behind on-demand loading, because discovery is its whole job**: a session that does not know `swift-reviewer` exists will not invoke a skill to find out. Agents are workers, not documents. What DID come out of that section is the Axiom plugin's domain enumeration — the session's own agent listing names every one of them with its description, so a second copy in CLAUDE.md could only go stale. What stayed is the part that listing cannot tell you: which half runs in a remote Linux session, and that an ephemeral container installs it from a `SessionStart` hook.

**`docs/DEVICE-PASS.md` joins the LINE-length cap but not the size cap.** A queue's length is a function of how much unvalidated work is outstanding, not of documentation hygiene — capping its size would just push a real backlog somewhere invisible. But it is written one-item-per-line for the merge reason, so the line cap is exactly the guard it needs. `docs/DECISIONS.md` stays exempt from both.

**navigation.md: audited claim-by-claim (doc-verifier), and the audit paid for itself in falsehoods, not bytes.** It went over budget by taking main's newer build-162 band law in the merge, which forced the audit early. Three claims were wrong:

1. **The no-state-writes-during-layout law cited an exemplar that violates it.** "Measure from `UIFont` metrics instead, as `OverflowCapsuleRow` does" — `OverflowCapsuleRow` writes `@State` from a `GeometryReader` and renders inside catalog rows, i.e. inside the TabView subtree the law is about; only its TAG widths come from `UIFont`. The live exemplar is `ScopeSegmentedControl`'s pure width read, which the same file already documents 120 lines down. A wrong exemplar under a correct law is worse than no exemplar: it is the thing that gets copied.
2. **The facet row's own law had lost KIND** (it leads the exercises facets, as of #494) and called the kit facet "category" when the code and every sibling doc say "type".
3. **A cross-reference pointed the wrong way** — "mechanism and cost in the pinning law above" when the pinning law is below, and restating it there duplicated the Today band law's evidence in full.

The trims that followed were all provenance rather than law: how the principal-item asymmetry was measured, which builds produced which screenshot, a forums thread number, and API trivia for a bottom accessory that no longer exists. Every "don't re-try X, it failed in build N" stayed — that is a law, and the file's header says so. One law was MOVED rather than trimmed: a raised key's cap and hit target being one rectangle is key anatomy, so it now lives in design-grammar.md's key family beside the rest. navigation.md 24,734 → 23,862; a split at the catalog/search seam was considered and is not needed yet.

**Validation.** Docs and one CI workflow line; the Swift diff in this PR is #507's. `kit-test`'s budget step is the verifier and now covers the new file's line length.

**2026-08-01 — The week band owns Today's one sticky slot: a top `safeAreaInset` costs the large title, and nothing may usurp the band** — Dave, on build 162: "the Today heading is hidden. Seems like something may be rendering in front of it. It shows up at the top upon scrolling down, but is invisible when scrolled to the top."

Nothing is in front of it. The title is not drawn at all: a top `safeAreaInset` shifts the scroll's resting offset by the band's own height, and the system bar's title math reads that as already-scrolled — so at rest the reserved title zone is empty, and the INLINE title appears only once real scrolling begins. **This is #521 exactly**, which measured it on the catalogs' `List`, moved their facet row out of the inset, and left a note saying Today's `ScrollView` was unproven and the device pass should check the title at rest. Same bug, second surface. The pinned-inset mount is now RETIRED app-wide rather than carried as a hazard.

The band pins as a **`Section` header inside the scroll** instead — the same mount the catalogs' facet row uses, and it works for the same reason: a header lives in the scroll's own layout, where the navigation bar never sees it.

⚠️ **A scroll gets exactly ONE sticky header, and the band gets it** (Dave, immediately after the first cut handed the pin off to history's month landmarks: "the band must pin at the top and not be usurped by anything else. Pin other stuff below it as necessary"). SwiftUI has no stacked pinning — a second `Section` does not pin BELOW the first, it REPLACES it — so the only faithful reading is that the band is the surface's sole `Section` and everything else rides as plain rows. #506's month landmarks are demoted accordingly: they still name the month you are reading, they just travel with the rail. If stacked pinning is ever wanted, it needs a mechanism that does not exist here yet; do not reach for a second `Section` to get it.

Two details fall out. The band's section holds the whole timeline BELOW the landing, so it stays pinned all the way down while the anchor sits just above it — which keeps `scrollTo(.top)` seating the anytime row with the band pinned directly beneath, as the inset did for free. And the present entries moved into `presentEntries(viewportHeight:)`: this timeline stack is at the type-checker's budget, and a `Section` wrapping four inline entries is exactly what tips it, as #506's own comment warned.

⚠️ Unchanged and still owed a device pass: build 152's ghost (the pull walking the large title over pinned chrome) and the pull's answer line rendering in the gap.

**2026-08-01 — Catalog round: the filters say what they hid, hand-built routines stop vanishing, and one verb across three create rows (#507)** — the fifth decision-sheet round (Q13-A, Q14-A, bulk b8/b9/b11/b12; b10 held back).

1. **A facet that hides a match now SAYS so** (Q13-A). The exact-name collision guard checks the UNFILTERED set, so an exact match can never hide behind a create row — but a PARTIAL one could: "bench" with Kind=Cardio active hid Bench Press and offered Create "Bench", which is the easy path to a near-duplicate exercise. An "N more exercises hidden by filters · show" `QuietKey` now sits ABOVE the create row (what is hidden has to read before the offer to create, not after it), tapping clears the facets, and the empty state names the filters as the reason when they provably are one. ⚠️ It is a plain row inside the list's ONE section, NOT a second pinned header — the facet row spends that single pin (2026-08-01, third mount), and `.listStyle(.plain)` pins one header at a time.
2. **The count had to be FREE, and that reshaped the engine.** The first implementation derived it as `unfilteredCount() - shown`, and the trap is that it LOOKS cheap. `unfilteredCount()` genuinely was a second full ranking pass, but it was a CLOSURE the summary popover fired on OPEN — so it cost nothing while you typed, which is exactly why it was written as one. **A row in the list cannot defer.** Reading it there moves that pass into the render path, per keystroke, for a question nobody asked — the cost the per-scope match counts were retired over (2026-07-25). ⚠️ Getting the framing right matters more than the fix: "a second ranking pass per keystroke" was never true of the code being replaced, and a comment saying so would teach the wrong lesson to whoever reads it next (swift-reviewer). `FindOrCreateEngine.outcome(...)` returns the sections AND the hidden count from ONE pass: each candidate is SCORED first and classified by facet SECOND, so an excluded match is counted where it was already being examined. Output is unchanged by the reorder (scoring is per-item pure, so filtering before or after yields the same set in the same order), the deferred pass and its closure both delete, and the popover's "N of M shown" prints `shown + hidden`. ⚠️ It is not free in the other direction, and that is recorded rather than hidden: with a facet active a keystroke now scores every candidate rather than the surviving subset. That is the work an unfiltered pass has always done, and it is the price of being able to say what was hidden.
3. **A hand-built routine under Effort or Style groups as "not rated"** (Q14-A) instead of vanishing. Those two facets resolve only through `routine.catalogTemplate` (effort was declared non-derivable 2026-07-20 — a display-era call, not re-litigated), so a from-scratch library EMPTIED its MINE tier under them: the flag-don't-hide law's own failure, in a corner nobody had looked at. New `.unrated` `Section.Kind`, rendered through `MissingEquipmentHeaderRow`'s `sentence:` override — same collapsible shape, same law (narrowed, never vanished), describing the ROUTINES and never the user. It sits after BOTH tiers rather than inside MINE: it is not a narrower slice of yours, it is the rows the facet has no opinion about, and splitting it per tier would print the disclosure twice for a distinction only hand-built routines can have. It still clears every facet it CAN answer (`allowsIgnoringRating` forgives only the two that route through `catalogTemplate`).
4. **The create-row verbs converged** (b12): the verb PREDICTS the tap — Create COMMITS inline, Add OPENS something. Routines read `New routine "<query>"` (neither of the law's two forms) and Kit read `Add "<query>" as equipment`; Kit was the one row in the app that said Add and committed anyway, while the live Add consumers ("Add to routine…", the session overview's "Add exercise") both open a picker. All three catalogs now read `New <object>` empty and `Create "<query>"` queried. The Kit row's second act — joining the active kit — is carried by the landing, which puts the new piece in MINE with the entrance flash.
5. **Smaller items.** b8: `ExerciseEditorView(prefillMuscleGroup:)` had existed with ZERO call sites, so a create under an active Muscle facet still defaulted to Chest and landed outside the very facet you were browsing; `filters.muscle` threads through now (the only facet that maps to a draft field). b9: "Create X" on the bodyweight-only kit can't create (the null kit is immutable), so it redirects to the kit tray — which used to arrive with no connection to the tap that summoned it, and now carries an amber reason line. ⚠️ Every OTHER route into that tray clears the reason, so a later deliberate open can't inherit a stale explanation. b11: an arrival expands only its OWN missing-equipment tier — a landing is always something you just made, so it is always MINE, and opening CATALOG's group too dumped a wall of unrelated rows on screen at the moment the eye was going to the flash.
6. **Four corrections from review, three of them copy.** The hidden-by-filters row is QUERIED lists only — with no query the create row reads "New exercise" and opens the editor, so there is no near-duplicate to guard against, and the summary chip's "N of M shown" is already saying the same thing one row above; unqualified, it was a permanently-present second copy of that fact over every browse. "equipment" is a MASS noun and cannot take a bare numeral ("3 more equipment"), so the kit scope counts in **pieces** — the app's own countable word (the live session's "piece 3", the resolve sheet's "add the piece"); `searchNoun` keeps the mass noun for prompts, where nothing counts. The b9 redirect line named a kit that exists under NO SUCH NAME ("Bodyweight only …") — the null kit is literally named `null`, which is the only string the switcher has ever shown for it — so it borrows `kitHint`'s established sentence verbatim and the app keeps ONE account of what null is. And the unrated sentence says nothing about WHO made the routines: "routines you built" is false for an imported one (`ShareImportSheet` also produces routines with no `catalogTemplate`), and provenance was never the point.
7. **b10 (the facet row's trailing fade) is HELD BACK**, on the issue's own condition ("device-verify the overflow first"). That the row overflows is provable — five chips plus a summary chip do not fit a phone — but whether a fade helps, or whether the half-cut chip already reads as scrollable, is a judgment that needs glass. A mask over an opaque pinned band is also the kind of change that fades the chip you are reaching for. The checkbox stays open.

**⚠️ navigation.md is at 24,394 of its 24,576-byte budget** after this round's laws, with the post-mortems already compressed twice. The next session that adds a law there has to split the file by path scope (the catalog/search sections are the natural seam) rather than compress again — flagged for Dave, since it restructures a shared agent doc.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; nothing in Kit or CLI moved. Six new engine tests pin the hidden-count contract (query misses are not counted, unrated rows are shown not hidden, templates that fail a facet are, and `shown + hidden == unfiltered` — the identity the popover now prints), and `routineFacets` was rewritten to the new unrated behaviour. ⚠️ Device pass: the hidden-by-filters key appearing and clearing under each scope's chips, the "not rated" group under Effort on a from-scratch library, and the bodyweight redirect's reason line.

**2026-08-01 — Record round: month landmarks, what the plan asked, and a run that states its headline once (#506)** — the fourth decision-sheet round (Q11-A, Q12-A, bulk b6).

1. **Committed history carries MONTH landmarks** (Q11-A) — one flat newest-first run had nothing to orient by at depth. ⚠️ The issue's implementation hint is STALE and the divergence is the entry's point: it named the week strip's sticky-band `visualEffect` mechanics, which builds 159/160 DELETED (the band is pinned chrome on the scroll's shell now), and a render-time offset could not hand off between successive headers even if it survived. The honest mechanic is what the search results already use: real `Section`s with `LazyVStack(pinnedViews: [.sectionHeaders])`, keyed year+month so a December→January crossing never folds two years together. The label is lowercase mono like every other date line — the rail's all-caps headings stay dead (2026-07-23 round 2b); a month is a DATE, and it speaks the datelines' grammar — with the year only when it isn't this one. Two riders the pin needs: the spine draws THROUGH the header (an opaque band across the rail would read as a broken line), and its background bleeds past the stack's 16 pt column (rows slide UNDER a pinned header, and the gutters would show them through — the sticky-band law's own trap, inherited).
2. **The record answers "did I hit what the plan asked?"** (Q12-A): `SetLog` has carried that day's targets since sessions began snapshotting, and no record surface read them. Each completed set now prints a faint `target:` line under its result, in the shared `MetricSummary` vocabulary so the reference reads exactly like the prescription did. ⚠️ FLAT ink, ALWAYS shown when a target exists — not just on a miss. The direction inks (2026-07-30) mark a PLAN's movement against a performance, never a performance against its plan; and a line that appeared only when you fell short would make its own PRESENCE the judgment the flat ink exists to avoid. An untargeted effort prints nothing rather than "target: —". The label is the app's OWN noun for a prescription (the TARGETS section, "Target heart rate"); the issue's shorthand "asked:" personifies a plan, which is the closest the voice gets to a first person (swift-reviewer).
3. **b6 (the run de-dupe) is HELD BACK, and the evidence is the entry** — the issue conditioned it on verifying the wording first ("medium confidence that the two lines may read as reinforcing rather than redundant"), and verification says reinforcing. `RunRecordSection`'s stat line prints distance · **pace** · elevation, with **no time**; the set row prints distance · **duration** · pace, plus (as of item 2) the `target:` reference. Filtering the row out deleted the effort's only second-precision duration AND the prescription this very round exists to surface — worst on exactly the targeted runs where both matter most. The first implementation was also per-block, so a run + outdoor-walk cooldown lost BOTH rows and the record showed a stat line over nothing (swift-reviewer). Making the row honestly redundant means moving duration into the stat line first, which is a `RunRecordSection` change and its own decision, not a filter. #506's b6 checkbox stays open with this reasoning on the issue.

**Deliberately NOT in scope**, per the decision sheet: an in-app streak surface — the streak stays widget/Operator-only.

**2026-08-01 — Build-160 device pass: the rack wraps, the pinned chips find their third mount, and a date row never stands alone** — six fixes off Dave's first pass on the anytime rail, five cosmetic-looking and one structural.

1. **The rack WRAPS, and the fit machinery is deleted.** "Train" rendered as "Tr…" beside a "2 more" key — the greedy `UIFont` fit measured the row BEFORE the card's own 12 pt pads, so it always believed it had 24 pt it did not have and packed one key too many. The fix is not a better estimate: `FlowLayout` measures each key and wraps, so every pick is a full key on however many rows it takes, and the "N more" overflow (plus its panel, plus the `GeometryReader` that fed the fit — a state write during layout, the class navigation.md bans in the TabView subtree) all delete with it. **Estimating what a layout can measure was the bug.** Keys take a fixed 42 pt height so an SF Symbol's taller line box can't stagger a wrapped row, a `minimumScaleFactor` guards a very long custom name, and the card pads equally on all four sides (Dave: "the padding inside the dashed region should be equal on all sides" — the raised key's plate is visible ink under its cap, so the key's FRAME is its visual box and one pad value reads even).
2. **The offer node goes SOLID.** A dashed 18 pt circle of 2.6 pt dashes read as a rendering fault rather than a grammar. The card's own dashed border carries "offer"; the dot just marks the row.
3. **A date row NEVER stands alone.** Carried-over work suppresses the rest-day card (2026-07-14), which left "today · sat · aug 1" hanging over the amber lane with nothing beneath it — the gate was on the group's CARDS, not on the entry. Now the whole `TimelineItem` is gated: today earns a row when it has something to show, and the carried entries below carry their own dates.
4. **The carried lane drops its "was" prefix.** Every other entry prints a bare date, and a past date under an amber node beside an amber card is already past tense — the word was carrying tense that position had covered since the dates left the cards.
5. **A raised key's cap and its hit target are ONE rectangle.** The panel's back key shipped as a 34 pt cap inside a 44 pt hit frame, and `RaisedKeyStyle` plates the frame it is GIVEN — so the plate drew a second, larger rounded rect around the small cap (the broken double-box in Dave's screenshot). It wears `HeaderIconButton`'s anatomy verbatim now: one 44 pt frame carrying fill and border, then `.raisedKey()`.
6. **The facet row finds its THIRD mount: the list's one pinned section header.** Dave: the chips "aren't sticky, they're scrolling out the top of the viewport." Both prior mounts are ruled out — a top `safeAreaInset` (#494) desynced the system large-title bar (#521, measured), and #521's fix (first list CONTENT) kept the title honest but let the chips scroll away. `.listStyle(.plain)` pins the CURRENT section's header, so one section wrapping the whole list makes its header pin for the entire scroll, and a section header lives inside the list's own layout — it touches neither the safe area nor the nav bar, which is why it can satisfy both constraints at once. ⚠️ The cost, accepted and recorded: MINE/CATALOG tier labels become plain rows and stop pinning (only one header pins at a time, and the chips have to outlive the tier boundaries — a tier label is a divider you read once, the chips are a control you reach for at any depth). Presented and picker surfaces keep the top inset: their chrome is app-drawn, so no large-title machinery runs on them.

⚠️ Device pass on this round: the wrapped rack at both Dynamic Type extremes (the card grows taller with every pick — a 6-sport rack is three rows), the chips actually pinning under a working large title on all three tabs, and the pull check that predates it.

**2026-08-01 — Quick start round: sport-named sessions, an earlier reveal, and freestyle as a first-class completion (#505)** — the third decision-sheet round (Q9-A, Q25-A, Q26-A, bulk b7, the #520 rider), reconciled against #502's rebuilt Today surface before any code.

1. **Q5-B (instant-feeling config sheet) is DELIVERED, not implemented here.** The decision predates #502, which rebuilt quick start as the anytime card's morphing panels: last outing, preset chips, Open default and a green Start all sit IN the panel, and `ExerciseConfigSheet` survives only as the "Custom…" escape — whose commit key is already pinned below the scroll (thumb-reach, no scrolling to commit) and whose prefill already rides #187's add-time resolution (own bumped default → catalog assignment → floor). The "one tap" comment the issue wanted corrected died with the #502 rewrite. Adding a second per-exercise memory layer on top of #187 would be the premature-abstraction class; declared superseded instead.
2. **Quick-started sessions are named for their sport** (Q9-A): `startQuick` seeds `routineName` from the exercise before the first append — a week of runs read as identical "Scratch workout" cards forever, and the Δ matcher paired every scratch session by that one shared name. The guards that land WITH the naming were reshaped by review, and the reshaping is the entry's real lesson:
   - **The quick-start marker is EXPLICIT** (`WorkoutSession.isQuickStart`, constant-default additive — census: EXCLUDED, restore degrades to unmarked, accepted over an interchange contract change riding this round). Every proxy failed review: `routine == nil` is ALSO every wrist import (no import path links the relationship — a scheduled one-step cardio routine run from the wrist would have stayed due forever, the catalog's own Steady Run shape), and single-effort-ness changes when core work joins the run. Phone quick starts stamp it at `startQuick`; wrist ones at `materialize`, by RECOMPUTING the deterministic quick-start plan identity (`WatchBridge.quickStartIdentity`) against the incoming uuid — no wire change; an old uuid-less watch degrades to unmarked.
   - **The completion pool is ONE function now** (`WorkoutSession.completions(of:in:)` + `recentCompletionDates`): review found FOUR hand-rolled copies (Today's rail, the tab icon's `TodayStatus`, the widget snapshot writer, the recap's next-up) already drifted into three variants — my guard had landed in one, so the rail and the icon would have disagreed by construction. The shared rule: identity wins; the name fallback requires `routine == nil` (a LIVE reference to a different same-named routine is that routine's performance — the nuance ONE copy had and three lacked) and excludes `isQuickStart`. `CompletionPoolTests` pins all three rules, including the wrist-import case the first guard draft broke.
   - **The Δ header's name fallback gates on sharing ≥1 exercise** (b7), and the committed card's `netGain` wears the same gate so the card and the record resolve the SAME previous session; a quick-start record's `linkedRoutine` is nil — "the routine this record links back to" must never be one it didn't come from.
3. **The anytime entry reveals once the KIT is settled** (Q25-A): the gate drops from `allSetupDone` to `equipmentStepDone` — step 1 keeps the landing's focus, and from step 2 on a spontaneous run is a valid second door; a committed session dissolves the scaffold naturally.
4. **"Train freestyle" completes the schedule step** (Q26-A): no-schedule is a valid choice by the anti-obligation stance, and the step now models equipment's #232 answer — its own stored flag (`SetupState.trainingFreestyle`, written via `@AppStorage` so the scaffold re-renders), ORed into `scheduleStepDone` AND into `TodayStatus.current` (review: without the second, the freestyle user's tab icon sat on the to-do clock indefinitely while the timeline read 3 of 3 done — the parameter defaults false so the test fixtures keep their meaning). The door is a `QuietKey` under the step's CTA (`SetupRow.quietCTA` — the escape-hatch grammar, inside the card so the rail's inset comes free); done reads "Training freestyle · No set days. Workouts land here as you do them." and a later real schedule takes the title back. Phrased as fully valid, never as skipping. Copy-reviewer passed all three strings clean.

⚠️ Scaffold behavior (reveal timing, the freestyle door, freestyle-done rendering) needs a fresh-install device pass.

**2026-08-01 — Live logging round: the value wears input chrome, and a done block is a correction door (#504)** — the second decision-sheet round (Q7-A, Q8-B, bulk b4/b5).

1. **The metric card's big value wears `MetricStepperRow`'s outlined-input treatment** (Q7-A): the most-tapped value in the app (it opens the whole scrubber) rendered as bare mono text — the exact discoverability miss Dave caught on device 2026-07-16 and fixed on the stepper row; the fix never crossed to the card. Same grammar (r8 `Theme.border` stroke, horizontal-12 padding), stretched full-width so a short numeral keeps the same wide target, ≥52 pt tall (the 44 pt floor with the numeral's own bulk); the "prev:" annotation stays OUTSIDE the outline — a fact beside an input, not part of it.
2. **A completed BlockBar segment is a correction DOOR, never a jump** (Q8-B; Dave verbatim: "Don't jump to redo unexpectedly, but make redo an option - or perhaps frame it as a way to make corrections"). Fixing a fat-thumbed set cost Overview → block → Redo → re-enter; now the bar's done blocks answer a tap with a confirmation dialog carrying the set's logged values ("Logged 135 lb × 8…") and an explicit action in the block's own noun ("Correct set 2" / "Correct piece 3" — `WorkUnit.rowLabel`, the vocabulary law), which runs the overview's exact redo mechanics (`jump(to:redo:)` + the #512 `reopened`/`cursorMoved` emits + rest ends). Fire-time re-checks (the one-shot deferred rule) cover a set reopened or a session finished while the dialog stood. A 9 pt block sits far under the 44 pt floor, so the hit shape inflates by negative inset (MetricStepperRow's precedent); `BlockBar.onCorrect` is nil at every other mount, leaving them inert. ⚠️ VoiceOver deliberately keeps the bar as ONE summary element — the overview's Redo is the accessible correction route.
3. **`HoldRepeatKey` finally shows its repeating state** (b4): the hold-to-repeat mechanism had zero on-screen echo — a held key looked exactly like a resting one while the value climbed. While repeating the cap wears the raised fill and a data-green stroke (green is data in motion), dropping on release AND on cancel (`endRepeat` owns the reset, so the scroll-steal path clears it too).
4. **Self-reported cardio seeds the duration scrub from the session's own clock** (b5): on a `.selfReported` hero (indoor console sports) the phone timed the effort precisely (`session.effortElapsed`) yet offered a blank tape. The wheel's binding now seeds an untouched duration with the elapsed — a SUGGESTION in the binding's get, never a write: nothing lands on the log until the user commits, and an entered actual or a target always wins.

Review round (swift-reviewer, all applied): the value box's sibling `Spacer` was deleted — two infinitely-flexible HStack children split the slack EQUALLY, closing the "full-width" outline mid-card; the hit inflation went ASYMMETRIC (`BlockHitInflation`: sides ±1.5 = half the bar's spacing, 12 up / 24 down = 45 pt) — a uniform −17 overlapped neighbors by 31 pt so the right third of every done block opened its NEIGHBOR's dialog, and reached into the header band; every block wears the Button at ONE identity (hit-gated by `allowsHitTesting`) so the green→purple roll stays a color animation instead of a remount; and the dialog's title/message read STRINGS captured at tap time with the action's guard isDeleted-FIRST — a wrist discard can delete the session under an open dialog, and the title closure re-runs on every body pass. Verified clean: BlockBar's equal-width distribution survives the Button wrap, the scrubber copies its binding once at init (the climbing elapsed can't fight the scrub), and the beat/finish races are closed from both sides.

⚠️ Segment taps and the hold echo are XCUITest-invisible (gesture/visual layers) — device pass with the rest of the 2026-08-01 run.

**2026-08-01 — Workout lifecycle correctness: every finish door is the same door, and the single-effort key slides (#503)** — the first decision-sheet implementation round (Q2-A, Q3-A, and Dave's Q4 follow-up answer "Swipe to finish").

1. **The exit dialog's Finish routes through the recap** — it was the ONE finish path leaving `finishSession`'s default `dismissAfter: true` in place, so it skipped the entire completion ritual: diff tally, HR summary, save-as-routine, the first-finish tip, and the `.plusplusWorkoutFinished` post (Continue's alone), which meant no Today landing and no green→purple card conversion. One argument fixes it; the recap is now the single convergence point of every door, which is what makes "posts exactly once" true by construction — plus a one-shot `recapContinued` guard, since Continue's dismiss animates and a second tap could land before the cover cleared. New smoke flow (`testEarlyFinishRoutesThroughRecap`, the 10th): end early after one set → recap appears → Continue → Today landing, which proves the notification fired for this door.
2. **Salvage's honest-duration anchor gained its tripwire.** The behavior itself landed with watch-repair stage 1 (`finish(at: session.lastActivityAt)`); the #503 checkbox test lands now (`salvageCarriesHonestDuration` in EffortClockTests): a set logged ten minutes in, salvaged days later, carries endedAt at the set's stamp and banks 600 s — never the gap.
3. **The single-effort commit key SLIDES** (`SlideToFinishKey`, Views/Components): on a session that is one continuous effort, logging and ENDING are the same decision, and that decision must never belong to a stray tap forty minutes into a run. The raised-key grammar supplies the mechanism — the cap slides the length of its own base plate, worn full-width as the track, sinking onto it while touched, committing at the end of travel; an abandoned drag springs home, which is the affordance teaching the slide. Both "Finish workout" sites converted (the log dock and the count-up timer card); the exit dialog's Finish stays a tap, already behind a confirm. VoiceOver/Switch Control/Voice Control activate it DIRECTLY (`accessibilityRepresentation` Button, same `completeSetButton` identifier) and Return finishes via a hidden keyboard-shortcut sibling — ⚠️ but XCUITest does NOT ride the representation (review): `tap()` synthesizes a TOUCH at the frame, not the accessibility activation VoiceOver performs, and a touch dies on the 10 pt drag threshold, so under `--uitest-reset` a tap commits (test-only door, StartFlashButton's skipped-flash precedent — the first single-effort smoke flow would otherwise have gone red on a claim three docs stated as fact). Drag transients are `@GestureState`, not `@State` (review): a CANCELLED sequence — a call, the app switcher — never runs `onEnded`, and `@State` left the cap stranded sunk mid-track with a stale latch eating the next slide; GestureState resets on end AND cancel through the spring transaction. The cap scales with Dynamic Type (`@ScaledMetric` — the tap keys it replaces grew at AX sizes), the slide mirrors under RTL (`layoutDirection` signs the translation; `chevron.forward` flips itself), and the drag rides `.simultaneousGesture` with the first-event direction latch so it can never starve an enclosing scroll. ⚠️ The slide gesture itself is XCUITest-invisible — device pass, like every gesture layer.

**2026-08-01 — A routine header's in-kit chip opens the tapped piece, not the catalog** — #470's KIT row sent an available piece to `CatalogScopeView(scope: .kit)` in a bare sheet, and it was wrong twice over. Wrong destination: tapping "Barbell" answered with the full add-mode equipment catalog opened at "Ab Crunch Machine", anchored to nothing about the chip (Dave: "I don't understand why"). Broken mechanics: the presented form pushes row detail with `.navigationDestination(item:)` and deliberately owns no `NavigationStack` ("presented inside someone else's stack") — the two precedent sheet sites honor that by wrapping (`RevealSurface`'s Edit-your-kit, the template gear-check), this one presented it bare, so every row tap inside the sheet was the documented silent-no-op class (ui-interaction.md), invisible to CI. The chip answers PER-PIECE now, mirroring the amber branch Dave already kept per-piece: an in-kit chip resolves its name by direct fetch (the `ModelContext.routine(uuid:)` pattern — no standing all-equipment query for one tap) and presents `EquipmentDetailScreen` in its own `NavigationStack`-wrapped sheet, where membership, config and the exercises/routines cross-links live and the cross-links push within the sheet. Rider: the synthetic "Bodyweight" tag an equipment-free routine shows flowed through the same handler — never a kit member name, so tapping it opened the RESOLVE sheet for equipment that doesn't exist; it renders inert now (`interactive: false`) because the ring means door, and there is no door. ⚠️ Device pass: chip → its detail sheet, cross-links push inside it, back dismisses, and an equipment-free routine's Bodyweight tag neither rings nor taps.

**2026-08-01 — Health single-writer rule: a watch involved in the session writes Health; otherwise the phone** — Dave's #519 answer, verbatim. Before this, the writer followed who FINISHED: a phone finish always wrote retrospectively (`HealthRecorder`) and told the wrist to discard its live HK session — throwing away the device that actually measured the training (HR, Apple's energy estimator) in favor of the one that measured nothing, and double-counting nothing only because the wrist obeyed. Now participation decides, not finish location:

1. **The wrist saves when it logged, whatever closed the session.** `finishClosedByPhone` and `shipShareAndLeave` save the wrist's HK session when `wristLogged`, discard otherwise (a glance that logged nothing still reaches Health not at all — the browse rule). The wrist's own finish already saved. ⚠️ The gate is `measuredByIndex` OR the journaled reducer's watch-origin logs (swift-reviewer): the measured map is view `@State` and rebuilds empty across a relaunch, so a resumed run that logged nothing more would have discarded despite wrist sets in the journal — while the phone, reading durable UserDefaults, stood down. The early-leave save is the wrist's LAST chance (no view code runs when the phone's finish op arrives later), so the Health window ends at leave even if the phone keeps going — accepted: the wrist records what it measured.
2. **The phone stands down when the wrist LOGGED.** `finishSession` gates both phone writers on the new `LiveMirror.watchParticipated(sessionId)` — its own registry key, marked only on `.logSet` ops (swift-reviewer: an adopted wrist emits `.restEnded` when a phone rest expires on its screen, so any-op participation stood the phone down for a passive glance and NOBODY wrote), window-free (the 12 h window belongs to salvage's "still running?"; this asks "did the watch train?"), bounded by session count, cleared with the liveness entry. When true, the retrospective write is skipped and a live phone recording (`LiveWorkoutController`) is DISCARDED, not saved — the wrist's builder was the one on the body. ⚠️ The result import's unconditional registry clear moved behind a `phoneIsAuthoring` guard (layered review): the wrist's early-exit result merges into the still-open session BEFORE the phone's finish, and the old clear erased the participation fact right before the decision read it — a double write on the commonest split-finish path. A finished/foreign session's import still clears, and a stale entry for a finished session is harmless (the decision only ever reads the open session's id).
3. **Accepted edges, chosen over connectivity-dependent rules:** a wrist that logged OFFLINE hasn't delivered ops when the phone finishes, so both write once (double-count, rare, self-limiting); a wrist app killed mid-run whose run view never remounts before the phone's finish saves nothing, and the phone still stands down when the queued ops arrive, so nobody writes (the durable record is intact — Health is a bonus, never a gate).

**2026-08-01 — The rail turns date-first, and quick start becomes its ANYTIME entry: a live card whose keys morph into their own config** — Dave, iterating in the prototype: pop the dates out of the cards, center the rail dots on them, hang the cards below — and give quick start a card that "always renders just above what's scheduled or done today, below the other future stuff." Then, on the dashed variant: a tapped key should expand to fill the card with "further options, config, detail, and a button to start it," the expanding key's border overtaking the dashed one, plus a way back. Built as prototyped, merged on his word.

**The rail restructure.** Every dated entry renders its date on its OWN row — the node CENTERED on it (both stand one node-diameter tall), the card below, dates gone from card metas (a fact printed twice an inch apart reads as a glitch; committed subtitles and the future/carried captions all slimmed). Per-ENTRY, not per-day: two workouts one day print the day twice, which is what a log does, and it keeps the LazyVStack free of grouping state. TODAY is the one multi-card group ("today · thu · jul 31" over rest/due/pending cards, node green when anything can start); the carried lane's date row is its "was tue · jul 7" line in advisory amber (the tense grammar moved up a row, still never dressed as today); setup rows stay the one undated class, node beside content as before.

**The anytime entry.** Below the future, above today, every day: "anytime" sits in the DATE POSITION (it is the entry with no date), the node is a DASHED ring, and the card is a dashed shell — the dash is now one grammar, "not yet a thing," shared with the future cards' stroke. The landing seats the anytime row under the (facts-only) band, so the offer and today are both on screen at open. The band drops back to tally + bar, still pinned; the QUICK START label died with the rack's band residency (the "anytime" dateline labels the group now).

**The shell morph.** A tapped key grows INTO its config panel: `matchedGeometryEffect` pairs the key's CHROME with the panel's (content fades — matching content reflows text mid-flight), on `Theme.Anim.selection`, panel `zIndex` above the outgoing rack, the panel's solid border OVERTAKING the dashed shell (which fades back on the way home behind the ‹ key). ⚠️ mGE and never a measured FLIP, and this is load-bearing: Today lives in the TabView subtree, where writing layout geometry into state breaks the search-role morph (nav-diag 4e) — the effect is the one lawful way to frame-match here. Panels: a sport gets its last outing ("last · 5.1 km · 28 min", first-match scan, panel-only), preset chips (round numbers in the profile's OWN unit, never conversions — a "5K" chip on a miles profile would prescribe 3.107; `QuickStartPresets`), Open as the default (untargeted → the count-up clock), "Custom…" escaping to the full `ExerciseConfigSheet` (the triad law lives there; chips never grow wheels), and a green Start (`StartFlashButton`, whose deferred fire the start-guard round already covers). Train offers BOTH paths — "Start empty," and "Pick a routine" handing off to the Routines tab (`onGoToRoutines`, a pointer, never a second library). "N more" morphs into the hidden sports one level deeper. ⚠️ The green + still opens the picker SHEET: the mock's in-place chip grid didn't survive contact with a 345-exercise catalog — a multi-select is a searchable list.

⚠️ Known trades, eyes open: the always-reachable pinned rack is gone (scroll deep into history and the keys scroll away; the landing mitigates at open), on scheduled days the rail's first actionable thing is the offer above the plan (watch whether that reads as "skip your plan"), and the morph's feel — timing, the dash fade, chips at AX sizes — is device-only. The pull ghost check carries over unchanged; the shorter band shrinks its surface area.

**2026-07-31 — The play key dies: every start has a first-class home, and the tray goes with it** — Dave, closing the loop the rack-out-of-tray round opened: the tray's first two rows "are already pretty well served, by the routines tab itself, and today's workout card on the today view. That leaves just the scratch routine thing. Maybe we kill the play button and just put a way to start a scratch routine right on the today view, along with the cardio quick start stuff?"

The tray died of its own success at delegating: once every start it offered had a first-class surface — today's routine on its own card, the library on its tab, the sports on the band's rack — its only unduplicated row was the scratch start, and a sheet holding one real act is chrome, not access. The scratch start joins the rack as its LEADING key: **"Train"**, wearing the build-as-you-go dashed square (`plus.square.dashed`) — the voice's imperative for the workout that isn't one sport; the universal start anchors the row, the picked sports follow, and the fitting pass reserves it alongside the green +. `SwapInSheet` deletes whole, with the trailing play key, its sheet mount, and the pending flags it dismissed into; `swapInCandidates` renames to `startableRoutines` (the "swap in" framing was the tray's); the due-but-empty card's "Start a workout" key begins a scratch session DIRECTLY (identifier follows: `startWorkoutButton`); the smoke test's Today-landing sentinel moves to the band's edit key. ⚠️ Lost knowingly: the primed today key, the previous round's one genuine win — the today card it duplicated IS the surface, a flick away at worst. If the device pass misses a start reachable at any scroll depth, the answer is the band (already pinned), never a resurrected sheet. ⚠️ ui-interaction's detent-follows-depth law loses its last live consumer with the tray; it stands, exemplar in git history.

**2026-07-31 — The rack leaves the tray: a pinned band copied into a sheet is the same UI on screen twice** — Dave, on seeing the merge: "seems kinda weird to have identical ui both inside and outside of a sheet." He's right, and the reason is structural, not taste: the band is PINNED chrome, so when the tray opens at `.medium`, both copies of the identical rack are literally visible at once — the "second mouth" was redundancy wearing completeness' clothes. The refined merge keeps its one genuine win: **today's scheduled routine primed at the top of the tray** (the play key's likeliest intent, a different FORM from the rail card so it duplicates intent, not UI). The rack is the band's alone; the tray holds what you CHOOSE.
