The `horizontalPadding` parameter and the tray's quick-start handoff flags died with the copy; the root's ScrollView and the config sheet's onDismiss handoff (both from the same review round) stay — they were correct independent of the rack.

**2026-07-31 — The start tray becomes the complete start surface: one rack, two mouths** — Dave asked whether quick start and the play key's sheet should merge. They should, and the merge is composition, not migration: the tray (`SwapInSheet`) now leads with **today's scheduled routine as a primed key** when one is due (the likeliest intent behind the play key; green start grammar is lawful — it IS today's occurrence), then the **same `QuickStartRow`** the header band shows (one component with a `horizontalPadding` parameter, so the two mouths can never drift — same imperative labels, same "N more", same edit key), then the two existing choose keys. The band stays the fast path; the play key now opens everything startable in one place.

⚠️ This is NOT build 158's regression un-reverted: 158 removed quick start from the tray because it was the ONLY home and "a one-tap start that first has to open a sheet is not a one-tap start". With the band as the primary mouth, the tray's copy is completeness, not the path. The two-keys-tall compact detent died with the merge — `.medium` is the honest height for a four-section tray, and the depth-driven detent plumbing (ScaledMetric, selection binding) deleted with it. ⚠️ The ScaledMetric was ALSO the tray's Dynamic Type protection (a11y audit 2026-07-13: the compact height grew with type size), so the root stage now SCROLLS — at AX sizes four sections outgrow any fixed detent, and a key below an unscrollable fold is unreachable chrome (swift-reviewer caught the regression before it shipped). The config sheet's committed Start now rides onDismiss like every other handoff in the flow, closing the one pre-existing same-transaction present the reviewer flagged for the record. The tray's quick-start taps hand off through the same pending-flag mechanism as every other act it dismisses into (the sheet-from-dismissing-sheet drop class).

**2026-07-31 — Quick start leads the band** — Dave, same round: the keys above the week status. Actions first, facts under them; the caption-adoption fix survives the flip because each caption still sits directly above its own content (QUICK START over the keys, the tally sentence over its bar). The ++ key was already the leading toolbar item app-side — the prototype had drawn it trailing, a mock fidelity fix only.

**2026-07-31 — The band is chrome, full stop: pinned via safeAreaInset, and the keys stop scrolling** — Dave, fifth round, superseding the boundary mount within the hour: the tally, the completion bar and the quick starts "shouldn't be a part of the timeline or the overall page scroll (except just the little scroll that moves the heading into the top)". Pinned chrome on the scroll's shell — the catalogs' #494 `safeAreaInset` mount — with every trace of the in-scroll era deleted: no `visualEffect` offset, no reservation, no anchor compensation. The scroll holds rail items only; the landing's `scrollTo` seats the date line under the band because the inset shrinks the content area, and the keys hit-test plainly (the mid-float device concern died with the float).

⚠️ **This knowingly re-runs build 152.** The sticky-in-scroll law existed because PINNED broke the pull: the rubber-band walks the large title down over pinned chrome ("Today" slid over the block bar), and Today carries the app's only `.refreshable`. Dave's ask is explicit and the law is his to reverse, so it ships with the history attached: the pull is the #1 device check, and if it reads broken on glass the recorded fallback is the boundary-mounted sticky band at `8b9e16b` — not build 152's revert, and not the reservation. navigation.md's law is rewritten to match.

**And the rack stops scrolling** (same round: "the horizontal scroll isn't great"): hidden overflow in a pinned band has no affordance, so the row fits what it can and collapses the tail into an **"N more" Menu** — `OverflowCapsuleRow`'s 2026-07-19 rule at key scale, widths from `UIFont` metrics with the container width read through the same background-reader idiom that component already ships (not a layout probe; the morph law's cited exemplar). Every pick stays reachable: visible keys at one tap, the tail at two, the green + always last. At least one sport key always shows — one real key plus "N more" beats only chrome.

**2026-07-31 — The band mounts AT the boundary it divides, and the reservation dies** — Dave, fourth round: the band-height gap between the upcoming items and today read as inconsistent spacing, "whether past present or future." The gap was the RESERVATION — build 158's hidden-copy-with-spine mechanism, which existed only because the band mounted at the TOP of the scroll, away from the space the landing needed held. The pinning `visualEffect` (`minY < 0 ? -minY : 0`) is mount-point-agnostic, so mounting the band at the today boundary — between the future section and the date line, exactly where the reservation sat — makes the band ITS OWN reservation: the landing seats the anchor at the viewport top and the band is simply there, the hidden copy and its spine overlay delete outright, and item spacing is uniform at every scroll position. One deliberate behavior change: scrolled up to the week ahead, the band now sits INLINE as the future/today divider instead of floating at the screen top — the strip belongs to the boundary it describes, and the week-ahead browse trades a floating tally for a rail with no phantom gap. ⚠️ The keys-hit-where-they-draw device check is unchanged (the band still floats once scrolled past); what dissolves is every question about the reservation — there is nothing left to reserve.

**2026-07-31 — The keys get their own name: one QUICK START label, and the shelf ships** — Dave, on the five-direction exploration: the pinned rack stays ("I like having them up top"), but the week tally directly above the keys read as if it were describing the buttons. The text was being ADOPTED: the tally was the band's only words, and an unlabeled row of salient controls will claim whatever text sits nearest. The fix is ownership, not separation — an ALL-CAPS mono `QUICK START` section label above the rack (the treatment the app reserves for exactly this; "Quick start" is the feature's settled name), and ONLY there: the tally is its own sentence and self-labels, so a `THIS WEEK` label would be redundancy dressed as symmetry. The band also draws routine detail's hairline shelf at its lower edge now (Dave saw direction 1 wearing it and kept it) — ⚠️ gated on the band having content, because a zero-height band still has width and the setup scaffold collapses both sections; a stray hairline under the title is the invisible-edge class the shelf exists to fix.

**2026-07-31 — Quick start moves INTO the sticky band** — Dave, second round on the same note: the header row below the band still read as "in the timeline". Anything sitting in the scroll flow between rail segments does, however it dresses — so the keys join the tally inside the pinned band itself, where they are unambiguously chrome, reachable at any scroll depth, and the rail now runs unbroken from the week ahead to the date line (the reviewer's spine-interruption flag dissolves). The band mounts twice (sticky + hidden reservation), so the reservation reserves the taller height and the spine pass-through survive automatically. ⚠️ The trade accepted knowingly: the band floats on a `visualEffect` offset and these are its first INTERACTIVE elements — whether a key hits where it draws mid-float is THE device check this placement buys; the fallback if it misses is the below-band header row, one mount away in the same file. (Also from this round: the prototype's "12 day streak" line was mock-only — the real week strip has never shown a streak; the Streak home-screen widget is the app's only streak surface.)

**2026-07-31 — Quick start leaves the timeline for the header zone, and its keys speak the imperative** — Dave, on build 159: the keys "don't belong in the timeline", and they should say "run", not "running".

Both halves follow laws the surface already had. **Placement**: the rail records OCCURRENCES — scheduled work, carried work, committed sessions — and a row of keys is an OFFER, so it moves to the zone the week strip established for surface furniture ("the tally is the surface's week header, not an entry on the timeline"): full width, no spine, between the week-strip reservation and the date line where the timeline begins. Still scroll content (the large-title law), and deliberately NOT inside the sticky band itself — the band floats on a `visualEffect` offset, and interactive keys rendering offset from where they hit-test is a device-only bug class a fact-only band never has to answer for; if the device pass wants the keys always reachable, moving them INTO the band is a one-line promotion to attempt then, not now. The row scrolls full-bleed with a 16 pt `contentMargins` so a long rack slides under the screen edge instead of clipping at a column.

**Voice**: a key is a command, so it wears the imperative (`QuickStartLabel`, the FormCues name-keyed shape — verbs need judgment a derivation can't make): Run · Run inside (treadmill) · Walk · Hike · Ride · Spin · Row · Swim · Swim open water. The authored labels are unique by construction (a test pins it), customs fall back to their modality's verb, and where no honest verb exists (a generic-cardio custom, the Elliptical) the NAME stands — a noun key beats a wrong verb. If two picks still resolve to one verb (two climbers, a custom bike beside Cycling), the colliding keys fall back to their names: two keys both reading "Ride" is a coin flip. ⚠️ "Spin" and "Swim open water" are judgment calls flagged for the device pass; overruling either is a table edit.
**2026-08-01 — The facet row rides the list on tab roots (the pinned inset broke the large-title bar)** — the 2026-07-31 facet round shipped the row as a top `safeAreaInset` on the List, with "filter row vs large-title overscroll" flagged for the device pass and "row as first list content" pre-named as the fallback. Dave's device pass came back worse than the anticipated overscroll cosmetics, and the fallback is taken.

What the two screenshots showed (pixel-measured from the PNGs, 3x): **(1) At rest** the Exercises tab drew NO large title — the bar row, then a title-sized dead band (~116→168 pt), then the chips. The safe-area geometry still reserved the expanded-bar zone (the chips band sat exactly one large-title height below the collapsed bar), but the bar painted itself in its *scrolled-under* visual state: large-title text hidden, no inline title either. **(2) In both states — at rest and scrolled** — the bar drew its scrolled-under hairline at the same fixed y (~115.5 pt, the collapsed bar's edge-region boundary). Around it: ~8 pt of system gap above, but only 4 pt below to the visible chip — the 4 pt being `FacetChip`'s 44 pt hit frame around the 36 pt chip — which is the "imbalanced spacing" Dave photographed. Neither metric is app-tunable; the line itself is the system's Liquid Glass scrolled-under edge, not anything the app draws.

Likely mechanism (inferred — the desync itself is the measured fact): a pinned top `safeAreaInset` shifts the List's resting offset by exactly the band's height (52 pt ≈ one large-title zone), and UIKit's large-title tracking reads that as "already collapsed" — permanently. Visual state (collapsed, hairline, no title text) desyncs from layout state (expanded, space reserved). This is the Today week-strip lesson holding a second time: **anything a large title can travel over has to be scroll content** — a `safeAreaInset` is pinned, so it fails the same way (navigation.md already said so; the facet round hoped the no-`.refreshable` case was exempt — it isn't).

The change: on TAB roots the filter row is the list's first content (zero row insets, no separator, clear row background) and scrolls away with the list; PRESENTED and PICKER surfaces keep the pinned, opaque inset. The SEARCH tab is deliberately swept along (`mode.isTab` includes it, though its inline-title bar was never broken): the tabs and search are one surface by design, and a row that pins on one and scrolls on the other would read as two — carve out `isSearchSurface` only if the device pass wants the pin back there. A rider fix: `FilterSummaryChip`'s Button goes `.buttonStyle(.plain)` — as list content it joined the build-12 row-tap-routing class (a tap on the row's dead band would have popped the popover) — their chrome is app-drawn (`pushedScreenChrome` / sheet header), so the large-title machinery never runs there and the pin has no failure mode. Cost accepted: chips don't pin on tabs. Not tried: `.safeAreaBar` (iOS 26's blur-integrated pin) — same pinned-accessory geometry class, unverifiable from a remote session, and the design wants an opaque band, not blur; anyone re-attempting a pinned form owes a device pass first, since the whole failure is invisible to CI (XCUITest sees the chips either way).

**2026-07-31 — Wrist quick start closes the repair program** — #513's last item (decision sheet Q24-A). The quick-start sports ride the plan push as synthesized one-step scratch plans (`PlanRoutine.isQuickStart`, additive — an older watch renders them as plain rows, which still work), sourced from the same device-local `QuickStartPicks` Today's rail reads, resolved by name with stale picks dropping. The wrist renders them under a "Quick start" section, the sport's name alone (a one-step plan has no count worth naming); one tap opens the run, the first log originates the session, and everything downstream — the untargeted effort saying nothing, no "1/1", the finish, the mirror, the import merge — was already built by stages 1–4 and #514. `restSeconds` is nominal: a single step never schedules a rest.

**2026-07-31 — Watch repair stage 4: the wrist renders the reducer** — the deferred half of #322 and the root of half the assessment's breaks: `WorkoutRunView` drove from frozen local `@State` while dutifully journaling a reducer it never read. It renders the shared state now (#513), and the wrist copy fixes rode along (#514).

1. **The screen reads the journal it keeps.** Step index is the shared cursor (an explicit cursor parked on a completed slot yields to first-incomplete), the step list is the reducer's (so a phone append/swap/remove reflows the wrist — a plan RE-PUSH still never touches a live session; only deliberate `.stepsChanged` ops do), rest renders from the shared register (a phone ±15 s or Skip moves the wrist countdown, and expiry fires the wrist haptic for phone-authored rests too — B9's parity), a phone hold shows a dedicated paused screen, and a phone finish/discard closes the view. Discard sends NO result — a result would resurrect the discarded session through the import.
2. **Adoption happens at APPEAR, origination stays at first log** (`adoptIfPresent` vs `beginIfNeeded`): resume-after-relaunch and both-devices handoff seat at the shared cursor before any log, while browsing a routine still never births a session.
3. **What stays local is what only the wrist knows**: `measuredByIndex` — per-step HR windows and GPS splits, keyed by the SHARED slot. The result payload is index-faithful now: the wrist's measured entries where it logged, the reducer's actuals where the phone logged, bare incompletes elsewhere — so the import walks it by order whatever mix of devices did the work, and the wrist logging at the shared cursor is what ends the B4 clobbering.
4. **#514's copy honesty**: count-of-one on the kicker and the commit key ("Log", never "Log rep", about one run), the untargeted effort says nothing instead of "— reps", the finish line claims "Synced" only when the phone is reachable ("On its way to your iPhone." otherwise), and End early sits a deliberate gap below Log — the rest screen's own adjacency lesson.

Remaining from #513, next unit: wrist quick start (the sports ride the plan push as one-step scratch plans). ⚠️ The whole stage is device-validation-heavy: reducer-driven rendering, adoption, rest parity and the paused screen are all XCUITest-invisible.

**2026-07-31 — Watch repair stage 3: the phone becomes a real peer** — the last structural asymmetry from the assessment: the watch ran a real reducer while the phone projected ops raw, and the phone did things it never said. Stage 3 (#512) closes both, all additively.

1. **The op vocabulary can say "held" and "the plan changed".** `.paused(at:)`/`.resumed(at:)` and `.stepsChanged(steps:)` join the Kit with their own LWW stamp registers (`pauseStamp`, `stepsStamp` — the birth list always loses to an explicit change). An OLDER peer cannot decode an unknown kind: its `try?` drops just that op, pause degrades to invisible there — the pre-#512 behavior, pinned by a test that decodes a bogus-kind op and expects the throw.
2. **The phone gained the reducer discipline** it always demanded of the watch: a durable applied-op ledger (duplicates refuse by opId — the sendMessage error-fallback CAN deliver twice) and a durable pending buffer (an op that outran its `.started` parks and replays when the session materializes, instead of dropping). Both bounded per session, cleaned with the session's lifecycle, UserDefaults-backed so A4's ack-on-return holds across relaunch.
3. **The phone emits what it does**: pause/resume from the Pause and Resume keys (the wrist's mirrored ledger banks the held stretch via `pauseClock`/`startClock`, so a paused wrist-mirrored row stops accruing training time), cursor moves and redos from the overview's jump affordances, and a whole-list `.stepsChanged` after append/resize — a diff would need its own ordering protocol; the list is small, replacement is idempotent.
4. **The wrist honors a phone hold** with one reducer read pre-stage-4: the commit key dims and disables under "Paused on your iPhone." on the adopted session only (the authoring-id match keeps a displaced foreign state from reaching it); End early stays available.
5. **The wrist's durable ingest keeps A4 now**: a queued phone op parks in UserDefaults synchronously inside the delegate callback, applies on the main actor, and a relaunch drains what a death in between left behind — the watch-side violation the stage-2 review flagged. And the Kit reducer buffers a FOREIGN session's ops (bounded) instead of dropping them, so a reachability flip that delivers a new session's live ops before its queued birth loses nothing; a losing birth still drops (dead history would churn every replay).

Deliberately deferred: the Live Activity's own PAUSED phase (the island still shows working while held) — display polish that belongs with #157's Live Activity controls, not this correctness stage.

**2026-07-31 — Watch repair stage 2: one identity on the wire** — the assessment's highest-leverage structural finding was three subsystems keying identity three ways (plan by NAME, mirror by `sessionId`, import by name+startedAt), which disagreed exactly when it mattered. Stage 2 (#511) collapses them, all additively.

1. **The result carries the session's own id.** `SessionResult.sessionId` (additive optional) is stamped from the wrist's frozen authoring id — captured BEFORE the finished op, which clears it — and the phone's import keys on it first, falling back to the (name, startedAt ± 1 s) heuristic only for older watches. A fresh import stamps the id onto the new row, so redelivery dedupes by identity, not heuristic. The deferred never-end-a-phone-authored-session guard (`LiveMirror.phoneIsAuthoring`, an in-memory marker set by `begin`/cleared by `end`) landed and then MOVED under review: the wrist's `.finished` op reaches `project()` before the result ever reaches the merge, so a merge-only guard was dead code (swift-reviewer) — the projection's `.finished`/`.discarded` cases enforce it, the merge keeps it as backstop, and the wrist wrapping up its share of an adopted session leaves the phone user's workout running for their own Finish to close.
2. **Routines cross the wire by uuid.** `PlanRoutine.uuid` and the `.started` op's `routineUuid` (both additive; `State.routineUuid` rides the journal) carry `Routine.uuid`, so wrist adoption matches identity instead of name (names are not unique), and `materialize` resolves the routine's transition setting by uuid first, name for older peers. Backward-compat is TESTED: hand-written pre-#511 JSON for the op, the result, and the plan all decode with nil identity (synthesized enum Codable uses decodeIfPresent for optional associated values — verified, not assumed).
3. **Phone-driven rests finally cue the wrist.** The rest notifier now tracks the phone's rest register at the ingest site: a phone `.restStarted` schedules the "rest over" notification (with the up-next step's name), ±15 s reschedules it, Skip and the session's close cancel it, and an hours-old op from a drained queue can never resurrect a stale rest. This closes the assessment's B9 for the notification layer; full haptic parity rides stage 4's reducer-driven wrist UI.

**2026-07-31 — Watch repair stage 1: the adapters stop losing what the core keeps** — the architecture assessment's verdict was "sound model, half-finished implementation", and stage 1 (#510) closes the data-loss class without touching the model.

1. **Salvage learns what "orphan" means.** `resolveOrphanedSessions` exempts any session whose mirror ops are still arriving (`LiveMirror.isLiveElsewhere` — a UserDefaults registry stamped per projected op, cleared by lifecycle ops and result import, 12 h window). Glancing at the phone mid-wrist-workout used to force-finish or delete the live session. And a salvaged finish now anchors to `lastActivityAt` (newest completed set, else start) instead of relaunch time — the crash-to-reopen gap is not training time (#503's half) — then emits the lifecycle op via `closeRemotely` so the wrist journal closes too.
2. **The result import MERGES.** A dedupe match used to skip the wrist's authoritative `SessionResult`, throwing away session and per-set HR on every mirrored wrist workout (the ops carry no HR fields). It now fills the existing row — HR, measured extras with profile widening, completions the ops lost, the honest `endedAt` — and never downgrades, so redelivery stays idempotent.
3. **A dead journal can't poison the wrist.** `.discarded` never sets `endedAt`, so the journal's `isFinished` guard kept a discarded state forever and the reducer then silently dropped every future phone session's `.started`. `State.isClosed` (finished OR discarded) now gates the journal and adoption, and the Kit reducer gained a DISPLACEMENT rule: a newer `.started` for a different session replaces stale state (one human — a session that has genuinely begun means the old one is over), while a replayed old `.started` is refused by stamp comparison against `latestStamp`. Three new Kit tests pin it (609 green).
4. **Late ops can't mutate a committed record** (`.logSet`/`.reopen`/`.cursor` gained finished-guards) and a wrist-measured extra on an untargeted step now widens the log's snapshot profile in the mirror path, matching the import path's rule.
5. **The wrist implements `HKWorkoutSessionDelegate`** — a session the system kills no longer dies silently with the last HR frozen on screen; the runtime tears down and live readings clear, summaries stay.

**2026-07-31 — The design-law audit and the decision-sheet round** — the five law docs were verified claim-by-claim against the code (drift fixes: PR #501), a ten-domain flow audit produced a decision sheet, and Dave answered it in full. The outcomes that are LAWS live in the rules files; the ones recorded here so they aren't re-litigated:

1. **Type is the system ramp, worn plainly** (now in design-grammar.md): a law, not tokens — dynamic text styles only, fixed sizes reserved for display numerals, mono is data, weight carries emphasis. Deliberately NO Theme type-token layer: the system styles are the ramp, and abstraction waits for real duplication pain.
2. **OS-bug laws carry recheck tags** (navigation.md): a law that encodes an OS-26 bug is tagged "(recheck: iOS 27)" so a workaround doesn't fossilize past the SDK that caused it.
3. **The streak stays widget/Operator-only** (Dave): no in-app streak surface. Deliberate scarcity — recorded so a future session doesn't "fix" it into Today.
4. **Left as-is, deliberately (Dave):** routine-reorder stays untaught (no tip, no handle); the equipment resolve sheet stays per-piece; the Type facet stays on the presented/onboarding kit picker; the drawer and Operator get no discovery teaching.
5. The decided flow changes (early-finish routes through the recap; salvage anchors to last real activity; quick start keeps its sheet but turns instant-feeling and appears once the equipment step is done; the schedule step gains a valid no-schedule completion; BlockBar segments become CORRECTION affordances, never surprise jumps; the catalog names what filters hide; hand-built routines read "not rated" under Effort/Style; the GitHub wizard authorizes first and verifies each step; a broken sync shows as an amber advisory on Today; the calendar ask moves to the first fixed-weekday schedule AND stays in settings; the exercise sheet's Remove gains a confirm; the record shows a faint per-set "asked:" reference; history gets sticky month headers; quick-started sessions are named for their sport; the wrist gains quick start) are tracked as implementation issues. The watch↔phone integration is under an architecture assessment before its items proceed (Dave: "may be fundamentally broken — assess").
**2026-07-31 — The cardio push's design review lands five fixes, and the merge with main resolves two facet systems into one** — a design-and-flow review of #482 against the settled laws, applied on the branch so the PR merges as one coherent statement.

1. **One facet grammar.** #494 landed the facet-row return on main (single-select `FacetChip` Menus, `FilterSummaryChip`, `CatalogFilterState` applied in the engine) while this branch carried #475's own row (multi-select `SelectableChip`s, an inline Clear key, a muscle multi-select pushed onto the stack). Two grammars for one row, and the branch's violated two settled laws (summarize-never-insta-clear; single-select Menus only in v1). Resolution: main's grammar wins; the cardio push keeps its two real contributions rebuilt on it — **KIND (Cardio · Strength · Mobility) leads the exercises facets** as a `CatalogKind?` in `CatalogFilterState` (nothing else reaches cardio as a set: every cardio row files under Full Body), and the modality search terms join main's synonyms in the shared haystack. `CatalogFacetRow` is deleted; the Favorites chip died with it (curation is the MINE tier and the fav swipe — 2026-07-25's reasoning, which main's grammar preserved). The same merge deduped what a textual merge cannot see: main's bare "Swimming" seed row yields to Pool Swim / Open Water Swim (#478's `PaceReference` and authored modality), and the "Stationary Bike" synonym follows its renamed row.
2. **A finished session's noun is a SNAPSHOT** (`WorkoutSession.summaryModalityRaw`, stamped in `finish()`, read as `summaryWorkUnit`). The finish screen resolved `modality` live while the history rows and Today's committed cards read the FIRST log's unit (a per-row relationship walk was too expensive in a list), so a run-then-lifting session said "5 sets" on one surface and "5 reps" on another. Sessions snapshot — resolve once, at the moment the session becomes a record; pre-field records fall back to the first-log read they always did. EXCLUDED from the interchange (derivable from the logs; census carries the reason). The wrist's finished line joins the same rule through `routine.sessionModality` and `WorkUnit.summaryCount` — it said "1 set logged" about a walk, the exact line #491 removed from the phone.
3. **The count-up effort clock anchors on the session's running-time ledger** (`effortAnchorSeconds`, in `elapsed()` space; `markEffortStart`/`effortElapsed`). The first fix for the pause-unmount data loss hoisted a wall-clock anchor to the session VIEW and re-implemented pause banking beside the ledger that already does it — one level short, since in count-up mode the displayed elapsed IS the logged duration and view state still dies with the process. Both `@State` vars and the second `isPaused` observer are deleted; pauses drop out by construction because both terms of the subtraction read the same ledger. ⚠️ The stamp sites are where an effort genuinely BEGINS: the cursor moving, and a **rest ending** (any of its doors, with the paused-bank hold excluded) — the last stamp wins, so round two of an open-ended interval no longer starts its clock, and its logged duration, two minutes of recovery deep.
4. **`isSingleEffort` gains the cardio gate its own sentence promised** ("a run, a ride, a swim"). Ungated, one ad-hoc strength set auto-finished on log — overriding "ad-hoc sessions never auto-finish" (design-review 9, Dave-decided) for a shape the build-158 quote never covered. One bench set is a session you probably ADD to; one run is a session you end.
5. **Count-of-one stragglers.** The overview's per-block set rows printed "Effort 1" over a single effort (now `WorkUnit.rowLabel`, which refuses to number a row with no siblings; the live row says "live", not "current round"); and quick-start picks — name-keyed device state — now FOLLOW a rename (`QuickStartPicks.rename`, called from the editor's save and the indoor-bike merge, whose own rename is the proof the fragility is real).

**2026-07-31 — The finish states what a cardio effort measured beside what it measured last time, and prints no delta** — the fourth moment the cardio plan promised (find · prescribe · do · **finish**), and the one easiest to lose as a footnote.

It reuses `DiffLedger` rather than growing a second grammar: two columns, the arithmetic left to the reader, an em-dash where there is nothing to compare. The card only needed the left column to be nameable — a plan says "target", a finished workout says "did" — so `leadingTitle` is the whole addition, and the reflow, the truncation floor and the one-sentence VoiceOver row come along unchanged.

⚠️ **`changed` is deliberately EMPTY, so nothing inks.** Direction ink (2026-07-30) marks a PLAN moving against a performance. Here both columns are performances, and painting one green would be **#453 arriving through a different door** — which is the whole reason this surface needed rebuilding.

**And the paced exercises now LEAVE the delta tally.** That is the substantive half. `RoutineDiff.delta` compares a static plan target against a past actual and calls the gap an improvement, so a routine asking 8:00/mi printed a green PR over a 9:00 run that nobody ran. Weight and reps do not have that failure — both of their sides are performances — so the split is by `isPaced`: a profile carrying distance or pace takes the two-column recap, everything else keeps the tally it was always right for. A signed number is unrepresentable in the recap, so the misread cannot recur, and `netText` stops aggregating a fabricated pace gain into the session total.

⚠️ The recap sits ABOVE the tally: on a run-plus-core session the run is what you just did and the core is the footnote. On a pure run the tally is empty and the recap is the whole thing.

⚠️ This does not close **#453** — the record screen still runs the old comparison, and Today's pending card is the surface that already fixed it. The finish is the second of three.

**2026-07-31 — A measured target ends its own effort, and an outdoor round finally records its own split** — the last unbuilt piece of PR 7. Dave's answer to "which targets should auto-advance" was **only measured ones**, and the interesting part is what that excludes: a console number you type at the finish never advances anything, because the alternative is the app guessing when a machine's readout got there — the class of lie this whole push exists to remove.

**Duration already did it** (the auto-timer expires and logs itself), so the work was distance under a live GPS fix. Building it surfaced the reason it had never been built: **the meter re-bases per EXERCISE, never per round.** Its total describes the whole 6 × 400 m, not the rep you are running, so there was no honest way to say a rep was over — and for the same reason `completeCurrentSet` refused to record distance at all unless the block held exactly one round. A 6 × 400 m logged six efforts with no distance on any of them.

`distanceAtRoundStart` is the fix, and it is the wrist's own pattern arriving late: `WatchWorkoutController.beginStep()` has banked per step since #473. Bank the meter's reading on the same edge that already re-anchors the count-up clock, subtract, and a round's own distance exists. Auto-advance compares against it, and the record keeps six splits instead of none. ⚠️ It is self-correcting against `syncLocation`'s re-base whichever order the two land in: a fresh exercise re-reads a meter at zero, and banking zero is exactly right.

⚠️ **Pace stays SINGLE-ROUND, deliberately.** The meter's average is over the whole exercise's moving time, so on rep four it would describe the entire run and file it as that rep's split. A multi-round effort takes its pace from #302's derivation instead — this round's distance over this round's time — which is the split it wanted in the first place, and a derived value is never stored so it cannot leak into the ledger.

⚠️ **The two cases the plan flagged, answered rather than left to fall out.** **Paused:** no advance. The meter is paused too, and moving a workout the user is holding is the app acting behind their back. **Mid-rest:** no advance. `currentLog` during rest is the effort that has NOT started, so nothing has reached its target. Both ride the same guard shape the GPS re-base and the island refresh already use, plus `lingeringLog` for the +1 beat, where the commit has landed and the screen has not caught up.

⚠️ It rides **every new GPS reading**, not the 30 s island tick: a target reached is the moment the effort is over, and half a minute of running past it is not that moment. Its haptic is `.rigid` rather than the tap's `.medium` — the workout moved without you asking, and the hand should be told something happened rather than that you did it.

⚠️ Indoors this is silent by construction and that is the point: distance is not measurable there, so the hero is the clock and a duration target ends the effort. A treadmill's distance stays yours to enter, and stays a number nothing advances on.

**2026-07-31 — The phone records the workout while it happens, behind a switch that is off** — PR 10 of the cardio plan, the one deliberately left unstarted until Dave asked for it. **iOS 26 is the first release where an iPhone can ORIGINATE an `HKWorkoutSession`** and drive a local `HKLiveWorkoutBuilder`; before it the phone could only mirror the wrist, which is why `HealthRecorder` writes the workout after the fact. Three things follow from writing it afterwards, and all three are real:

- **Energy was a fabrication question, so the app recorded none.** `HealthRecorder`'s own comment says the phone measures no energy and inventing `activeEnergy` would be wrong. A live session gets Apple's estimator, so a phone-tracked ride finally moves the Move ring. This is the amendment to 2026-07-13's "the phone measures no energy" — it was true of a retrospective write and is not true of a live session.
- **Live heart rate lagged** (#418). The passive anchored query reads whatever Health has, watch→phone sample delivery is batched, and the newest visible sample was routinely older than the 180 s freshness gate — so the capsule sat blank for a whole workout while the finish-time statistics query found the samples had been there all along. A locally-originated session's builder delivers what it collects, and `LiveHeartRateLabel` now prefers it, with a 30 s window rather than 180: a stream that has gone quiet for half a minute has genuinely stopped, where an anchored query's silence means nothing.
- **The workout existed only at the end**, so a crash mid-run left no trace of it in Health.

⚠️ **`LiveWorkoutSettings` defaults OFF and that is the design, not caution.** The switch changes who owns the save, who measures energy and which stream the capsule reads — three things only a device with a real wrist can judge. Off, every line of this is inert and the app behaves exactly as it has since #90. There is no data shape to unwind, so flipping it back IS the rollback.

⚠️ **Exactly ONE writer, decided synchronously.** `LiveWorkoutController.finish` returns whether the live builder owns the save, and `finishSession` skips `HealthRecorder` when it does — two writers for one workout is a DUPLICATE in Health, which is worse than either path alone. The answer comes back before the controller's own asynchronous end sequence precisely so the decision never waits on a callback that might not arrive.

⚠️ **The end sequence is exact:** `stopActivity` → wait for `.stopped` → `endCollection` → `finishWorkout` → `end()`. `.stopped` is not `.ended`, and calling `end()` early persists NOTHING — there is no save-on-end convenience. The tail lives in the session delegate because `.stopped` arrives asynchronously, which is also why the GPS route is handed over at `finish` and held: the delegate is given a state change and nothing else, and an outdoor run recorded live would otherwise lose the map the retrospective write gave it. That would have made the switch cost something.

⚠️ **Recovery ADOPTS before creating, and lives inside `start` rather than at launch.** A session survives the app, so starting a second one raises `anotherWorkoutSessionStarted` and loses both. Recovering at launch instead would leave a recovered session sitting orphaned, holding the slot for a workout already finished or discarded; `start` is the only moment the app actually wants one.

⚠️ **One sensor owner per session, by degrading rather than negotiating.** The wrist starts its own session when its run view appears, and the phone cannot see that. So `didFailWithError` gives up the claim instead of fighting for it — `ownsRecording` clears, and the caller's retrospective write is a complete fallback it was using anyway.

⚠️ **The background mode was investigated and deliberately NOT added.** Apple's `UIBackgroundModes` table lists `workout-processing` as **watchOS only**; the iOS sample the plan cited documents no key at all. Adding a value iOS does not recognize risks failing App Store validation, which would break the TestFlight path this project ships through — a concrete cost against a speculative benefit. The app already declares `location`, which covers the outdoor case that most needs it. **Open question for a Mac session: check Xcode's Background Modes capability list on the real SDK.**

⚠️ **Two things this does NOT claim.** It does not close #418: whether a real wrist streams into a *phone-originated* session (as opposed to a mirrored one) is exactly what cannot be established from here, and the issue stays open until a device says otherwise. And per-leg `HKWorkoutActivity` — #378's recorded limitation, where a run-plus-lifting session classifies wholesale — is NOT built: `beginNewActivity` is documented against a multisport container type, and shipping an unverifiable mechanism inside an unverifiable feature is two guesses stacked. The single-activity live session is the part worth device time first.

**2026-07-31 — A record of one thing numbers nothing** — the pass the entry below deferred. The record surfaces still said `?? .set`, so a logged walk's row read "Set 1" and its card said "1 set", and the fix is not a better fallback noun: at a count of one there is no label, full stop.

`WorkUnit.summaryCount(_:_:)` and `WorkUnit.rowLabel(_:index:total:)` are the two shapes a record needs, and both are `kicker`'s rule one surface later — nil at one, the sport's own word above it. Five surfaces consume them: Today's committed card, the record card and its subtitle, the record's per-set rows, and the live session's finish line and exit dialog. **A steady forty-minute walk now reads "jul 31 · 42 min"**, and its record row carries its result and no leading label at all.

⚠️ **The exit dialog needed a second sentence, not a substituted word.** "Finish keeps the 1 set you logged" has no repair that keeps the count — the honest form names the act ("Finish keeps what you logged"), so the count-of-one rule costs one branch rather than one lookup, and that is the shape to copy anywhere else the noun is load-bearing in a sentence.

⚠️ **`rowLabel` takes the DIVIDER where the live caption keeps the nil, and the split is deliberate.** A row in a list of rows has siblings to be told apart from — a walk divided into three hill repeats reads "Round 1/2/3", which is the word the divider authored it with — while the kicker describes the single thing in front of you and prints nothing. Both agree on the case the whole entry is about; only above one do they diverge. A Kit test asserts each side, because a fallback that leaked the other way would undo this.

⚠️ **`summaryCount` also drops the count from a genuine one-SET strength session**, which is a real behavior change and the intended one: the rule is a property of the count, not of the sport, and a card that prints "1 set" for a lift while printing nothing for a walk is two rules wearing one coat. The duration already says how long it was.

⚠️ Left in place: `RoutineCardContent.factLine` still says `?? .set`. It has no call sites — the routine-detail rebuild (#470) replaced it with the spec table — so changing it would assert a rule about a surface nobody can see. Delete it or wire it up before deciding what it should say.

**2026-07-31 — One effort is not a repetition of anything: the commit key becomes the ending** — Dave, on build 158: "it's weird to me that the cardio workout execution view assumes repetition. Like if I go for a run, usually I'm just gonna run, and then stop, not log rep."

He is right, and the interesting part is that the app already knew. `WorkUnit.kicker` refuses to print a count of one, the block bar hides at one, the island shows no progress at one, and the commit key already drops the noun at a block total of one — so a quick-started run said "Log", not "Log rep". The rule existed; it had just never been applied to the SHAPE of the ending.

**With one effort, "log it" and "finish" are the same decision, and the app asked for both.** A routine session already merged them (logging the last clock-hero set calls `finishSession` directly). An ad-hoc one did not — and ad-hoc is exactly what quick start creates, so going for a run ended at "All added exercises done. Add another, or finish and log the workout", which asks about repetition at the one moment there is none. `WorkoutSession.isSingleEffort` is the answer in one place, and the commit key both names the ending ("Finish workout", the exit dialog's own words rather than a second phrase for one state) and performs it.

⚠️ The condition is the SESSION, not the block. A run followed by core work must advance to the core, so it keys on nothing pending plus a single logged effort, never on `blockCount == 1`.

⚠️ It is live, not stored, and the async finish RE-READS it rather than trusting the value captured at log time — the StartFlash rule. Adding a second exercise during the 0.75 s beat has to call the finish off.

⚠️ **This does not strand the build-as-you-go path** (#239), which was the objection worth taking seriously: adding an exercise mid-session is already reachable from the overview sheet, so the interstitial was not the only way to reach it. It was just the only way that interrupted a run to ask.

**The other half, built on Dave's go-ahead: the control that DIVIDES an effort never borrows the rack's noun.** Two thirds of it turned out to be done already, which is worth recording so nobody re-derives it: both prescription sheets label that stepper `blockUnit.plural.capitalized`, so it has said "Pieces" on an erg and "Reps" on the track since #472; every cardio catalog row already defaults to `sets: 1`; and Rest hides at a count of one. What was left was the fallback. `ExerciseModality.workUnit` returns nil for exactly two families, walking and hiking — a walk is a walk, nobody does eight of them — and all three surfaces fell back to `?? .set`, so the app offered, on a walk, three SETS.

`WorkUnit.divider(_:)` falls back to `.round` instead. Rounds is sport-neutral, the app already owns the word (jump rope counts in them), and it keeps the CAPABILITY: hill repeats are a real thing to author even where one continuous effort is the honest default, so hiding the control outright would have traded one wrong answer for a missing one. ⚠️ It is scoped to the AUTHORING control. The kicker, the inline caption and the commit key keep the nil, so a walk still prints no `ROUND 1 OF 1` and its key still reads "Log" — a test asserts that separation, because a fallback that leaked into execution would undo the count-of-one rule this whole entry is about.

⚠️ Left alone deliberately: the record surfaces still say `?? .set`, so a logged walk's row reads "Set 1" and its card says "1 set". Same class of wrongness, different surface — a record describes what happened rather than what to author, and the count-of-one rule probably says that row should have no label at all. Worth its own pass.

**2026-07-31 — The catalog expansion round: 88 exercises + Foam Roller + 8 templates, three authored attributes, hidden search synonyms, and facet filtering returns** — Dave asked how comprehensive the built-in catalogs were; the audit found the gaps clustered (calisthenics progressions, oly lifting, everyday staples, prehab, warm-up drills, swimming; 68 of 101 equipment types gating exactly one exercise; PPL missing its Leg Day B) and Dave ordered all of it resolved plus three structural asks. Four sub-decisions, one PR:

1. **Content lands as data only.** 88 exercise rows (345 total), one equipment type (Foam Roller — passes #222's genuinely-requires rule; six rolls on a new `roll()` helper, the stretch shape), 8 templates (50 total), ~87 form cues. Everything rides the existing audit rules: technical bar lifts at doubles/triples, holds at honest seconds, steady cardio one piece, driver-hijack respected (Swimming's distance profile carries no fabricated duration). Swimming is equipment-free like Running — a pool is a place, not equipment — and joins the deliberately-silent locomotion set.
2. **Movement pattern · mechanic · laterality are COLUMNS on `BuiltInExerciseDefinition`** (app-side static, resolved through `Exercise.catalogDefinition`, nil for customs, zero migration/interchange/watch ripple — the `equipmentCategories` shape, chosen over parallel tables for in-row reviewability across a 345-row sweep). Mechanic is programming semantics (main lift vs single-focus accessory — core flexion work reads isolation), laterality's rule is "a SET belongs to a side" (alternating-within-a-set stays bilateral), and cardio carries no pattern (its identity is modality). The tripwire in `ExerciseAttributeTests`: a strength compound either names a pattern or sits in the test's `deliberatelyPatternless` set, exactly one of the two — because `mechanic` defaults `.compound`, a forgotten row fails loudly. Custom-exercise configurability of these is a deliberate NON-goal this round (follow-up issue).
3. **Synonyms are a hidden static table folded into the existing haystacks** (`CatalogSearchSynonyms`, name-keyed like FormCues), NOT a second demoted scoring pass — the muscle-group precedent is "typing reaches it", one pass per row per keystroke, and ranking ties still break by name. kb/db/trx derive from name families instead of forty hand rows. Genericized brand terms (trx, stairmaster, bosu) are legal as hidden terms — #222's no-brands rule governs NAMES. Highlighting stays name-only (an unhighlighted row reads fine), and `Collisions` stays exact-real-name-keyed so a synonym match never suppresses a create row. Operator's resolver deliberately does NOT read synonyms yet (its exact-not-fuzzy discipline; follow-up).
4. **Facet filtering returns, reversing 2026-07-25** (Dave: "bring filtering back", form delegated). The pick: a per-scope row of SINGLE-select `FacetChip` Menus pinned above the List via top `safeAreaInset`, led by `FilterSummaryChip` when active (the summarize-never-insta-clear law finally has its consumer), state in `CatalogFilterState` — a value struct per `CatalogScopeView` instance, never persisted, reset on scope change, applied in `FindOrCreateEngine` BEFORE scoring so facets narrow and the query ranks. Single-select because the deleted `MultiFacetChip` was a Menu multi-select and the 2026-07-28 law made that illegal (a multi-select is a list, not a Menu) — a facet that outgrows single-select gets a tray later without moving the architecture. The chips are REBUILT on `SelectableChip`'s tinted-ground anatomy; the git-history versions wear the retired solid blue and must not be copied. Kit availability remains NOT a filter (#113 — the missing-equipment disclosure is unchanged, and filters run before the partition so a filtered row still groups honestly). Known tension, shipped deliberately: a `safeAreaInset` is pinned, so the large title travels over the row on tab-root overscroll — catalogs have no `.refreshable`, so the exposure is cosmetic; on the device-pass list with "row as first list content" as the named fallback.

Follow-ups filed from the PR: ExerciseSimilarity gains the movement-pattern signal (Kit change, own eval); custom-exercise attribute editing; Operator synonym support; multi-select facet trays; splitting SeedData.swift. Dave has a separate session on filtering — this PR is the design of record; that session should rebase or stand down (his call: build it here).

**2026-07-31 — The Claude Code setup was audited against Anthropic's stated best practices and tightened in five places** — the audit (official docs + best-practices guidance vs this repo) found the setup strong on eight of ten themes; the gaps became this change set. (1) **`.claude/settings.json` gains a shared `permissions.allow` list** — swift test/build, the toolchain installer, read-only git plus the branch-workflow verbs (add/commit/checkout/push to `claude/*`), and read-only GitHub MCP tools. Dave develops mostly from the Claude iOS app, so every avoided permission prompt matters more than it would at a desk; writes that gate anything (merge, dispatch, force push) deliberately stay prompting. (2) **`app-surfaces.md` (48 KB, loading on every app-code task) split three ways by path scope**: `design-grammar.md` (color/keys/tags/motion/copy laws) and `navigation.md` (tab bar/search/scroll/landing laws) load only for view-layer work, and the slim `app-surfaces.md` map keeps the broad paths. Every ⚠️ law survived verbatim or compressed; the duplicated `.searchScopes` renders-once law was consolidated; superseded Find-or-create-era narrative was cut in favor of present-tense law. Net 48.4 KB → 40.4 KB, and Models-only sessions now load 2.8 KB instead of 48. (3) **CI's agent-doc budget gains a 24 KB per-rules-file cap** — the same mechanism that saved CLAUDE.md, because the line-length cap alone could not see a file-level monster; the remedy when it binds is split-by-scope, never raising it. (4) **A PreToolUse hook (`protect-generated.py`) hard-blocks Edit/Write inside `.xcodeproj` bundles** — generated by XcodeGen, gitignored, so any edit there is silently lost; the block message points at project.yml. (5) **Two workflow lines**: bug fixes write the failing test first (testing.md), and uncertain-scope work plans before editing (CLAUDE.md Session Discipline). Also recorded: **remote is the primary development surface** (CLAUDE.md Tooling) and the deliberate absence of a checked-in `.mcp.json` — a project-scoped XcodeBuildMCP entry would fail-load in every remote (Linux) session, which is now most of them.

**2026-07-30 (build 158 feedback) — One indoor bike, quick start on Today itself, and a rail that does not come apart** — three device-pass notes from Dave, all on the surfaces the cardio push touched.

**2026-07-30 (eighth) — A cardio record derives its own pace, and nothing that WRITES is allowed to read the derivation** — #302's second half (the count-up stopwatch shipped in #479). Distance and duration fix pace, so a record holding both was asking the user for a third number it could compute. `SetLog.actual(.pace)` now falls back to `LoggedActuals.pace` over the logged distance and the logged time, and `LoggedActuals.pace` forwards the profile's `paceReference` — without that a 1000 m pool swim in 20:00 printed 10:00 instead of 2:00, because the distance unit alone says a metric pool splits per 500 like an erg.

**Two clauses keep it honest, and both are the point rather than caution.** It derives only from ACTUALS, never from a plan: row 400 m of a planned 500 in 1:40 and the pace is 2:05/500m, not the prescribed 2:00 — the same fabrication `LoggedActuals` exists to stop, arriving by the back door. And it is never STORED, so it tracks its inputs: correct the duration afterwards and the pace follows, where a stored derivation would keep the old answer while claiming to describe the new one. That is `CardioTargets`' law one layer down, for the same reason.

**The interesting part is the split between `actual` and `storedActual`.** Once a value can be computed, every `actual(x) == nil` guard in the app quietly changes meaning from "nobody wrote this" to "and nothing to derive from either" — and three of them were load-bearing. The GPS write-back in `ActiveSessionView` fires on "not already edited", and would have stopped recording a MEASURED pace the moment an outdoor run had a distance and a duration, which is always; a meter's average is over moving time and the derivation divides by elapsed, so the measurement is the better number and has to be able to win. `WorkoutSession.complete` prefills every unset actual from its target, and would have frozen the prescription's pace onto the effort — worse, it would have won forever, since a stored value is the override. And `saveAsRoutine` graduates a session into a template, where a derived pace would have stored all three of distance/duration/pace, the exact state `CardioTargets` exists to prevent.

So `storedActual(_:)` is the pre-derivation lookup and the rule is one line: **`actual(_:)` to show a fact, `storedActual(_:)` to decide whether to write one.** `RoutineDiff.Prior` already reads `extraActuals` directly, so a derived pace cannot reach the ledger or manufacture a pace PR — which is #302's "needs care in the diff", answered by keeping derived values out of every comparison rather than by teaching the comparison about them. Prev on the set screen shows it, and that surface prints no delta by design (2026-07-28).

Not done here, deliberately: the record does not MARK a derived pace as derived. On a prescription the derived row is greyed with a caption because you are choosing which two to author; on a record there is nothing to choose, and the number is the same one the console showed. Revisit if a device pass says otherwise.

**2026-07-30 (seventh) — The island and the wrist show what the effort is actually doing** — Display only; sensor custody and the wrist's RUN quick-start stay #379's. The Dynamic Island read `SET 1 · 0/1 done` for forty minutes of a ride, which is three separate untrue things at once: the wrong noun (PR 1 fixed that), a count of one presented as progress, and no mention of the two numbers the phone was measuring the whole time. `ContentState` gains `distanceText` and `paceText` as additive optionals following the `isTransition` precedent. ⚠️ **Already FORMATTED, deliberately**, and for the same reason `workUnit` is a String rather than a `WorkUnit`: the widget extension has no profile, no distance unit and no pace reference to denominate a raw number with, and shipping three more fields so it could re-derive what the app already knows would be three more ways for the two to disagree. `progressLine` resolves to the cardio reading when there is one, the `n/m done` count when there is more than one round, and **nil otherwise** — "0/1 done" is the island's version of the count-of-one lie, and the progress BAR is hidden at a total of one for the same reason the app's own block bar now is. ⚠️ **The measured values are pushed, not date-derived**, unlike the elapsed clock which the island renders natively from `sessionStart`. Without a refresh they would freeze at whatever they read when the set began and sit there for the whole effort — a stalled number the island cannot tell you is stale, which is the exact class of lie this push exists to remove. A 30 s `Timer` publisher drives `syncActivityWorking`, gated on an outdoor effort actually running (not paused, not resting, not finished) so a strength session spends none of ActivityKit's update budget. ⚠️ It is `@State`, not a computed property and not a plain `let`: a publisher rebuilt on each body pass is a NEW publisher, `onReceive` resubscribes, `autoconnect()` restarts the timer from zero, and on a screen that re-renders every second a 30 s timer built that way never fires at all. Formatting happens app-side and reads the LOG's profile, so a pool swim's split reaches the Lock Screen as `/100yd`. **On the wrist**, the step view gains the distance line it never had: the watch has been measuring per-step distance since PR 2 (it is what a logged piece records) and simply never showed it, so a rower pulling a 500 had no way to know how far in they were. Same grammar as the pace line — live value first, target after the separator, accent once the target is met. ⚠️ Needs a device pass: the island through a real outdoor run, and the wrist through an erg interval.

**2026-07-30 (sixth) — Heart rate becomes a logged fact, per set, on every workout** — Reverses 2026-07-10, which made heart rate guidance-only with no actuals. The session-level `averageHeartRate`/`maxHeartRate` pair has existed since the Health work; what was missing is the unit that means anything. **A session average over an hour of lifting and resting says almost nothing**, and a 4 × 500 m erg piece wants four numbers, not one. So `SetLog` gains `actualAverageHeartRate` / `actualMaxHeartRate`, additive optionals mirroring the session pair, and they apply to EVERY workout rather than only cardio — a heavy triple spikes a heart rate too, and the set that spiked it is the interesting row. **Still not a `WorkoutMetric`**, and the reason is better than the one first given (lint compatibility, which is moot pre-release): a `WorkoutMetric` is *an editable quantity you dial* — step, range, wheel values, `incremented`/`decremented`, one `Double`. A heart-rate TARGET is a zone or a bpm range, which no scalar expresses (hence `HeartRateTarget`), and an ACTUAL is measured rather than set, so the stepper apparatus would be dead weight on both sides. **The window is the whole design.** Drawing it from the session start averages the rests in; drawing it from the previous set's completion folds the rest in and drags every set down. `currentSetStartedAt` is stamped on every cursor move — which fires for a new round of the same block as well as a new exercise, so it keys on the LOG's order rather than the exercise key — and the summary runs over that window at completion. ⚠️ It fires on the way into the rest screen too, which is correct: the next set's window starts when the last one ended, and the rest is the recovery, not the effort. **The wrist gets its own**, because the wrist is the device actually wearing the sensor: `WatchWorkoutController` accumulates a per-STEP reading list (the `HKLiveWorkoutBuilder`'s own statistics are session-wide, which is exactly the wrong window) reset by `beginStep()` at each log, and `WatchSync.StepResult` carries the pair home as additive optionals. A watch build that predates them decodes nil and the phone records nothing rather than inventing a number. Nil stays nil throughout — no sensor, no access, no samples — because a fabricated zero is the anti-shame rule's inverse. Rendered on the record screen between the set label and its result: a faint mono glyph and number, a fact about the set rather than its headline. Interchange three-step move done (`SetDTO` fields, the census, the round-trip assert, PLATFORM.md with the fields in its executable example). Kit 588 / CLI 26 green. **Deliberately deferred:** the on-demand HealthKit curve for the record screen (`HKStatisticsCollectionQueryDescriptor`), which is its own surface and its own empty-state problem — an empty result is indistinguishable from a denied read, so it has to say so plainly. ⚠️ Needs a device pass: log a set with a watch on and confirm the record shows a per-set number that differs between sets.

**2026-07-30 (fifth) — A cardio effort gets a hero, and the hero is a fallback chain** — A lifting set is a discrete thing you record after it happens; a cardio effort is one continuous thing you WATCH while it happens. The set screen only ever spoke the first dialect, and the gap showed worst on the efforts this whole push makes easy to start: **an untargeted cardio effort had no clock at all.** Quick-starting a run produced a distance card reading "—" with a Log key under it and nothing moving on screen, because `driver` falls back to the first tracked work metric (distance) and the timer dock was gated on `driver == .duration`. A studio ride, which now ships open-ended deliberately, was the same. And an untargeted TIMED effort was worse than nothing: `DurationTimerCard.totalSeconds` fell back to a hard-coded thirty seconds, so it counted down from a number nobody chose and logged itself at zero. **`CardioHero` (Kit, pure, 10 tests) is the chain**, and it is a chain rather than one rule because Dave's note was right that distance-to-go is not guaranteed: *progress toward a target when that metric is measurable RIGHT NOW* → *the best live measured value* → *elapsed, counting up*. ⚠️ `measurable` is what the DEVICE can read this second, not what the exercise tracks — duration always, distance and pace only while a location fix is fresh — and passing the tracked set instead would put distance-to-go on an erg. The chain always terminates, because the clock always runs, and a test asserts that over every profile-and-target combination the app can produce. ⚠️ It returns nil for rep work, which is the gate that makes every strength surface byte-identical; a test walks the whole catalog to prove no rep exercise gets a clock. **The degrade is spoken, not silent.** A five-mile run with GPS denied resolves to the clock and reports `unmeasurableTarget`, so the dock prints "no gps fix · timing it instead" rather than quietly showing a different number than the one you asked for. A distance-AND-duration prescription degrades to the duration countdown instead, since the clock can watch that. **The timer card has two shapes now.** Counting down is unchanged and keeps its quiet "log now", because a screen that completes on its own has no primary key (the rest-screen law, 2026-07-27). Counting up earns the filled commit cap, labelled in the sport's own noun ("Log effort", "Log piece"), because an open-ended effort has no other ending. Both are DATE-based like the rest timer, so backgrounding cannot drift them, and the clock rolls to h:mm:ss past the hour — a ninety-minute hike read "90:00" before, which is a minutes value on a clock face. ⚠️ The dock restarts on a `countdownSeconds` change, since the overview sheet can edit a live set and a countdown still running to the old number is the same class of lie. ⚠️ **`clockStageMetrics` is not `secondaryMetricsList`**: that list excludes the DRIVER, and on a calorie-driven effort the clock is the hero while calories still needs its card, so reusing it made the card vanish. **The block bar hides at a count of one**, the same law the kicker already follows: a count of one is not a count, and a single inert block stating nothing for forty minutes is worse than no bar. **Deliberately NOT in this PR** and tracked as the remainder of the plan's PR 7: auto-advance into the rest engine (the riskiest single change, and the rest engine's pause banking wants a device pass of its own), the per-effort console-entry step at the finish, the cardio finish recap, and spoken split cues. Kit 587 green on Linux. ⚠️ Needs a device pass: the hero across a steady run, a distance interval and an indoor erg, plus a run with Location denied.

**2026-07-30 (fourth) — The pace denominator splits off the distance unit, and swimming ships** — `DistanceUnit` baked the reference in: meters meant `/500m`, because the only meters-denominated exercise in the app was an erg and ergs speak splits per five hundred. That held until swimming, where a metric pool is ALSO denominated in meters and speaks `/100m`. One unit, two conventions, no way to say which — so swimming was **unexpressible** rather than merely missing, and adding the rows without this would have shipped a pool swim quoting erg splits. `PaceReference` (Kit, pure) is the denominator as a value in its own right, owning its label, its meters, its dialable range, its default and its wheel step; `DistanceUnit`'s five pace members are now thin forwards to `defaultPaceReference`, so **every number the app had before is asserted literally unchanged** in `PaceReferenceTests`. `MetricProfile.paceReference` is the override, absent meaning "the unit's own", which is why every profile ever written decodes byte-identically. ⚠️ An unknown reference from a future build falls back to the unit rather than failing the profile, the same tolerance `metrics` already has for an unknown metric. **`DistanceUnit.yards` joins** (25 yd stride and floor, a length of a short-course pool; splits dialed to the second like an erg's; mph on the speed dial, since yards is a US denomination). ⚠️ Adding an enum case is the class of change that broke `Exercise.modalitySymbolName` on PR 1: the Kit compiler caught two nested switches (`WorkoutMetric.stepChoices`, `MetricTape.scrubberTape`), but `RunRecordSection.splitLabel` is an APP file and Linux cannot compile those — it was found by grep and is now the only exhaustive switch over `DistanceUnit` outside the Kit, commented as such. **The reference is threaded, not half-threaded.** Every `WorkoutMetric` API that formats or bounds a pace takes an optional `paceReference` defaulting to nil, and every call site that has a profile passes it: the two prescription sheets, the set screen, the overview, the exercise editor, the picker (`MetricWheelSheet`), the scrubber, the increment sheet, `Prescription`, `MetricSummary` and `RoutineDiff.signedPace`. ⚠️ The `ExerciseDraft` clears the reference when the UNIT changes — it was chosen against the old denomination, and "/100yd" on a kilometer profile is not a thing anyone meant — and re-applies the stored one on load, which is why every `load` writes the unit first and the reference second. **Catalog:** Pool Swim and Open Water Swim, both `.swimming`, both in yards at `/100yd`, open water carrying `isOutdoor` so GPS engages and Health files it against the open-water location. ⚠️ Both AUTHOR their modality, for the third time and the same reason Running, Walking and Hiking do: they are equipment-free (a pool is not gear), so derivation cannot tell them from a road effort with identical metrics. They keep form cues rather than joining `deliberatelySilent` — swimming is skill cardio, like Rowing and Ski Erg, and stroke sequencing is real coaching. **Interchange three-step move done**: `paceReference` on `ExerciseDTO` and `SetDTO` under the same explicit-profile gate as `metrics`, written only when the profile OVERRIDES its unit; the census in `InterchangeMapping`; the round-trip assert (deliberately a MISMATCHED pair — per-100 m on a mile-denominated profile — so a mapping that silently re-derived the reference from the unit would fail rather than agree by accident); and PLATFORM.md, whose examples are executable, gaining a Pool Swim entry that `DocsConformanceTests` decodes and validates. Kit 573 / CLI 26 green on Linux. ⚠️ Needs a device pass: a pool swim's split should read `/100yd` on the prescription row, in the picker, on the set screen and in history.

**2026-07-30 (later still) — A cardio prescription is TWO of distance, duration and pace, and the sheet computes the third** — The planning sheet offered all three as equal steppers, which let you author a contradiction ("2 mi, 10:00, 6:00/mi") and then left the execution driver to be settled silently behind your back. They are not three independent numbers; any two fix the third. So the third now renders as a `DerivedMetricRow`: same 52 pt row, no field border, no ± pair, ink a step back, a lowercase `derived` caption. It is an ANSWER, not a control. **Deriving is not deciding**, so the row stays tappable — tapping PROMOTES the metric to one you set, and `CardioTargets.evicted` picks which of the other two steps back (pace if stored, else duration: one rule that always keeps the value you just touched and prefers to keep a metric that ENDS the effort over one that describes its rate). ⚠️ The order inside `promote(_:)` is load-bearing: read the computed value BEFORE evicting, or the inputs it is computed from are already gone, and the number you tapped is not the number you get. ⚠️ And the derived value is still never STORED (the PR 1 invariant) — `MetricProfile.driver` picks the highest-priority work metric with a target and distance outranks duration, so a stored derived distance turns "30 minutes at 9:00/mi" into an odometer. The law engages only where a profile tracks two or more of the triad (`CardioTargets.applies(to:)`), so **every strength sheet is byte-identical**; where the two stored values cannot produce a third (a zero distance, a missing partner) the row falls back to the normal stepper rather than showing a derived "—", which would state nothing and take the steppers away. Heart rate moves up to sit WITH the work targets on a cardio profile instead of after them: on a spin bike its old slot was below resistance, power and cadence, which buried the one cardio target that is actually a prescription under three console dials. Both sheets get the same treatment and the wiring is duplicated by hand between them, as `rowText`/`stepTarget`/`applyReps` already are — the part that could genuinely drift is the law, and that lives once in Kit. **Templates can now say it.** `RoutineTemplate.Entry` gained `extraTargets` and `Block` gained its own `restSeconds`, which is what #380 named as its blocker: "6 × 400 m with three minutes between" was previously unauthorable in either half. `Entry.workSeconds` runs the same `CardioTargets.estimatedSeconds` rule `Routine.workSeconds(for:)` does, so a template's number cannot drift from the routine it becomes, and the catalog ships six real cardio sessions (Steady Run, Quarter Mile Repeats, Erg 500s, Studio Ride, Trail Hike, Long Walk). ⚠️ Instantiation CLEARS the whole triad before applying an authored cardio target: the add path prefills from the exercise's bumped defaults (#187), so a "500 m at 2:00" template landing on an erg you last rowed for twenty minutes would otherwise arrive carrying that duration as a third value. ⚠️ The catalog content test widened with it — a timed exercise may now be prescribed by a clock OR by a cardio target, but never by nothing: an authored template with no target charges the flat per-set estimate, so "Steady Run" would advertise five minutes. ⚠️ Needs a device pass: promote each of the three in turn on a run and on an erg, and check the picker opens on the value you tapped.

**2026-07-30 (later) — The wrist records what it MEASURED, not what it was asked to do** — Found while auditing the cardio plan, and it is a correctness bug rather than a cardio nicety. `WorkoutRunView.log(_:)` composed a result by copying the plan: `step.targetWeight`, `step.targetRepsLower`, `step.targetDuration`, and `extras: step.extraTargets ?? [:]` — the prescription, labelled as the outcome. Row 400 m of a planned 500 m piece, tap Log, and the phone recorded 500 m. `WatchBridge` then did the same thing from the other end (`log.extraActuals = extras`, the step's own targets), so the lie was written twice. And `WatchSync.StepResult` had no extras bag at all, so a wrist-logged run could not carry its distance or pace home even in principle — while `WatchWorkoutController` was already running a real `HKWorkoutSession` with a `LivePaceMeter` computing both and throwing them away. **The distinction that fixes it is not planned-versus-actual but WHO KNOWS THE NUMBER.** Some metrics the user ASSERTS by tapping Log — a rep count, a load, a damper setting, an incline: they set the machine, they did the reps, and the tap is the claim. Carrying those forward is honest, and it is what "log as planned" has always meant on the wrist (weight editing is still a phone affordance). Other metrics the device MEASURES — distance, pace, calories — and there the plan is not evidence of anything. So `LoggedActuals` (Kit, pure, Linux-tested) records a measured metric when it was measured and **omits it when it wasn't**: a nil actual is honest and the phone can fill it from the console afterwards, where an inherited target is the anti-shame rule's exact inverse, manufacturing an achievement instead of scolding a shortfall. The same positive-measurement gate #378 applied to run summaries applies here (standing still for a whole piece measures zero metres, which is not a distance). ⚠️ Per-effort, not per-session: the run view stamps `stepStartedAt` and the odometer reading as each step's screen appears, so a 4 × 500 m erg comes home as four real splits rather than one session average — `LoggedActuals.pace` derives each from that piece's own distance and elapsed time, through the same `CardioTargets` math the prescription card uses. `StepResult.extraActuals` and `Step.modality` are additive optionals, so a watch build that predates them decodes nil and the phone records only the three columns. ⚠️ The fallback for that stale case is to record NOTHING extra, not to fall back to the targets — the old behavior was the bug. Kit 565 green on Linux. ⚠️ Needs a 2-device pass: row short of the target and confirm the record says what you actually did.

**2026-07-30 — Cardio stops lying: one modality resolves the Health type, the work-unit noun, and an honest estimate** — The audit that opened the cardio push found four statements the app makes that are simply untrue, and all four trace to the same absence: nothing in the app knew what KIND of workout was happening. A bike ride, a hike, a swim and an indoor row all filed to Apple Health as `.traditionalStrengthTraining`, because `HealthRecorder` and `WatchWorkoutController` each carried their own two-value ternary that could only say "outdoor run" or "everything else". A distance-target five-mile run estimated at 45 seconds, because `Routine.estimatedSeconds` charged a flat 45 s per non-duration set. A distance interval read `ROUND 3 OF 8` in the kicker, `Log set` on the button and `set 3/8` in the Dynamic Island, three vocabularies for one thing, because the `driver == .reps ? "set" : "round"` ternary was hand-rolled at eight sites and forgotten at a dozen more. And `Routine.isCardio` tested `allSatisfy { tracks distance or pace }`, which called an elliptical routine strength (it tracks neither — duration plus resistance) and a loaded farmer's carry cardio (it tracks distance). **The fix is one derived answer, in the Kit, that every surface asks.** `ExerciseModality` — which already existed, deriving a search-row icon from gear plus metrics — grew the cases it was missing (running, walking, hiking, swimming, elliptical, stairClimbing) and now answers `isCardio`, `searchTerms` and `displayName` too. `SessionModality` resolves a whole workout from its legs: one family names it, strength-plus-cardio is cross-training, several cardio families is mixed cardio, and mobility work never renames a session (a hamstring stretch after a lifting day does not make it a mobility workout). `SessionModality+HealthKit` maps that to `HKWorkoutActivityType` behind a `canImport` guard, so the phone and the wrist read ONE map and can no longer disagree — they did before, the phone filing anything with a GPS segment as an outdoor run while `PlanRoutine.isOutdoorRun` demanded every step be outdoor, so the same run-plus-core session recorded two different ways depending on which device logged it. `WorkUnit` carries the per-sport noun, researched rather than guessed: rowers do PIECES, runners and swimmers do REPS ("interval" technically names the recovery, not the work), cyclists do EFFORTS, lifters do SETS, and walkers and hikers do not do eight of anything, so their unit is nil. ⚠️ **The kicker disappears entirely at a count of one** — "EFFORT 1 OF 1" on a steady forty-minute ride implies a second one is coming. `CardioTargets` lands here rather than with the prescription sheet because the honest estimate needs it: distance ÷ pace is what makes a five-mile run read forty-five minutes. Its central invariant is that **a DERIVED value is never stored** — `MetricProfile.driver` picks the highest-priority work metric that has a target, and distance outranks duration, so a stored derived distance would silently turn "30 minutes at 9:00/mi" into a distance-driven odometer. Keeping derivation out of storage means the settled driver rule needs no exception, and which metric is derived falls out of which of the three are nil. Kit 555 / CLI 26 green on Linux. ⚠️ Needs a device pass: does a ride land in Fitness as a ride.

**2026-07-28 — A form that validates against a `@Query` must exempt the row it just wrote** — Dave: saving a brand-new custom exercise flashed "An exercise with this name already exists." on the way out. It did, and the editor was right to: `save()` inserts the `Exercise` into the context, the `@Query allExercises` sees it on the very next pass, and the sheet keeps rendering through its dismissal animation. On the create path the duplicate check excluded `editingExercise?.name`, which is nil — so for those last frames the field compared the draft to the row it had just created, went red, and dimmed Save under the user's finger as the sheet slid away.

**2026-07-30 — The smoke suite drifted from the app for six days because a non-required check can sit red and nobody finds out** — `ui-test` failed on EVERY push to `main` from 2026-07-24 (`b4702f0e`) to 2026-07-30 (`56083bc9`): 22 consecutive runs, every one of them merged on the three required checks, and builds 157 and 158 both cut in that state. #483 recorded it as four pushes since 2026-07-28, which was simply the window I had looked at; walking every `ci.yml` push run on main gave the real one.

**Nothing was wrong with the app.** Four tests, three unrelated causes, each one a screen changing shape while the only thing that could see it was already red:

- **#445** retired the custom `SegmentedTabs` control, and the schedule mode moved onto a `NavigationSelectRow` — a row that PUSHES its options. `testSetupTimelineOnboarding` was still tapping a segment that no longer existed. This is the one that went red first and alone, 2026-07-24.
