Shape copied from `RailGestureRecognizer`: a non-interactive probe reports geometry, the recognizers attach to the enclosing `UIScrollView`, and each row gates its pair to its own probe bounds — so N rows means N recognizers, fine for a dozen rail rows and precisely why the 157-row catalogs went native instead. ⚠️ Judged on TRANSLATION, never velocity: velocity at the instant a pan wants to begin is one noisy sample, and a thumb pivots at its base — it reads horizontal for a frame before it goes vertical. (`PopGestureGate` still reads velocity; that older gate is a candidate for the same treatment if it ever misbehaves on a pushed screen.) All six documented laws are preserved by hand: live commit at the halfway threshold (build 17), the 36 pt momentum floor (build 31, now fed by UIKit's `velocity * 0.1` projection), tap-vs-drag exclusion (build 33, now real arbitration), the gesture-inside-`.offset` ordering (build 36 / #276), cancellation reset (bug-hunt finding 3 — and UIKit reports `.cancelled` for real, which is the only reason `@GestureState` was needed there, so both vars are plain `@State` now), and both edges. ⚠️ ALL of this is CI-invisible: XCUITest dispatches through accessibility and bypasses gesture overlays, and `ui-test` is skipped on branch builds. Device pass, in order: diagonal flicks scroll every catalog; catalog swipes still favorite / toggle kit / delete on both edges and full-swipe commits; the ROUTINE-DETAIL RAIL is the risky half — swipe a rail row both ways, confirm DUPE/DELETE reveal and stay revealed on a gentle release, that a tap opens the exercise sheet, that a tap on a revealed action hits the action and not the row, and that the superset ring drag still works and still blocks row swipes while live. The rail commit is independently revertible.

**2026-07-27 (later still, SUPERSEDED same day) — The intermittent dead scroll is a MAIN-THREAD STALL in the sync dirty gate, not a gesture conflict (#169, hypothesis)** — ⚠️ **FALSIFIED**: Dave reported it happens while sitting on the same tab, and `.syncsProgramOnClose()` only fires on `onDisappear`. Kept because the underlying cost is real and still worth fixing on its own merits (see the fix direction at the end), just not this bug. Original entry follows. — Dave, on the fix above: "I'm experiencing the problem when trying to scroll via moving my thumb vertically roughly in the middle-right of the screen." Middle-right is simply where a thumb rests, which argues the cause is not spatial at all — it is temporal, and the trigger is a tab switch. `.syncsProgramOnClose()` is attached to every catalog root (and the editors), and a native `TabView` fires `onDisappear` on the tab you LEAVE, so every tab switch calls `GitHubSyncCoordinator.requestSync`. That schedules `Task { @MainActor in … }`, sleeps **1500 ms**, and then runs `hasLocalChanges` **synchronously on the main actor** — which is a full archive rebuild: `loadBase()` (read + decode the whole base snapshot), `InterchangeMapping.exportBundle` (fetch and map EVERY exercise, routine, equipment, library and finished session), `routeSidecars` (fetch every finished session and read `routeData` — an `.externalStorage` attribute, so **one disk read per GPS run**), `localFileMap` (JSON-encode every template and session file), then a `Data`-by-`Data` comparison of the whole map. All on the main thread, ~1.5 s after you land on the new tab — exactly when the first flicks happen. A blocked main thread means the touch sequence is delivered late and collapsed, which reads as "my swipe did nothing"; retry until the stall clears, then it scrolls. That accounts for every property of the report: intermittent (gated on `isConnected` and on scrolling inside the window), across many surfaces (the modifier is attached broadly), position-independent, and **worsening over time** (cost grows with session count and GPS routes). ⚠️ This is the cliff a previous session already wrote down and deferred: "the dirty gate materializes all route blobs per edit boundary — revisit with a digest before #381-era mileage." The mileage arrived. ⚠️ NOT yet confirmed on device and NOT profilable from a remote Linux session — the decisive cheap test is to disconnect GitHub and see whether the dead scroll disappears, since `requestSync` returns immediately when `!isConnected`. Fix direction (unbuilt, needs Dave's call): route bytes are immutable once a session ends, so the gate should compare a stored DIGEST rather than materializing blobs; and the pure part (encode + compare) belongs off the main actor, leaving only the context-bound fetches on it.

**2026-07-27 — The tab bar no longer minimizes on scroll** — Dave: "could we stop minimizing the TabView tabs on scroll? I think that's left over from an earlier experiment." It is. `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` arrived with the bottom accessory, whose `.expanded` / `.inline` PLACEMENT it drove — and placement was what decided whether the scope control showed icon + label or icon only. That control moved to the navigation bar and the accessory was deleted on build 149, so nothing in the app has read `tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement` since. What the modifier still did was shrink the bar out from under your thumb on the way down a catalog, on a surface whose whole job is scrolling a long list. Removed; the bar stays full size. Content passing under it is already handled by the `.soft` bottom scroll edge effect on each scrolling root (the third answer that edge has had — see 2026-07-26), which is the piece doing the occlusion work, not the bar's size. ⚠️ The read-only-placement law is kept as history on `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md` for whoever brings an accessory back: the minimize behaviour is what moves between the two placements, so `.inline` is a scrolled-down state, never the resting one.

**2026-07-26 (last) — The scope control is APP-PLACED, at the TOP, and search no longer empties the navigation bar** — four more builds after the Picker went native (144–147), each retiring one container. Recorded in full on `ScopeSegmentedControl` so nobody re-walks them.

**144 — `tabViewBottomAccessory`, edge to edge.** The double background was indeed the padding, and the control finally looked right. It also does NOT rise with the keyboard, so search's own keyboard buries it — and on this surface the keyboard is up most of the time you want to change scope. The container is retired for this control, not just this build.

**145 — a `.bottomBar` `ToolbarItem`,** the Photos Years/Months/All recipe (r/SwiftUI 1o2vdp4). It lands in the SAME ROW that `Tab(role: .search)`'s field expands into, so it renders behind the field. Photos gets away with it because its search is a small BUTTON in that row, not a full-width field. ⚠️ The piece that makes that recipe work at all is `.sharedBackgroundVisibility(.hidden)` on the item — a segmented control brings its own track, so the toolbar's shared glass would wrap it in a second shape. That modifier is TOOLBAR-ONLY, which is part of why the accessory could never be fixed.

**146 — app-placed as a BOTTOM `safeAreaInset`,** above the field. Same collision as 144 from the other direction: when the keyboard rises the field DETACHES from the tab bar and rises into the same bottom safe area an inset occupies, so the control stacks behind it.

**147 — a TOP `safeAreaInset`, under the navigation bar.** Nothing competes for that band, and since the search surface carries no title (`.navigationTitle("")` + `.inline`) the control fills a space that was empty anyway and names the catalog while it's there. It wears `.tint(Theme.background)` so the SELECTED segment goes dark, matching the tab bar's structure below it: lighter track, darker selection. 146 dropped that tint reasoning the dark pill would vanish into the dark row — which ignored the system's own track sitting between the two, and left the default lighter-pill look, the inverse of the bar it sits above.

**And the bottom scroll edge effect went `.hard` → hidden → `.soft` in one day.** Dave: "kill the blurred background area behind the TabView tabs row. I don't like that." `.hard` (build 139) exists because the bar's glass alone is too clear to occlude rows passing under it — section headings were legible below the search field — but it turns the system's dissolve into a full-width blurred slab for the floating bar to sit on, which is what he was looking at. Hiding it outright shipped on 148 and brought the read-through straight back: his own 148 screenshot has an equipment row's name legible through the search field and through the collapsed tab button. So the answer is the middle one, `.soft` — the system's gradient, which only shows where content is actually under the chrome and leaves an empty stretch clean. On the SCROLLING CONTENT, never a background on the bar, which is the mistake build 133 made with the hand-drawn one.

**And then the app took the whole bar row.** Dave, on build 150: make the control take the available width, less an even gap matching the ++ key's inset from the screen edge. Two rounds of trying to position it had failed for one reason — **a `.principal` item is a TITLE VIEW, and UIKit centres a title view in the BAR, not in the space left between the side items.** On his screenshot the control's centre sat at exactly half the screen width, so the gaps came out 76 pt left and 40 pt right: a difference equal to the difference between the two side items' widths (a 42 pt ++ key against a 78 pt kit switcher), which moves every time the kit's name changes. No padding constant fixes that class, which is why the 10 pt nudge of the round before was the wrong shape of answer. `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity)` did nothing either — the bar proposes an unbounded width to a title view, so the control simply took its ideal size, which is why the build "looked unchanged". The fix is to stop letting the system place three items: with NO leading or trailing items the title view gets the whole bar, so one `.principal` item holding the ++ key, the control and the switcher at an explicit `width - 32` lands the row on the app's own content column, and every gap in it is the app's to set. Width comes from a `GeometryReader` that only READS (never writes state — the morph law is about layout feeding back into state, which this doesn't do), and it is gated to the search surface: the closure re-runs on height changes too, and rebuilding the view re-runs the ranking pipeline, so leaving it on a scrolling catalog would re-rank the whole list whenever the tab bar minimised. Three details from the review round, each a small law: the control carries NO hard `minWidth` (an `HStack` already caps a flexible sibling at its share, and a floor makes the fixed-width ROW overflow and shear the keys off both ends of a narrow screen); the width is OPTIONAL so a zero-size first layout pass — which on the search tab is the first activation — leaves the row at its ideal size rather than collapsing it; and the control takes `.padding(.bottom, 4)` because `RaisedKeyStyle` pads every key by its travel to make room for the plate, seating the visible caps 2 pt above the row's centre.

**Then the control moved ONE ROW UP, into the bar itself** — Dave, same session: "could we give that segmented control the icons too… and make it sit between the ++ button and the kit switcher?" A `.principal` `ToolbarItem`, which is the slot the other four roots put their title in — and on this surface the control IS the title, which is why the surface carries none. That also retires the inset's one weakness: a band holding a control sat directly beneath a bar holding two keys with nothing between them. Two modifiers make a segmented control legal in a toolbar, and neither is obvious: `.sharedBackgroundVisibility(.hidden)` on the item (it brings its own track; the toolbar's shared glass would wrap that in a second shape — the same box-in-a-box that killed the accessory, and the exact piece the Photos recipe hinged on), and the `.avoidHidingContent` behaviour below, which is now load-bearing for the control and not just the keys. `.controlSize(.large)` comes off: that was the Photos proportion for a control owning a whole row, and in the bar it has to match the raised keys beside it. **And "icons too" turned out to mean icons INSTEAD.** On iOS a segmented control gives each segment a title OR an image and never both — `NSSegmentedControl` can do both, `UISegmentedControl` cannot, and SwiftUI inherits the UIKit limit: Apple DTS answered it directly (forums thread 816517), `.titleAndIcon` on a segmented picker renders the title and drops the icon, and the HIG says pick one, don't mix. The `Text("\(Image(systemName:)) \(label)")` interpolation was written first on the theory that one attributed `Text` sneaks past that; it was shipped-then-pulled the same hour, because a segment title resolves to a plain `String` and the glyph would have silently vanished — and the smoke test, loosened to a CONTAINS match in the same commit, could no longer tell that outcome from success. Glyphs are also the half that FITS: the principal slot is a residual (leading and trailing items are laid out first), and three words beside the ++ key and a variable-width kit switcher overflow it at rest on a 393 pt phone — a segmented control doesn't scroll, it truncates. Nothing is lost that the app doesn't say elsewhere: the tab bar carries the same three symbols WITH their words, and the field's placeholder names the scope. Each segment gets an explicit `.accessibilityLabel` (otherwise VoiceOver reads the symbol name), and the smoke helper falls back to POSITION if that label doesn't reach XCUITest, so a name miss can't masquerade as a missing control.

**And the toolbar keys stay put while search is active** (`.searchPresentationToolbarBehavior(.avoidHidingContent)`, iOS 17.1+). Activating search otherwise tells the navigation bar to clear its other content and give search the room — the system's `.automatic` behaviour, and the same mechanism that emptied the top band on build 143 before the title came off. The ++ key opens the drawer and the switcher changes kit; losing both the moment you tap the field is a dead end, not a decluttering.

**2026-07-26 (later still) — The accessory's scope control is the NATIVE segmented `Picker`, and hand-rolling one was never going to work** — Dave, after build 139: the pill still didn't move, and the whole control "looks jank." Three builds went round this (137 native-then-rejected, 138 hand-rolled bare segments, 139 the pill rewired to slide), so the research finally got done properly.

**The effect is the control's, not the styling's.** iOS 26's "bubbly" interactive glass — the selection that stretches and settles under your thumb — is available to exactly TWO components: tab bars and segmented controls. Nothing an app draws itself reproduces it. `ryanashcraft/FabBar`, which recreates the iOS 26 tab bar faithfully, ends up hosting a real `UISegmentedControl` and overlaying custom labels on it for precisely this reason, and its author notes that mixing custom UIKit controls with SwiftUI's `.glassEffect()` costs framerate during touch. So the hand-rolled row was chasing something structurally out of reach, and no fill was going to close the gap.

**And the pill genuinely could not animate there.** Build 139 rewired it to the canonical `matchedGeometryEffect` form — segments publishing source frames, one persistent pill following the selected id, animation on the value rather than the tap site — and it STILL didn't travel on device. The accessory is a system-owned container that re-renders outside the app's animation transactions, so neither `withAnimation` at the tap site nor `.animation(value:)` reliably reaches into it. Worth recording as a general constraint: **do not expect app-authored animation to survive inside `tabViewBottomAccessory`.**

**The "double background" was caused by PADDING.** The accessory always draws a Liquid Glass capsule and no API removes it. Build 137's Picker then carried `.padding(.horizontal, 12)`, which inset the Picker's own segmented track inside that capsule — two concentric shapes, the box in a box Dave rejected. Edge to edge the two silhouettes coincide instead of nesting. That reframes the whole 137 rejection: the Picker was never the problem, the inset was.

**The fallback, if a double survives edge-to-edge**, is Apple DTS's answer on developer forums thread 803030 (the same question, asked about Photos' Years/Months/All): render the segmented Picker ONLY in `.inline` placement — where the accessory merges into the bar row and brings no capsule of its own — and put something else in `.expanded`. That sample also sets `.tabViewStyle(.sidebarAdaptable)`, which the engineer called essential to the accessory behaving. Same thread documents a rendering bug where the segmented control comes out invisible inline, so that placement needs a device check rather than trust.

Labels still adapt to placement (words `.expanded`, glyphs `.inline`, where three words collide; the accessibility label stays the word either way), and the control takes no `.tint` — in the accessory it wears the iOS 26 system look, the same call the app's other three native segmented pickers took. Smoke tests move from app-set `scope-<case>` identifiers, which a native control doesn't have, to `app.segmentedControls.buttons[label]`.

**2026-07-26 (later) — The catalog tabs LEAVE THE BAR while search is active, and the scope pill is rewired to actually slide** — Dave's build-138 device pass, five items, all chrome.

**`Tab.hidden(_:)` gets what the cancelled nested-TabView cut was for.** Dave asked, in that round, for Today to be the tab beside the search field rather than whichever tab he came from — and the reason the nested cut existed at all was that there seemed to be no other way to change which tabs the bar carries. There is: `.hidden(_:)` on a `Tab`, which **preserves the hidden tab's state** where a conditional `if` destroys and recreates it. Hiding the three catalog tabs while search is active leaves exactly one non-search tab, so Today lands beside the field with no nesting and no lost navigation stacks. Scope is the accessory's job in there anyway, so three tabs saying the same thing were redundant.

**The bar needs a flag the app owns.** `tab` is written by the system's `TabView` selection binding, outside any animation transaction of ours, so driving `.hidden` and the accessory's `isEnabled` off `tab == .search` makes both snap. A `searchActive` mirror set inside `withAnimation(Theme.Anim.standard)` fixes it, and because both read the SAME flag the tabs collapsing and the accessory arriving are one movement rather than two. The accessory's content also carries a `.transition(.opacity.combined(with: .offset(y: 8)))` — the row's height change belongs to the system, the fade and rise are ours, which is the shape of Apple's own `tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled:)` example. ⚠️ `land(on:)` clears `searchActive` BEFORE writing `tab`: a landing selects a tab that is hidden at that instant, and a selection the bar drops is a silent dead tap (the build-76 class), so the tabs are given back first rather than asking the bar to select something that isn't there.

**The scope pill was wired backwards.** Dave: "the background of that segmented picker doesn't animate its position." Build 138 gave each segment its own conditional pill, all sharing one `matchedGeometryEffect` id — but only one exists at a time, so a scope change is an INSERT and a REMOVE with no single view whose frame can travel between the two positions. It cross-faded in place. The canonical form inverts it: each segment publishes an invisible `Color.clear` SOURCE frame, and ONE persistent pill in the container's background matches `id: scope, isSource: false`. One view, one identity, a target frame that moves. The animation also moved off the tap site onto `.animation(Theme.Anim.selection, value: scope)` — the accessory lives in a system-owned container that can re-render outside our transaction, so a `withAnimation` around the binding write was never something to lean on, and the value form also covers scope changes made from elsewhere.

**The pill's fill is `.ultraThickMaterial`** (Dave's call, after `.thinMaterial` in 137 and an opaque `Theme.surfaceRaised` in 138 both disappeared into the accessory's glass), plus a hairline `Theme.borderStrong` stroke so the edge is defined whichever way the material resolves. ⚠️ Worth knowing: system materials are veils of the SYSTEM background, so in dark mode a thicker material goes DARKER, not lighter — the pill reads as a capsule punched into the glass rather than one sitting on it. That is real contrast, but it is not the light platter a light-mode segmented control draws; the opaque `Theme.primaryFill` + `Theme.onPrimary` pair is the route to that if the dark direction reads wrong.

**Today's tab icon joins the calendar family**: `.toDo` → `calendar.badge.clock`, `.done` → `calendar.badge.checkmark`, `.clear` → `calendar`. Not `calendar.badge.exclamationmark` for the to-do state, deliberately — the app doesn't draw alarm glyphs any more than it writes "due" (#172, anti-shame). The three states themselves are unchanged (Dave's own 2026-07-24 call that any workout today flips the icon to a checkmark).

**Scroll position carries between a catalog tab and search.** A `Tab`'s content is its own view tree, so the Routines tab's list and a search tab dialled to routines are two different scroll views showing the same rows — scroll one, tap Search, and you were back at the top, which is the discontinuity that most broke the "one surface changing scope" feel. A `scrollAnchors: [FindScope: AnyHashable]` in the root is the only place both can see. Two gates make it behave: the root's setter only writes when this is the SHOWING tab (all four instances stay mounted, so an ungated setter lets three lists nobody is looking at overwrite the one that is), and `CatalogScopeView`'s only writes on an EMPTY query (a ranked result set is a different list; letting it write would mean leaving search scrolls the whole catalog to wherever your last query happened to land). Reading is unconditional in both, which is what makes the restore work in both directions.

⚠️ **`.scrollPosition(id:)` is the first thing to delete if the search field ever stops appearing on a cold first tap.** It is a continuous state write from inside the TabView subtree — the family that has broken `Tab(role: .search)`'s morph three times on this branch (nav-diag 4e). It is narrower than the `.onGeometryChange`/`PreferenceKey` probes that caused it (one write when the top row changes, not a layout-phase callback), and Dave's call was to ship it with an easy revert rather than hold it. Nothing else in the round depends on it. If the `id:` form turns out not to take on `List`, drop the feature rather than escalating to the iOS 18 `ScrollPosition` + `scrollTo(y:)` form, which observes scroll GEOMETRY and is a worse bet against the same bug.

**2026-07-26 — FIVE TABS over ONE screen: Today · Routines · Exercises · Kit · Search, all four catalog tabs rendering the same view** — Dave, across three device rounds in one day. The end state reverses the tab COUNT of the day before while keeping everything that round proved, and it is the arrangement build 131 reached for before `CatalogScopeView` existed to make it honest: the catalogs are tabs again, but they are now tabs over ONE screen rather than four screens. A tab decides which catalog, never which screen.

**The route there, in order, because each step killed an option.** (1) `Tab(role: .search)` is SETTLED. The role is a package deal — the system floats search apart as the separated circle AND gives it the bar→field morph — and Dave wanted the second half ("the sort of search tab that makes the search input expand out of it to the side"). Proven by trying the alternative: a PLAIN search tab seats it in the group beside Today (build 135, Dave's ask) but leaves `.searchable` hunting the navigation bar this screen HIDES, so there was no visible input at all ("I don't see how to open the search input"); `DefaultToolbarItem(kind: .search, placement: .bottomBar)` + `.searchToolbarBehavior(.minimize)` rendered NOTHING either. A custom header magnifier (build 136) works but Dave killed it — the field belongs at the bottom, expanding out of the tab. (2) The scope control is a BARE SEGMENT ROW, not a `Picker(.segmented)`: the Picker brings its own segmented backing, which inside the accessory's glass capsule read as a box in a box (Dave's screenshot). `ScopeSegmentedAccessory` draws no background of its own — plain segments on the glass the accessory already provides. The custom `InlineWheelPicker` is deleted again, `SegmentedTabs` and `SearchScopeBar` stay deleted. (3) The catalogs go back in the bar, and the accessory shows ONLY while search is active: off search the tab bar IS the scope control, and two of them at once is one too many.

**"Feel like just switching search scopes"** was Dave's governing constraint, and it ruled out the two obvious implementations. Gating each tab's content on being selected, and `.id(scope)`, both tear the surface down and rebuild it on every switch — which is exactly what stops a scope change feeling like one. So nothing re-mounts: the views stay alive and `CatalogScopeView` resets in place, with an `.onChange(of: scope)`, only what genuinely cannot survive a scope change (its navigation path, its expanded missing-equipment groups).

**Two consequences of "a `Tab`'s content is its own view tree"** — the finding banked from the cancelled nested-TabView cut, now load-bearing rather than theoretical. Four catalog-showing tabs means four live INSTANCES of `CatalogScopeView`, and the Routines tab and a search tab dialled to routines are both showing routines. (a) Every broadcast needs ONE named owner, or an arrival's entrance flash plays on the surface you are being taken off: `ownsLandings` (`tabKey == scope.tab.rawValue`) makes the catalog TAB the consumer of arrivals and Operator pushes, never "whichever instance shows that scope". (b) A tab's content is built on FIRST selection, so a notification alone reaches nobody on a tab that has never been visited — the build-76 silent-dead-tap class. Every cross-tab landing therefore rides a pending SLOT consumed on receive OR on appear; `RoutineArrival` already worked this way, and Operator's routine push (a bare post until now) gained `OperatorArrival` + a single `OperatorDestination.show` door that both posting sites go through.

**The three catalog tabs pass their scope as a LITERAL**, and only the search tab reads the shared `scope` state. Reading it in all four would render one frame of the OUTGOING catalog before `onChange(of: tab)` caught up — a flash of the previous list on every switch. That leaves `scope` meaning exactly one thing: which catalog SEARCH is looking at, following whichever catalog tab you last picked, so opening search narrows where you already were.

**The query belongs to search and dies with it.** It carries across scope changes made INSIDE search (dial Kit while "bench" is typed and you see equipment matching bench), but leaving for a catalog tab clears it: no other tab has a field, so a surviving query would filter a list with nothing on screen explaining the filter and no way to clear it. That is the existing "a stale invisible query reads as data loss" law, not a new one.

**Return no longer navigates.** `openTopResult` and `.plusplusOpenTopResult` are deleted, along with the last `onSubmit` passthroughs in `HeaderSearchField`/`SearchFieldBody`/`CatalogTabHeader`. Submitting a search puts the keyboard away; it does not choose a result (Dave). It also takes the stacked-duplicate-push hazard with it — `path.append` is not idempotent.

**The accessory reads like a tab strip now.** The `.thinMaterial` pill was near-invisible against the glass it sat on (Dave: "the background on it is nearly invisible, it's such low contrast"), so the selected segment wears an OPAQUE `Theme.surfaceRaised` fill that SLIDES between segments via `matchedGeometryEffect` on `Theme.Anim.selection` — #216's law for this control, and what the retired `SegmentedTabs` did. Labels follow the placement the system hands out: icon + label when `.expanded`, icon ONLY when `.inline`, where three words collided in Dave's screenshot. The words survive in VoiceOver either way.

**2026-07-25 — TWO TABS and a SCOPE WHEEL: the bottom chrome goes back to the system** — Dave, after build 133. The hand-drawn `AppBottomBar` shipped three device rounds and its last one produced three bugs at once: content scrolled through the bar with nothing making it legible, the bar had no clearance above the home indicator, and the three scope labels sat off a common baseline. Every one of those is something a real tab bar does for free, so paying for them by hand a fourth time was the wrong trade. **`TabView` now carries two tabs — Today and Search (`Tab(role: .search)`) — and the three catalogs became a SCOPE you dial**, on the build-125 `InlineWheelPicker` restored from history and hosted by `ScopeWheelAccessory` in `.tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled:)`. The accessory slot is the one place the system agrees to put app content beside its own bar, so hit targets, Liquid Glass, scroll-edge legibility and safe-area clearance are the platform's again.

**Placement is the system's, not the app's.** `tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement` is a read-only environment value and `.inline` means "inline with the MINIMIZED tab bar", so `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` is what moves between the two: at the top of a scroll the bar is full and the wheel rides its own row above it; scrolling down minimizes the bar and the wheel goes inline with it, which with only two tabs reads as the wheel sitting between Today and Search. That is Podcasts' behaviour and it is SCROLL-driven — inline is a scrolled-down state, not the resting one, and the app cannot pin it. Worth re-testing if a minimize behavior that forces minimization ever turns up.

**An intermediate NESTED-TabView cut was built and cancelled** (outer Today · Routines · Exercises · Kit · Search, inner Routines · Exercises · Kit, with the outer catalog tabs dropped while search was selected so Today stood alone beside the field). Dave killed it before CI reported; it is one `git revert f5aafa7` away. Two findings survive it and constrain any future tab-bar arrangement: **a `Tab`'s content is its own view tree**, so a catalog reachable from two places exists twice with no way to share it, and **a native `Tab` item is not a view the app can decorate**, so per-scope counts can never ride tab labels.

**Two things the wheel's return required.** Its selection band used to be sized by a hidden label stack behind a `GeometryReader` + `PreferenceKey` + `onPreferenceChange` — a state write during layout, which is the documented iOS 26 trigger (nav-diag 4e) for `Tab(role: .search)`'s morph failing on FIRST activation, and the wheel now lives in the tab bar's own accessory. The band is measured from `UIFont` metrics instead: synchronous, writes nothing, three fixed words. For the same reason TodayView's onboarding step-height `.onGeometryChange` is gone; the reveal-upward headroom is now structural — the bottom setup step gets a viewport-tall top-aligned box, which is simultaneously the scrollable space it needs to reach the top and exactly the maximum downward scroll.

**Two costs, both accepted.** Per-scope match counts are retired: there are no scope labels in the bar to paint them on, and fitting a changing number into the wheel would re-measure its band on every keystroke and make it twitch under the thumb — the wheel itself is the cross-scope affordance now, one flick away. And switching scope resets that catalog's scroll and navigation path, because the search tab hosts ONE `CatalogScopeView` keyed `.id(scope)`; without the id SwiftUI reuses the view and the previous catalog's pushed detail, expanded groups and arrival slot come along with it. Keeping three mounted would fix the reset but fights `.searchable` needing a `NavigationStack` at the tab's root.

**2026-07-25 — The catalog tabs and the search scopes are ONE view: `CatalogScopeView`. Four screens deleted** — Dave, closing the loop he opened at the top of this effort ("the universal search views should replace those older tabbed views"): tapping **Routines** with search closed and scoping to **Routines** with it open now land on the same screen, so search adds a query rather than taking you somewhere else. `RoutineListView`, `ExercisesTabView`, `EquipmentTabView`, `FindOrCreateView` and the pushed `EquipmentCatalogScreen` are all GONE, absorbed into one file. `RootTabView` mounts one `CatalogScopeView` per `FindScope`, visible on `tab` alone — **nothing there keys on `searching` any more**, which is the structural proof the two surfaces really are one. The view has two modes: `.tab` (its own `NavigationStack`; the query arrives as a binding because the field lives in `AppBottomBar`, outside every tab) and `.presented(setupMode:)` (pushed chrome with its own expanding header field, an item destination per #291, and onboarding's pinned Done bar) — the second is what the equipment catalog was, so Today's setup step 1, the drawer's "Edit your kit", the picker's filter escape and the template gear-check all now open the Kit scope itself.

**All three scopes read alike: MINE then CATALOG, and no facet chips at all.** Dave decided both. The Kit tab therefore stops meaning "my kit" and becomes "equipment, mine first" — the whole catalog, one swipe from membership — which is exactly why the pushed catalog could be retired rather than kept beside it. Dropping the chips ("make it work just like routines and equipment") costs the Exercises tab its persisted Favorites/Muscle facets and the equipment catalog its Type tray and Name/Most-exercises sort; the field replaces them, because muscle groups already sit in `ExerciseFilterState.searchHaystack` and equipment categories in the equipment scorer, so typing "chest" or "bench" reaches what the chips reached. The `exerciseCatalog.*` `@AppStorage` keys died with them. Routines render as **cardless rows** outside Today (Dave) — a catalog list reads flat; the card stays where a card earns its weight, on the timeline.

**One swipe law across all three: LEADING is curation, TRAILING is destructive.** Exercises lead with FAV/UNFAV and trail with DELETE on customs only; Kit leads with ADD/REMOVE membership (the retired catalog screen's quick-add, now on the tab) and trails with DELETE on customs; Routines have no curation flag, so they only trail with DELETE. Catalog templates have neither and are plain rows. **The row context menus are gone** — they existed because search rows had no swipes, and unified the swipes ARE those acts, so the menu could only duplicate them. It also had to go: on the routines scope its long press fought `.onMove`'s, which is the same gesture, and reorder is load-bearing there. The one act with no other home on this surface, "Start", stays on Today and on the routine's own screen.

**Reorder narrowed to where it means something:** `.onMove` fires only on the routines scope, in tab mode, with an EMPTY query, over the MINE tier's doable section. A ranked or narrowed list has no order to write back, and writing one would silently destroy the user's drag-ordering. The companion guard is already in the engine: `rank(_:query:)` is a stable partition on `mine` when there's no query, preserving `Routine.order` instead of re-sorting alphabetically.

**`FindOrCreateEngine.matchCounts` is deleted.** Each scope's surface stays mounted and already builds its own sections, so it publishes its own count by summing them (`nil` at rest, so labels stay bare). Counting all three centrally meant a second full ranking pass over every type on every keystroke, on top of the one the visible surface was already running. Its three unit tests were rewritten against the section sums, which is now the real contract.

**Cardless is chrome, not content** (swift-reviewer catch, same day): the first cut of the routine row dropped the meta line with the card, so the Routines tab read as a list of bare names — no schedule, no estimate, no equipment — while Today still showed `focus · estimate` for the same routine. The tell was that `RoutineCardContent`, build 107's one routine body, ended up with zero call sites. Both routine and template rows render it again, and it gained `nameHighlight` + `leadingCapsules` so the search highlight and the "has <exercise>" match explainer ride the shared body rather than forking it.

**The PRESENTED equipment catalog stays one flat alphabetical run**, while the Kit TAB groups MINE/CATALOG. Not an inconsistency: that surface is where you ADD, and with the tiers every quick-add lifts the row you just swiped out of its place and drops it at the top, shifting the rows under your thumb. Losing your scroll position on every add is the opposite of "pick, tune, keep moving", and it lands hardest in onboarding step 1, which is a run of eight adds. The in-kit checkmark carries membership there; the split earns its keep on the tab, where you arrive fresh and want yours first.

**The facet-chip components are DELETED, not merely unused** — `FacetChip`, `MultiFacetChip`, `TrayFilterChip`, `FilterSummaryChip`, `SortChip`, `ActiveFacet`. Every filter row in the app went at once (Exercises' Favorites + Muscle, the equipment catalog's Kit facet + Type tray + sort, the picker's Muscle/Equipment/Favorites bar), so they were dead the moment the last one did. `FilterChipShape` stays: it is the rounded-rect key rule (r11, shape carries role), not a filtering one, and a dozen call sites lean on it.

**Also retired:** `FindOrCreateLaunch` and `.plusplusFindOrCreate` — the tabs' Add rows deep-linked into a pre-scoped search, which once the tab IS the scope means navigating to where you already are, so they became direct creates keeping the identifiers the smoke flows use (`newRoutineButton` / `createExerciseRow` / `addEquipmentRow`); onboarding step 2 now just lands on the Routines tab, which is the routine catalog. Empty states: with CATALOG always present neither list can empty, so "No routines yet" went, but the null-kit explanation stayed as a hint row above an empty kit — nothing else carries it. ⚠️ Device-only, all of it: the swipes on all three scopes, routine drag-reorder inside MINE, the three arrival choreographies, and onboarding step 1 now running on the Kit scope.

**2026-07-25 — Search ABSORBS the three catalog tabs into its field; the scopes ride above it as the TabView's bottom accessory. The chrome stays NATIVE** — Dave, closing out "is Find-or-create just a second copy of the Routines/Exercises/Kit tabs?": it partly was, but the fix isn't to delete either side, it's to make them ONE control. **The chrome is the platform's** (his correction mid-round, reaffirming build 10's trade): a native `TabView` with a native `Tab(role: .search)`, so the bar's morph into the search field, the Liquid Glass treatment, hit targets and accessibility all come from the system. An earlier cut of this round hand-rolled the whole bottom bar in order to pin Today beside the expanding field — Dave rejected it: the tabs and search are supposed to be SwiftUI's own. **What the app adds is the SECOND ROW:** while the search tab is selected, the three catalog scopes ride above the field in `.tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled:)` (new `SearchScopeBar`), so the tabs the field absorbed are still present as the thing you narrow by. **Today is a TAB, never a scope** — it holds a timeline of derived state (due-ness, carried-over, committed history), not a list of typed items, so there is nothing in it to search; that dissolves the "Today makes no sense with search" problem instead of papering over it. **`All` is gone.** In its place: **an empty query shows the scope's WHOLE list, grouped exactly as its tab groups it** (Dave) — search opens onto the content you were already looking at and typing narrows it, rather than replacing it with a blank screen. The "require more equipment" group now splits **inside each tier** (`MISSING_MINE` / `MISSING_CATALOG`) rather than pooling at the end: MINE/CATALOG is the primary division and kit availability the secondary, so yours-that-need-equipment stays with yours. Cross-scope discovery rides **per-scope result counts on the scope labels** (Dave: "just show the number of results in the tab labels, and no number when not searching") — the count sits on the very control that switches you there, so no link rows and no new row type enter the list. **Engine:** `FindScope` lost `.all` and gained `label` / `symbolName` / `searchNoun` (the Kit scope searches the equipment CATALOG, not your kit, so its prompt reads "Search equipment" — the kit-vs-equipment vocabulary law); `Section` lost `scopeTarget` / `moreCount` and the All-scope capping with them; new pure `matchCounts(query:…)` feeds the labels, computed by the SURFACE and published up through a binding (counting at the root needed duplicate always-live `@Query`s whose every change re-evaluated the whole tree, plus a fourth ranking pass per keystroke). **Deployment target raised to iOS 26.1** — `tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled:)` landed there and is the only form that reclaims the accessory's reserved space when off; the always-present form would leave a blank strip above the tab bar on every other tab. Orphaned and deleted: `InlineWheelPicker` (built for the retired scope wheel) and `CreateMenuRow` (built for the retired All-scope create chooser). An adversarial review of the interim custom-bar cut also caught two regressions worth recording because they generalize: a search field living OUTSIDE the results' `NavigationStack` stays submittable over a pushed detail (`path.append` is not idempotent — guard root-only affordances on `path.isEmpty`), and hiding tab roots by opacity rather than unmounting silently kills every `onDisappear`-based trigger (`syncsProgramOnClose`). Both are moot in the shipped native shape, which unmounts tabs normally, but the classes are real. **The rise is a TRANSITION, not a matched move** (Dave's call, after asking for a `matchedGeometryEffect`-style morph from the tab positions): native `Tab` items aren't views the app can address, so a tab label and an accessory chip can't share a geometry namespace — the only way to get a control that visually sits among the tabs and then travels is custom chrome, which was already rejected this round. So the accessory content rises with `.move(edge:.bottom)` + opacity, and `SearchScopeBar` reads `tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement` to tighten and drop its counts when the system places it `.inline` with a minimized bar (the Podcasts now-playing two-mode pattern Dave pointed at — note that its inline state is a MINIMIZED bar with the other tabs hidden, not an accessory wedged between full-size tabs, so "a segmented control between Today and Search while the whole bar shows" is not a placement iOS offers). ⚠️ **Device-only, #1 CHECK: whether the bottom accessory renders above the search field while the search-role morph is active** — that combination is undocumented, and the fallback (scope row inline in the surface) is the contingency if it doesn't. Then: the rise animation, the row against the keyboard, scope switching mid-query, counts at large Dynamic Type, and the standing iOS 26 `.onGeometryChange` morph bug (TodayView's onboarding probe, nav-diag 4e) which returns along with the search-role tab. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-25 — Kit-availability FILTERS are retired for a collapsible "N … require more equipment" group, doable-first, on Find-or-create / Exercises / Routines** — Dave: "kill the 'doable' filter and any other kit membership exercise and routine filters we may have elsewhere. Replace it with a collapsible 'missing equipment' group in the list / results, after the routines or exercises that are doable with the current kit. Collapsed by default." So the two availability FILTERS are deleted — the Find-or-create "Doable" `@AppStorage` chip (`FindOrCreateView`/`FindOrCreateEngine.doableOnly`) and the Exercises tab's 4-mode `GearMode` "Equipment" facet (`ExerciseFilterState.gearMode`/`pickedGearNames`/`matchesGear`, its `FacetChip`, and `GearPickSheet`) — and nothing is HIDDEN by kit availability anymore. Instead every list keeps the un-doable items but tucks them under a collapsible disclosure placed AFTER the doable ones, collapsed by default. **Header copy (Dave's own wording, over five proposed directions):** a sentence with a count + toggle chevron — "1 exercise requires more equipment" / "N exercises require more equipment" (noun "exercise"/"routine"), built once in `MissingEquipmentPhrasing.header(count:noun:)`. It describes the ITEMS (they require the equipment), not an obligation on the user, so it clears the no-obligation law and mirrors the row-level "needs X"; the header is deliberately NEUTRAL ink, not amber (amber stays the per-row advisory; an amber header would read as an alarm over a whole group). New shared row `MissingEquipmentHeaderRow` (`Views/Components/MissingEquipmentDisclosure.swift`), a plain scrolling row (not a pinned section header) whose whole surface toggles; collapse state is ephemeral `@State` per surface (collapsed on every entry — search state is ephemeral too). **Surfaces (Dave: "Routines tab too"):** three, including the Routines tab, which reverses that surface's flag-don't-hide behavior (#113) into doable-first + a collapsed missing group — on the Routines tab `.onMove` reorder now sits on the doable group only and `moveRoutines` reindexes `order` as (reordered doable)++(missing). **Engine:** `FindOrCreateEngine` stops dropping rows — `Result` gained `doable: Bool`, `Section` gained a `.missing(noun:)` `Kind` + an explicit `id` (so All-scope "MISSING_ROUTINES"/"MISSING_EXERCISES" never collide with the doable "ROUTINES"/"EXERCISES"), and `sections` partitions per type (All: capped doable overview then a missing group per type that still appears when a type has only missing results, so an only-missing query never empties; scoped: MINE/CATALOG for doable, then one uncapped missing group). Doability reuses the canonical helpers (`missingEquipment` / `gearAvailability` / template `equipmentNames`). The doable-hiding empty state + "Show all" key are gone (nothing hides now). Doability-needing cross-tab arrivals (`consumeArrival`) expand the missing group so a fresh item's entrance flash never plays on a collapsed row. `ExerciseFilterState.filteredExercises(from:kitNames:)` keeps `kitNames` in its signature (now unused) so its two callers don't churn; `kitDoableAlternatives`/`swapSuggestions`/`selectedEquipment` stay (substitution + the picker's uses-this-equipment axis, not kit-membership). App-target only; engine is pure + unit-tested (rewrote the `FindOrCreateEngineTests` doable-filter block into partition tests; deleted the `gearMode` `ExerciseFilterTests` case for a `missingEquipment` primitive test). ⚠️ Device-only (CI-invisible): the collapse/expand animation + chevron on all three surfaces, the arrival-reveal of the missing group, Routines drag-reorder within the doable group after the split. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — Find or create: the search field is the NATIVE `.searchable`, per-scope placeholder, no auto-focus (supersedes the same-day custom bottom-bar takeover)** — Dave: use the native search input and its native close button; make the placeholder "Search" on All and "Search routines / exercises / equipment" when scoped; and don't auto-focus the field on entry. This reverses the custom bottom-bar takeover decided earlier the same day (which hand-rolled the field with `SearchFieldBody` + a text Done key inside a `.safeAreaInset` bottom bar, explicitly to dodge the iOS 26 morph bug). Implementation: `FindOrCreateView` drops the custom bottom bar and attaches `.searchable(text:prompt:)` INSIDE the search tab's `NavigationStack` (placement B — the morph comes from `Tab(role: .search)`, not from where `.searchable` sits, so B works and lets the prompt read `scope`). The system field morphs from the tab bar at the bottom, carrying the native clear (✕) and Cancel; scope wheel + Doable filter stay the top controls, results in the middle. **Placeholder** is a per-scope `String` fed to `prompt:` — "Search" / "Search routines" / "Search exercises" / "Search equipment" (Kit scope searches equipment, so the single-item word, per the kit-vs-equipment vocabulary law). **No auto-focus:** the native default activates search only on a field tap (Apple: "search is only activated and deactivated by the user"), so simply NOT setting `.tabViewSearchActivation(.searchTabSelection)` and NOT arming focus on entry gives Dave's ask for free; a `@FocusState` + `.searchFocused` remains only for the deliberate "type a name first" refocus on the empty-query Kit create. The custom **Done** key and `onDone`/`previousTab` return-to-previous-tab plumbing are retired — leaving the surface is now a normal tab tap (native search has no tab-return concept). `SearchFieldBody` stays: the pushed catalogs/pickers/sheets still use it via `HeaderSearchField`. **Accepted tradeoff / ⚠️ device-pass:** this re-arms the documented iOS 26 morph bug — an `.onGeometryChange` anywhere in the TabView subtree (here, `TodayView`'s onboarding step-height probe, a sibling tab) can make the field fall back to the top `.navigationBarDrawer` placement on the FIRST activation instead of morphing (nav-diag 4e); it was the whole reason the custom path existed. Because this surface HIDES the nav bar, that fallback has nowhere to render — the failure mode is NO visible field on first entry, not a top bar, so it's the #1 device check (swift-reviewer). Retest on the shipping OS (may be fixed on 26.1+); if it recurs, the fix is to kill the morph trigger at its source (rework that probe), not to revert. Smoke flows updated: the field is now `app.searchFields.firstMatch` (no settable identifier on the native field), and the Done-key tap became a Routines-tab tap. App-target only; entirely device-only for real behavior (the morph, keyboard timing, native clear/Cancel are all CI-invisible). See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — Scope wheel finalized through an HTML prototype: left-aligned intrinsic band, in-band tappable direction chevrons, native-picker look, and accessibility** — Dave iterated the `InlineWheelPicker` on-device, then in a shared interactive HTML prototype (a scratch artifact with live sliders for inset / padding / gap / tilt + a chevron toggle) that let him dial the exact geometry without a build round-trip; the numbers he landed became the control's constants. The evolution from the first cut (build 124, centred band + blue-less white/grey): (1) **the band went CENTRED → LEFT-aligned**, its leading edge on the 16 pt content column so it lines up with the field and result rows above/below (Dave: match the buttons' left edge). (2) **The band is sized INTRINSICALLY** to the widest option label + even padding + reserved chevron/gap space — measured with a hidden width-probe `PreferenceKey` (a ZStack of all labels at semibold; the ZStack sizes to the widest) — not a fraction of the track. First cut re-measured cells that already carried a fixed width, so the band grew every render (a runaway-width bug caught in the prototype); the probe measures NATURAL width once. (3) **Faint chevrons INSIDE the band, on either side as needed:** with the band at the left edge nothing peeks left, so the left chevron is the "more that way" cue the last-option case needs; the right shows when later options exist. Tapping a chevron STEPS that way (each is a `Button` with a hit zone in the reserved gap, never over the label); they fade while the wheel is in motion. (4) **The 3D tilt softened** (rotation 32°→18°, flatter perspective) and made symmetric; Reduce Motion flattens it. **Accessibility (Dave: "make sure it's accessible", via the Axiom accessibility skill/auditor):** the segmented-control model — each option is a labelled `Button` carrying `.isSelected` (VoiceOver "Exercises, selected, button"; Voice Control by name; the 44 pt option row is the target; NOT one adjustable element, which would hide the per-option identifiers the smoke test needs and read worse for Voice Control); decorative SF icons + the supplementary chevrons are `.accessibilityHidden`; and crucially VoiceOver's reveal-scroll is prevented from mutating the selection — the scroll→selection sync is gated on `!accessibilityVoiceOverEnabled`, so navigating options doesn't change the scope, only a tap/drag does, and under VoiceOver the highlight pins to the real `selectedIndex`. Two-way `centeredID`↔`selectedIndex` sync: a tap/chevron sets `selectedIndex` (1-step animates the scroll, multi-step jumps to avoid intermediate-cell flash); a drag settle feeds back (non-VoiceOver). Semantic `.subheadline` fonts + the intrinsic band keep Dynamic Type intact. Smoke-test hooks unchanged (`scrollIdentifier` on the ScrollView, per-cell `findScope-*` identifiers). App-target only; the band fit/feel, chevron taps, 3D, and the VoiceOver/Dynamic-Type behaviour are all device-only (CI can't exercise them). See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md` (scope wheel).

**2026-07-24 — Filter chips grow their hit target vertically only, so a leading chip sits flush at the row edge** — Dave's screenshot: the Exercises tab's first filter chip ("Favorites") wasn't left-aligned with the ++ key / "New exercise" row / list rows above and below it, and the gap after it was wider than the gap between the other chips. Root cause: `SelectableChip` expanded its 36 pt visual chip into a 44 pt hit target with a symmetric `.padding(4)`, which also inset the border 4 pt HORIZONTALLY — so a `SelectableChip` leading a filter row landed at 20 pt (row padding 16 + 4) while its flush siblings (`FacetChip`/`TrayFilterChip`, which grow via `.frame(height: 44)`) landed at 16, and its 4 pt trailing inset widened the gap to the next chip. Fix: `SelectableChip` (and the two custom `equipmentChip`/`addEquipmentChip` in the exercise editor, same pattern) now grow VERTICALLY ONLY via `.frame(height: 44)` — the documented filter-chip idiom — so the border is flush and inter-chip gaps are purely the row's `HStack` spacing. Three horizontal filter rows led with such a chip and are corrected at once: Exercises tab (`LibraryView`), the exercise picker (`ExercisePickerView`), and Find-or-create's Doable row (`FindOrCreateView`). To keep the seven wrapping `FlowLayout` chip GRIDS visually identical (they relied on the chip's 4 pt horizontal inset for an even 16/16 gap), `FlowLayout` gained separable `horizontalSpacing`/`verticalSpacing` (the `init(spacing:)` still sets both); those grids now pass `horizontalSpacing: 16, verticalSpacing: 8` (the 4 pt vertical hit-target inset top+bottom makes the row-gap back up to 16), so visible spacing is unchanged. App-target only; layout is device-only to eyeball but the misalignment is deterministic. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — The custom `SegmentedTabs` control is retired; each site moves to the fitting native/purpose-built control** — Dave: "Our custom segmented control kinda sucks. Transition feels very sluggish, it sometimes looks crowded, and even sometimes overflows the viewport, requiring scrolling horizontally to see it. Let's kill it." `SegmentedTabs` (the matched-geometry sliding pill on `Theme.Anim.selection`, 0.25 s snappy — read as sluggish; equal-width mode crammed multi-word labels; the `widthsByContent` mode's unclamped horizontal `ScrollView` could slide its whole track off-screen) is DELETED, its 6 call sites replaced per-context. The rule: short single-word toggles → native `Picker(.segmented)`; multi-word modes that would truncate → a pushed `NavigationSelectRow`; the signature scope surface → a new inline wheel. Site by site: (1) **FindOrCreate scope** (All · Routines · Exercises · Kit) → **`InlineWheelPicker`** (new, `Views/Components/`), Dave's spec: an always-visible horizontal wheel with the selected scope CENTERED, its neighbours peeking at the edges, swipe-or-tap-a-peek to change, stopping at the ends. Built on native scroll mechanics (`ScrollView(.horizontal)` + `.scrollTargetBehavior(.viewAligned)` + `.scrollPosition(id:anchor:.center)` + symmetric `.contentMargins` that turn the leading-aligned snap into a CENTRED one), so it can never overflow the viewport the way the old track could; a `centeredID` `@State` is the single source of truth, two guarded `onChange`s keep it and the external `selectedIndex` binding in sync (an external scope change jumps WITHOUT animation, so an animated multi-cell scroll can't report intermediate cells that round-trip a wrong scope — swift-reviewer); the centred cell wears the blue `Theme.selected` pill (selection grammar), peeking cells dim via `.scrollTransition`, edges softened by a fade mask; a `scrollIdentifier` on the track lets the smoke test swipe to reach an off-centre (non-hittable) segment. (2) distance unit (m/km/mi), (4) appearance (System/Light/Dark), (5) weight units (lb/kg) → native `Picker(.segmented).labelsHidden().tint(Theme.selected)`, bound directly to the enum via `.tag` (index-Int bindings retired). (3) Schedule mode and (6) Voice cues (both multi-word) → **`NavigationSelectRow`** (new, `Views/Components/`): a bordered "configure" row showing the current value that pushes a selection list. `.pickerStyle(.navigationLink)` was NOT usable — it only renders as a push row inside a `List`/`Form`, and both hosts are plain `VStack` trays — so it's an explicit `NavigationLink` + a custom list; the hosts gained a `NavigationStack` wrap with `.toolbar(.hidden, for: .navigationBar)` so the sheet header stays the header and only the pushed screen shows a (accepted) system back bar. For Schedule the wrap lives INSIDE `ScheduleEditor` (self-contained), since main split it into an embeddable editor used by both the standalone `ScheduleTray` sheet and Today's slide-stage `ScheduleRoutineTray`, neither of which supplies a nav stack; `persistSchedule()` moved from the binding setter to `.onChange(of: scheduleMode)`. **Accepted tradeoff:** native pickers wear the iOS 26 system look, not the blue pill — the cost of "use built-in"; `.tint(Theme.selected)` nudges accent where honoured. **Also, same change (Dave's screenshot):** the shared `SheetHeader` was cramped against the sheet's drag grabber at the `.medium` detent — its top padding went 14 → 24, clearing the grabber across every tray. App-target only; the wheel feel, the two pushes + chrome, and the tray top are all device-only (CI can't exercise them). See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md` (scope wheel).

**2026-07-24 — Today view device-report round: rail-dot done marker, persisted setup milestones, a win is any workout, and "Schedule a routine" is a two-step tray** — Dave's on-device review of the Today view, six points, worked out in conversation. (1) **The done marker moved from the committed CARD back to the rail DOT.** The card's purple `checkmark.circle.fill` seal (2026-07-14) is gone; a committed `TimelineNode` now renders a FILLED purple checkmark circle instead of a stroke-only ring, and ALL rail dots grew to one uniform 18 pt diameter so the checkmark is legible and the row still reads even. This reverses two written laws — the build-33 "nodes are rings, never filled" rule and the 2026-07-14 on-card seal — Dave's call. The green→purple completion beat now animates INTO the persistent filled-checkmark state (the `converting`/`converted` flags on `TimelineItem` replaced `strokeOverride`-for-conversion + a one-shot `showCheckmark`; `strokeOverride` survives only for the carried-over lane's amber ring). (2) **Completed onboarding steps persist.** `setupActive` (`sessions.isEmpty`) still gates the INTERACTIVE scaffold (reveal-upward scroll + headroom), but the same three `SetupRow`s now also render once history exists, pinned to the very bottom of the timeline (after the committed sessions) as permanent "origin" milestones — done steps as committed-style cards, any unfinished step still actionable so setup stays reachable. Previously the whole scaffold vanished at the first logged workout. (3+4) **A day is a "win" when a workout was completed today AND no scheduled work is still outstanding — scheduled or ad-hoc alike.** ONE rule read by two surfaces. The Today top card: the rest-day/nothing-scheduled PLACEHOLDER is suppressed on a won day (`completedAnyToday && !promptsWorkout`), so a completed day shows just the committed card + the week-ahead (no placeholder, and no "Done for today" affirmation card — Dave: not needed); the "Rest day"/"Nothing scheduled" titles are unchanged for days that AREN'T won. The tab icon (`TodayStatus`): after the outstanding-scheduled-work check (which keeps the day `.toDo` even when an unscheduled workout was done), any finished session today returns `.done` — so an ad-hoc run flips the icon to the checkmark too, matching the card. Both surfaces already had "no outstanding work" guaranteed at the point the win is decided (the placeholder's render gate; the icon's early `.toDo` return), so "unscheduled-done-but-scheduled-remaining" correctly keeps the day open. (5) **The rest-day card's schedule offer became a two-step tray.** The old long, truncating "schedule a routine · it appears here on its day" `QuietKey` (which jumped straight to `RoutineSettingsScreen` for the first routine) is now just **"Schedule a routine"**, opening a sheet that LISTS the routines (each showing its current `schedule.shortLabel`) and, on picking one, SLIDES horizontally to the scheduler for it (the `SwapInSheet`/`GitHubSyncTray` in-tray slide idiom, `Theme.Anim.selection`, a back chevron returning to the list). `ScheduleTray`'s editor body was extracted into a reusable `ScheduleEditor` (all the mode/day/frequency/persist logic + the net-no-op entry-stamp restore); `ScheduleTray` stays a thin `SheetHeader` + `ScheduleEditor` + detents wrapper for its two existing callers, and the new `ScheduleRoutineTray` (in `TodayView`) is pick → schedule. App-target only; `TodayStatusTests` updated (two rest/clear cases backdated so a same-day ad-hoc session no longer reads as today's win, + three new win/outstanding cases). ⚠️ ALL of it is CI-invisible feel/layout — needs Dave's on-device pass: the bigger filled-checkmark dots, the persisted milestones at the bottom, the suppressed placeholder on a completed day, the two-step tray + its slide, and the ad-hoc → tab-checkmark. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — "Swap for…" is a suggestions tray, not a push into the whole catalog** — Dave: "'Swap for' should be a tray with suggested replacements based on their similarity to the original exercise, not a NavigationStack push into the whole exercise catalog." Both swap surfaces now lead with a shared `ExerciseSwapTray` (`Views/Components/`) that lists ranked similar moves; the full catalog is one tap further, behind a "Browse all exercises" `QuietKey`. **Similarity** is a pure Kit primitive (`ExerciseSimilarity`, Linux-tested): a fixed weighted sum of the three comparable signals the model carries — muscle group 0.6 (dominant, and the only muscle signal), movement family (`ExerciseModality`) 0.25, equipment-name Jaccard 0.15 — scoring 0…1, with a stable `ranked` that keeps ties in incoming (alphabetical) order. The app wrapper `ExerciseFilterState.swapSuggestions(for:in:kit:)` bounds the pool to the SAME muscle group (reads as "another <muscle> move like this one"; blunt for compounds, clean for isolation) and partitions kit-doable-first, each half similarity-ranked — but unlike the neighbouring `kitDoableAlternatives` it never gear-HIDES: not-in-kit moves stay, ranked last and flagged amber on the row (#113 flag-don't-hide). The tray shows the top 8 by rank; Browse all covers the rest, so the cap is never a silent truncation. **Presentation.** Planning (`ExerciseDetailSheet`): the tray STACKS on the detail sheet (no dismiss-then-present), a pick lands via `Routine.replaceExercise` (targets reset to the new exercise's defaults) + synchronous save, and "Browse all" reuses the round-2a `.swap` `PickerDestination` push exactly — through the existing `onSwap` closure, the escape hatch now the ONLY user of that push. Session (`SessionExerciseSheet`): the tray stacks on the exercise sheet; a pick commits in place with the exercise's DEFAULT `SessionExerciseConfig` (no configure gate — consistent with the planning swap's reset-to-defaults; the block's targets stay editable from its sheet), and "Browse all" presents the existing configure-before-add `ExercisePickerView`. **Both hand-offs SERIALIZE through `.sheet(onDismiss:)`** (swift-reviewer): a `browseAll…` flag is set, the tray dismisses, and only in `onDismiss` — after the tray is fully gone — does the next surface appear (the picker for the session, the detail-dismiss + nav push for planning). One teardown at a time; never two `isPresented` flips racing in a single turn (the documented drop class), and never a second sheet tearing down concurrently with the push. **Nil-origin routes straight to the full catalog** (also swift-reviewer): a swap target whose `Exercise` was deleted has no suggestions to rank, so the "Swap for…" button skips the (empty) tray and goes to the full-catalog picker/push — the pre-tray behavior, so a deleted-origin block stays swappable instead of dead-ending on a blank sheet. The rows reuse `ExerciseRowContent` (with the `modalitySymbolName` leading glyph) so a suggestion reads like a catalog row. Kit 467 green on Linux (+5 similarity tests); +2 app `swapSuggestions` tests. ⚠️ Device-only: the tray→push handoff timing (round 2a flagged this field-unproven), the session tray→browse-all onDismiss present, suggestion quality across muscle groups, and the stacked-sheet depth (overview → exercise sheet → tray).

**2026-07-24 — Find or create: the search field takes over the bottom tab bar** — Dave, revisiting a design element that got dropped in an earlier miscommunication: in Find or create, replace the four bottom tabs + the search circle with the open search field + a close key, at the bottom. Implemented CUSTOM (not the native `Tab(role:.search)` + `.searchable` morph): `FindOrCreateView` hides the tab bar (`.toolbar(.hidden, for: .tabBar)`) and anchors the field + a text Done key in a bottom bar (`.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom)`) — thumb-reachable, keyboard-aware (rides above the keyboard on focus, settles onto the tab-bar line when `.scrollDismissesKeyboard(.immediately)` drops it). The field LEFT the top: scope segmented control + Doable filter are the top controls now, results fill the middle, field at the bottom directly under the results. The custom approach is deliberate — going native would (1) restyle the field to system Liquid Glass, (2) push scopes toward the native `.searchScopes` segmented bar (losing the custom content-width control), and (3) hit the documented iOS 26 morph bug where any `.onGeometryChange` in the TabView subtree breaks the search-tab morph (and `TodayView`, a sibling tab, uses `.onGeometryChange`). Hiding the tab bar + a custom inset sidesteps all three. The close control is a text **Done** key, not a ✕ (the app reserves ✕ for collapse-search, and native bottom-search uses a text Cancel); `findOrCreateField`/`findOrCreateDone` identifiers unchanged, so the smoke flows still resolve the field by id wherever it sits. App-target only; ENTIRELY device-only for real behavior (tab bar hide, keyboard-ride, the bottom-field feel are all CI-invisible) — needs Dave's on-device pass. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — The Today tab bar icon is dynamic: dashed-open when work waits, filled-checkmark when today's done, plain-open when the day held nothing** — Dave's ask: make the Today icon depend on whether there's anything to do today (the three onboarding steps, or scheduled workouts). Three reads, mapped to SF Symbols: `.toDo` → `circle.dashed` (onboarding steps unfinished, OR a routine due today / a carried-over miss with content), `.done` → `checkmark.circle.fill` (every setup step finished, OR today's scheduled occurrence completed / banked early), `.clear` → `circle` (a rest day — nothing scheduled today, no setup pending). Implemented as a pure app-side `TodayStatus.current(routines:sessions:equipmentDone:today:calendar:)` (new `PlusPlus/Views/TodayStatus.swift`), following the `WeekPlan` precedent of computing over `[Routine]`/`[WorkoutSession]` rather than duplicating logic — it mirrors `TodayView`'s due math EXACTLY (same `RoutineSchedule.dueState`, same `scheduleAnchor` as `addedOn`, same identity-then-name `recentCompletions` pairing) so the icon and the timeline can never disagree. Classification mirrors the timeline's lanes: a `.due` occurrence is `.toDo` whether the routine is startable (dueRoutines) or empty (dueButEmptyRoutines, a repair card); a `.missed` day is `.toDo` only with content (matching `missedEntries`' non-empty filter); weekday membership + a completion-today (or a `.notDue` after early banking) reads the day as satisfied; frequency schedules float (no calendar day) and are satisfied via a completion today. **Why it lives at the root, not in TodayView:** the tab icon must stay live even when Today isn't the front tab, and a `TabView`'s non-selected tab bodies don't re-render eagerly, so a status TodayView pushes up would go stale — `RootTabView` therefore holds its own `@Query` (routines + finished sessions) + `@AppStorage(equipmentDone)` and computes the icon itself. Day rollover re-derives via a `dayToken` bumped on `.NSCalendarDayChanged` (the same guard TodayView carries against a resident app rendering yesterday's plan). Onboarding subtlety: all-setup-done falls THROUGH to the scheduled-work check, so a routine that became due during setup reads as the next thing to do (`.toDo`) rather than a premature checkmark; setup-done with no due work is `.done` (the setup itself was today's task). App-target only (TodayStatus + RootTabView + `TodayStatusTests`); Kit untouched. ⚠️ Device pass: the icon can't be exercised on Linux (app target is CI-only) and tab-symbol swaps aren't asserted by the smoke suite — confirm on device that the three states render and that midnight rollover flips a completed day from checkmark toward the next day's state.

**2026-07-24 — The standalone routine catalog is retired into a Find-or-create deep link** — Dave, from a device pass: an onboarding catalog surface "reachable only via the onboarding step" that should be a deep link into Find or create. A reachability map settled which surface: the ROUTINE catalog (`RoutineCatalogScreen`) is the onboarding-only, fully-redundant one — the Routines tab's Add already deep-links to Find-or-create (Routines scope), and the only remaining `RoutineCatalogScreen` entry point was onboarding step 2. (The *equipment* catalog, despite the "equipment catalog" phrasing, is NOT orphaned — the drawer's "Edit your kit…" and the in-picker equipment filter both use it, and its `setupMode` is a better guided "pick your equipment" step than the Find-or-create Kit scope — so it stays.) Change: onboarding step 2's action goes from pushing `RoutineCatalogDestination` onto Today's stack to `FindOrCreateLaunch.open(.routines)`. The end state is identical — a template/blank add lands on the Routines tab via `RoutineArrival` either way (RootTabView switches to `.routines` on arrival), so only the intermediate surface changes. `RoutineCatalogScreen` + `RoutineCatalogDestination` (and their nested `GearFit`/`TimeBand`/`CatalogSort`) are deleted; `RoutineTemplateDetailScreen` + `GearCheckTray` — which lived in the same file but are used by Find-or-create's own template-detail push — were EXTRACTED into `RoutineTemplateDetailScreen.swift` (they are load-bearing, not catalog-specific). Today's now-dead `RoutineTemplate` nav destination was removed (verified: after the catalog's deletion, nothing pushes a template onto `todayPath` — the only pushers were the deleted catalog and Find-or-create's own stack). The onboarding smoke flow was rewired from the catalog's search field to `findOrCreateField`. Audit of other candidates: `ExercisePickerView` (in-routine/in-workout selection) and the Exercises tab's inline create (that tab IS the full catalog) are distinct, not redundant — kept. App-target only; the onboarding step's deep-link flow is device-only to validate (CI's `ui-test` is skipped on branch builds). See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — Find or create: scope becomes a content-width segmented control, and a persisted "Doable" filter hides what the kit can't do** — Dave's device pass on the universal-search surface, worked out over a long design conversation. Two changes. (1) **Scope is a segmented control, above the field.** The four scope chips (All · Routines · Exercises · Kit) became `SegmentedTabs` — generalized with a `widthsByContent` mode (each segment sizes to its content + padding instead of equal `maxWidth: .infinity`; the HIG-legal non-uniform layout, "usually" uniform ≠ always) so "Exercises" gets its room and the short ones stay tight, the track hugging the group and scrolling horizontally only if it overflows at accessibility sizes; optional per-segment `symbols` (icons on the three typed scopes, "All" text-only — no natural glyph, and content widths mean no gap beside it); the sliding blue pill resizes as it moves. Scope moved ABOVE the field because it's a MODE (it reshapes the create verb and what an empty query browses), not just a result filter, and the field now sits directly above its own results. NOT the native `Tab(role:.search)` bottom-morph: the app deliberately owns its selection grammar (`RootTabView` comment: "No .searchable"), the native `.searchScopes` scope bar is system-styled (conflicts with the custom control), and there's a documented iOS 26 bug where any `.onGeometryChange` in the TabView subtree breaks the search-tab morph — and `TodayView` (a sibling tab) uses `.onGeometryChange`, so the morph is a latent double-affordance bug for us. (2) **A "Doable" filter** (`@AppStorage`, default ON) hides routines/exercises the active kit can't do, in All/Routines/Exercises scopes (Kit lists equipment, never filtered). It's a PERSISTED preference, not ephemeral like the query (the catalogs persist their availability facet too), and it's a single chip beside the scope — the persistent two-way control, so the trip back to filtered is the same tap, always reachable (a bottom reveal footer, considered and rejected, buried the return trip under a long list). Doability reuses the canonical helpers (`ExerciseFilterState.missingEquipment` / `Routine.gearAvailability` / a template's `equipmentNames` ⊆ kit). ONE exception keeps it honest: an EXACT-name match always surfaces regardless of the filter — search intent, AND the guard that stops the create-collision suppression (2026-07-24 above) from stranding a hidden row. Off reveals everything with the rows' own amber "needs X" tags (per-item and specific — the useful signal, vs a meaningless aggregate count of everything you can't do). Empty results never dead-end: when the filter alone empties them, the state reads "Nothing here your kit can do" + a "Show all" `QuietKey` (the Clear-filters grammar) rather than "Nothing matches." **Copy:** "Doable" beat "Can do now"/"Fits your kit"/"Equipped"/"In reach" — plainest, names the item-set (not the user), equipment-agnostic (a bodyweight push-up is doable, not "equipped"; principle 8 + anti-shame), and it doesn't collide with the adjacent "Kit" scope segment the way a kit-named label would. App-target only; engine logic is pure and unit-tested (5 new `FindOrCreateEngineTests`); the segmented-control fit + the flipped layout need Dave's on-device pass (CI-invisible). Related but SEPARATE: Dave also called for an app-wide voice change — user's-stuff LABELS use "my" (reversing the 2026-07-20 "your kit"-only possessive), prose keeps "you/your" (Apple's label-vs-prose convention) — tracked as its own follow-up PR, not in this change. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md`.

**2026-07-24 — Find or create suppresses the create affordance when the query EXACTLY names an existing item (no self-collision)** — Dave, from a Kit-scope screenshot: typing "Dumbbells" (an existing catalog equipment, 30 exercises) still offered "+ Add "Dumbbells" as equipment" directly above the identical "Dumbbells" row. Offering to create a thing that already exists under that exact name invites a duplicate (or reads as new when it isn't). Fix: a pure `FindOrCreateEngine.collisions(query:...)` returns one `Collisions` flag per creatable type (exercise / routine / equipment), each true when a non-deleted item — or, for routine, a catalog template — matches the trimmed query case-insensitively; an empty query never collides. `FindOrCreateView` drops the matching create per scope (Kit → equipment, Exercises → exercise, Routines → routine, and the All-scope chooser removes just the colliding item, hiding the whole row only if all three collide). This can never dead-end the surface: an exact-name match always ranks into the results, so the "create/add row is always present when results are empty" law (app-surfaces.md) is untouched — a collision means results are non-empty by construction. Only EXACT names collide; a partial match ("Circus Dumbbell" under a "Dumbbell" query) still offers create. `createEquipmentFromQuery`'s existing lowercased-name dedupe stays as defense. Engine-level, unit-tested (6 new `FindOrCreateEngineTests`); app-target only, CI-verified. See `.claude/rules/app-surfaces.md` (universal-search create-row law).

**2026-07-23 — Sheet/tray scroll indicators sit flush at the edge: pad a ScrollView's CONTENT, never the ScrollView itself** — Dave, from a screenshot of the New-exercise sheet: "The scroll bar on this sheet is more inset than I'd expect. Why isn't it all the way at the right edge?" Cause: the shared sheet idiom `VStack { SheetHeader; ScrollView { content } }.padding(.horizontal, 18)` applied the horizontal inset to the OUTER container, so the ScrollView's frame — and the vertical scroll indicator that rides it — sat 18 pt in from the sheet edge. The scroll indicator tracks the ScrollView's bounds, so padding the whole ScrollView drags the bar inward. Fix (mechanical, one class of 13 sites across 9 files — `ExerciseEditorView`, `ShareImportSheet`, `RoutineCatalogScreen`'s `GearCheckTray`, `LibraryView`'s `GearPickSheet`, `ExercisePickerView`'s two filter sheets, `EquipmentCatalogScreen`'s `EquipmentTypeFilterSheet`, `ScheduleTray`, `CatalogDetailViews`' `AddToRoutineSheet`, `EquipmentLibraryTray`, `EquipmentResolveSheet`'s two sheets, `GitHubSyncTray`, `RevealSurface`'s `WhatsNewTray`, `RoutineDetailView`'s `RoutineSettingsScreen`): drop the outer `.padding(.horizontal, N)` and put N on each visible sibling (`SheetHeader`, explanatory `Text`, bottom action button) AND the ScrollView's content, so the ScrollView spans full width and its indicator lands at the true trailing edge. Same visual margins for content; the bar moves out to the edge. `SheetHeader` deliberately carries no horizontal padding of its own — it inherits from the parent — so the padding lives at the call site, matching the per-context inset (18 for sheets, 16 for the pushed `RoutineSettingsScreen`).
