## Appendix: TestFlight build genealogy

> Moved verbatim from CLAUDE.md's Current State on 2026-07-10; kept for
> archaeology. CLAUDE.md tracks only the current build and safety warnings.

**TestFlight:** `.github/workflows/testflight.yml` (manual dispatch, any ref) archives unsigned, cloud-signs at export with an Admin-role ASC API key, and uploads; build number = run number — ⚠️ which means the NEXT build number is the workflow's next RUN number, not last-build+1 (runs 38–40 were consumed by past attempts; a mislabeled What's-New entry was caught at the build-42 dispatch — check `actions_list` for the latest run number BEFORE writing the entry, and expect a cancel/relabel/redispatch cycle if you guess). **Build 46** is current — build 45 + PR #297 (heart rate: live on-screen bpm during workouts [phone header capsule + watch run view], avg/max on the Complete screen / committed cards / session records, cardio zone/bpm targets in the planning sheet, the three-screen welcome flow) + PR #304 (flexible metrics — see the decision: the 14-metric curated vocabulary, per-exercise MetricProfile with seed-table resolution, equipment-suggested configs + EXERCISE CONFIG chips, the execution driver [reps > distance > calories > duration, target decides], interval blocks via per-block rest overrides + ROUND kickers, direction-aware diffs [−0:05 /500m in green], Running/Walking/Cycling, additive interchange/watch fields; rebased over #297 with the HR band hoisted to show on distance-driven intervals too). Build 45 was — build 44 + PR #293 (Dave's build-44 walkthrough, #291: the routine catalog rides the NavigationPath — `RoutineCatalogDestination` marker pushed from the Routines `+` and Today's setup step 2, registered at both stack roots, double-tap guards on the new appends; the ++ key on every root header via `AppMenuKey`; the build-33 regression smoke test now asserts back-pop depth). The ui-test proof run flaked once at the documented step-3 assertion (discriminator passed; green on re-run). ## 2026-07-23 — Articulation + physics round (build-117 device feedback)

**Decision:** joints may only bend as a reasonably flexible athletic human's do, and the mass model must satisfy real mechanics — both enforced by law, not per-move care. New invariants: `hangingHandsFoldOverTheBar` (a hanging hand bears load inside the wrap's palm-to-finger arc, thumb inward — the build-117 backwards wrap is unrepresentable), `hingesKeepTheirAxis` (swing-twist decomposition: elbows/knees/toes cannot bend off their hinge plane beyond the fleet's device-validated envelope; elbow roll = varus/valgus capped ±3°), `standingMovesBalanceTheirMomentum` (the zero-moment point — CoM projection shifted by the cart-table acceleration term, dt 0.25 s spanning bake knots, pad 0.075 for the conservative sole-corner polygon and un-authored preparatory sway — stays inside the contact-derived support polygon; ballistic moves belong to the ballistic law end-to-end), `nothingOutrunsGravity` (no grounded CoM accelerates down faster than free fall), `hangingMovesHangUnderTheBar` (pendulum equilibrium: strict ≤5 cm at still phases, ≤10 cm mid-rep — a gripped bar carries real torque), plus synthetic sway/2-g-drop provers on the ballistic-harness pattern. Wrist deviation tightened to a human ±40° (table + both arm solvers at ±38; squat 42→39, goblet 43→39 with the cup re-pocketed at wrist (46, 20, 39), 2.0 mm). **Kettlebell swing re-authored:** reps start/end STANDING with the bell hanging (analytic wrist (13.7, 24.0, 12.1), 0.0° off the handle), the float is a one-beat weightless turnaround with a hip-extension counterlean, gravity owns both descents, and the rest beat breathes at the stand — the old cycle held and rested at the float, a levitation. **Pull-Up is OUT of the catalog pending re-author:** its new hang/top configs pass every law (scratch-descent winners, one Euler sheet), but the path between them needs a better-conditioned station pin — the roll-bisection whips between solutions mid-pull (17 mm palm drift, spline teleports). Lessons banked: Euler-sheet path compatibility (legal endpoints in different sheets lerp through garbage), legs-solve-the-pendulum (grip solved by the arm, CoM by hips/knees — orthogonal), ZMP estimators must span bake knots, and a law's calibration needs a synthetic prover the moment tolerance grows.

**Reason:** Dave's build-117 device pass: pull-up fingers bent backwards around the bar with crossed elbows ("the mascot must only be able to bend its joints the way a reasonably flexible and athletic person would — figure out how to enforce that"), plus "fix the kettlebell swing and physics" with the per-part mass/CoM + momentum-laws prescription.

## 2026-07-23 — Run splits chart: bars anchor at the domain floor (the "vertical green lines" walk record)

**Decision:** `RunRecordSection.splitsChart` keeps its clamped y-domain (pace `min×0.9 … max×1.05`, so split differences read at all) but the marks moved from `BarMark(x:y:)` to the `yStart:`/`yEnd:` form anchored at the domain floor, and the plot gained `.chartPlotStyle { $0.clipped() }` so no mark in this chart can escape the frame again.

**Reason:** Dave's device report (2026-07-23): a 3.65 mi walk's record showed each pace bar as a full-screen vertical green line striping over the splits table and everything below. Root cause: `BarMark(x:y:)` draws from a ZERO baseline, and Swift Charts projects marks through the y-scale WITHOUT clipping them to the plot area — with the domain floor at ~13:40 /mi, each bar's body extended roughly four plot-heights below the 120 pt chart. The chart only mounts with ≥2 splits, so shorter validation sessions never drew a bar and CI can't render Charts at all; the first long outdoor session on device surfaced it.

## 2026-07-23 — Today-view design review round 2b: headings off the rail, "=" retired everywhere, workouts not sessions, and the schedule anchor (Dave-decided item by item)

**Decision:** Dave decided all eight findings from the Today-view review (screenshot + code audit, review artifact): (1) the CARRIED OVER explainer caption is DELETED, not fixed — it truncated mid-sentence on device ("do them whe…"); (2) the week tally says **workouts**, never "sessions" (#144: performed things are workouts; the type name had leaked), both sites — Today header + finish recap; (3) **the rail's all-caps headings die** (TODAY · CARRIED OVER · BEYOND THIS WEEK): the timeline's state grammar (dashed grey "not yet" / solid green + Start "now" / amber + "was wed" "carried" / purple seal "done") carries the structure alone. The date line ("thu · jul 23") STAYS — it's today's label, the tense system's pivot, and the opening scroll's landing; the cadence lines stay as the unlabeled far end. This also dissolves the review's IA finding — BEYOND THIS WEEK read as a section header for the next-7-days cards below it, which are not beyond this week; (4) **"=" renders NOWHERE** — not the pending card's all-unchanged collapse, not "n =" tails, not the recap tally's per-line "=", not the live set card's "last 130 · =" (supersedes the #246-era keep-it-legible call: `RoutineDiff.summary` no longer emits unchanged segments and `Segment.Kind.unchanged` is deleted; the recap tally lists only movers and hides entirely when nothing moved; a zero live delta shows just "last 130"); (5) the committed card's net-gain chip and the start tray's "today" pill move from stroked capsules to the soft r6 `CardTagCapsule` (accent-tinted) — the two data tags the 2026-07-20 shape sweep missed; (6) `BlockBar` a11y: bars beside captions are hidden from VoiceOver (the caption states the fact; the bar announced a bare "2 of 4"), the captionless live set bar gains a "Sets" label; (7) rail fact captions (dates, estimates, cadences, the pending meta + diff line) reflow to 2 lines at accessibility sizes (`factLineLimit`) — #164's reflow-don't-cap extended from headings to the rail; names stay 1-line; (8) the future card's vertical padding aligns with the missed card at 10 pt (same anatomy, same rhythm).

**Same PR — the Friday-upcoming bug (Dave's device report): a schedule never carries or banks a day it wasn't around for.** Scheduling a routine for Friday on Thursday hid tomorrow's occurrence: the reconstruction read a completion from earlier that week as a "genuine extra" (nothing outstanding on its day) and banked Friday — because the schedule has no memory of when it was set, #354's `createdAt` anchor covered only ADDING the routine. Fix, two halves: (a) Kit `occurrenceSatisfiedEarly` gains the guard that a completion **on or before the anchor day never banks** a future occurrence (it predates the plan it would be "extra" to; on-the-anchor-day included, since day granularity can't order a same-day workout vs schedule edit — the safe error is showing the occurrence); (b) the app grows `Routine.scheduleChangedAt` (optional additive column, lightweight migration, nil = anchor at `createdAt`; stamped in the `schedule` SETTER on real value changes only, so every write path — tray, Operator, undo, interchange import — funnels through one stamp and a sync pull writing the same value back can't move the anchor; EXCLUDED from the interchange like `uuid`) and passes `scheduleAnchor = max(createdAt, scheduleChangedAt)` as the Kit's `addedOn` everywhere (TodayView due/upcoming, finish-screen next-up, widget snapshot). Side effect, intended and worth naming plainly: the anchor is also the CARRY floor, so ANY real schedule edit clears currently-carried days — including one the old schedule legitimately owned (mon/thu carrying Monday, retime thu→sat, the carried Monday goes). A schedule edit is a re-plan; the new plan starts now. The alternative (splitting carry and banking anchors) would resurrect phantom carries the new schedule never owned, and telling those apart needs schedule history the model doesn't keep. Known trades, documented: banking by a genuine extra done the SAME day the schedule was configured is lost (day granularity); a repo restore stamps schedules fresh, suppressing pre-restore carried days once. Follow-up hardening (swift-reviewer, same round): every Operator undo restores the stamp alongside the schedule (all three snapshots — settings, structure, delete-recreate — else undoing a delete re-anchored to the undo moment and lost the carried Monday its comment promised to keep), and the ScheduleTray restores the entry stamp when an editing session nets no change (live per-interaction writes made an exploratory mode-peek a stamping round trip); plus the recap tally's per-row deload color joined netText's neutral grey (anti-shame drift, pre-existing). Kit 462 green on Linux (+3 regression tests: pre-anchor completions never bank, same-day never banks, post-anchor banking unchanged).

**Reason:** Dave's notes on the review artifact, one call per finding, plus his device repro of the Friday miss. The heading drop is the biggest visual change: the rail's redundant channels (border, node color, caption tense) had made the labels decoration — and one of them was mislabeling its section while another truncated. ⚠️ Device pass: the unlabeled rail's readability on a real timeline (especially carried cards with no section intro), the r6 accent tags beside r11 keys, caption reflow at accessibility sizes, and the Thursday-schedule-Friday-shows repro on Dave's store.

## 2026-07-27 — The exercise sheets' metric rows are headed TARGETS (and the run card's fabricated pace PR, filed not fixed)

**Decision:** the metric card on every planning/configure surface now sits under a `SheetSectionLabel("TARGETS")` — `ExerciseDetailSheet` (routine planning), `ExerciseConfigSheet` (configure-before-adding mid-workout), and `SessionOverviewSheet`'s `targetEditor`. All three already put ACTUALS directly below the same card (RECENT on the first, SETS on the third) in the same mono, so the two blocks read as one kind of thing; the header names which side of the target/actual pair the numbers are. With the header carrying the prescription sense, the `Target HR` row becomes plain **`Heart rate`**, matching the bare metric names beside it (Distance · Duration · Pace · Reps · Weight). Sets and Rest stay inside the section deliberately: the block's structural prescription is a target too ("3 sets × 8 @ 185 with 90 sec rest" is one sentence). `SheetSectionLabel` also gained `.isHeader`, so a VoiceOver user gets by navigation what a sighted user gets by layout — the rows themselves announce bare metric names, so without it the section is the one channel assistive tech never hears (#164). Copy-reviewer cleared both strings; TARGETS beat Dave's word "goals" because a goal reads long-term while a target is this routine's prescription, and the app already ships `Target HR`, `RepTarget`, and the "target · live actual" framing on the HUD.

**Reason:** Dave's device report on a Run block: "they kind of read as like, 'here's what happened, manually entered' instead of goals against which actual metrics will compare." An unlabeled card of mono numbers directly above a labeled card of mono numbers is genuinely ambiguous, and cardio makes it worst — a run's Distance/Duration/Pace look exactly like a result.

**Same report, diagnosed and NOT fixed — filed as #453:** today's run card showed a green `−3:42 /mi`. That is `RoutineDiff` working as specified and the specification being wrong for cardio. The diff compares the routine's staged target against the last ACTUAL performance (`TodayView.prior(for:)`), and #246 justified that with "a plan sitting below it is the normal morning-after state when the user out-lifted the plan" — an assumption that holds for weight (actuals drift up past a floor) and inverts for pace, because nothing ever carries an actual pace back into a routine target. So the plan's 12:00 /mi against a 15:42 /mi run renders the *gap* as a personal best, in celebration green, before the workout starts, every day until the target is met. It is the #246 anti-shame rule's mirror image: instead of shaming a regression it manufactures an achievement. Pace also outranks distance in `diffPriority` and has zero coverage in `RoutineDiffTests`. Left for Dave because the fix is a fork, not a bug patch: diff target-vs-target (correct model, changes the whole diff), drop the never-carried-forward metrics from the pending card (contained), or render them neutral. #453 also carries two smaller findings from the same screenshot — `estimateText`'s 5-minute bucketing rounds a single-block 12:00 run DOWN to "~10 min" (the card contradicting the only number in the routine), and a `Rest 60 sec` override on a `Sets 1` block that can never fire.

⚠️ Device pass: the header's fit above the card at large Dynamic Type on all three sheets, and that TARGETS/RECENT now reads as a pair on a cardio block.

## 2026-07-27 — RoutineDiff learns the whole prescription: sets, rep ranges, and a directionless `changedFields`

**Decision:** `RoutineDiff.Target` now describes the whole prescription rather than a subset of it, and the diff gained a second question it can answer. Three parts. (1) **Sets join the model.** `Target.sets` and `Prior.sets` are new, and `Delta.sets(Int)` renders as "+1 set" / "+2 sets". This closes a silent hole: `Target` carried weight, reps, duration and extras and had no set count at all, so a routine going 3×8 to 4×8 evaluated `.unchanged` and Today said nothing about an added round. `diffPriority` retyped from `[WorkoutMetric]` to `[Field]` and seats sets **LAST**, against the instinct that an added round is the bigger change. The two sides' set counts are not the same kind of number — the target's is PLANNED, the prior's is COMPLETED — so finishing 2 of 3 last time manufactures a "+1 set" out of a shortfall, and in a first-match-wins list that artifact outranks and SILENCES whatever genuinely moved (swift-reviewer, reproduced: a routine edited 3×8 to 3×10 reported the phantom round and hid the added reps). Placed last it can only speak when nothing real did. On the target side the count is the block's `group.sets` (superset members each run once per round, so every entry carries the group's count); on the prior side it is the highest set NUMBER reached (not the count of matching logs: an exercise can appear in two blocks, and counting logs compared one block's target against every block's total and went silent), and only when the prior session is the SAME routine — a set count belongs to a prescription, so an A/B split running bench for 4 rounds one day and 2 the next must not report a standing "+2 sets" for merely alternating. (2) **`Target.repsUpper`** makes a rep RANGE representable. An actual is always one count, so it never diffs alone; it widens `reps` into a band a prior actual can sit inside, with the upper clamped up to the lower — `RepTarget` drops an inverted range but the stored column does not, and interchange import assigns it straight from a hand-edited file, so an unclamped "10–8" reported an identical rep count as changed. (3) **`changedFields(target:prior:) -> Set<Field>`** is new and answers "what is not the same" where `delta` answers "what progressed": every differing field, in either direction, including the neutral-direction machine settings (resistance, incline, speed, cadence, RPE) that `delta` deliberately ignores — a changed setting is still a changed prescription. Reps there use the band test (8–10 asked for and 9 done is not a change; the same 9 against 10–12 is). A nil prior returns empty, since nothing is comparable against a performance that never happened. Supporting shape: a `Field` enum (`.sets` or `.metric(WorkoutMetric)`) and one `value(for:)` accessor per side, so neither function restates where a value is stored.

**Deliberately absent, and documented on `Field` so it is not "fixed" later:** the block's REST and the HEART-RATE TARGET. Both can change on a routine, but a `SetLog` snapshots them as targets in force at start time (`restSecondsOverride`, `targetHeartRateData`) and never records an actual for either, so there is nothing to diff them against — they belong to a target-versus-target comparison this type does not model. Also out of scope by construction: STRUCTURAL change (an exercise added, removed, reordered, or grouped into a superset), because this is a per-exercise value diff. An added exercise already surfaces as `.new`.

**Reason:** Dave, reviewing the Today-card ledger design: "Make Routine.DiffTarget know about changes to the number of sets, and anything else about a routine's exercises that could change." The ledger shows target beside previous actual and marks the token that moved, which needs every changed field rather than the single highest-priority one `delta` returns — hence `changedFields` rather than a wider `Delta`. `delta` still returns one value, so no caller's SHAPE changed — but its output does move on real data, because the same change feeds both callers a set count: the Today summary line and the finish recap tally can now say "+1 set" where they previously said nothing. Only the net chip (`netWeightGain`) is genuinely untouched.

⚠️ **Known consequence, same class as #453:** sets now joins the fields where a static target compared against a shortfall renders as a gain. Habitually finishing 2 of 3 sets will show "+1 set" in accent green on Today, every day, exactly as an unmet weight target already shows "+5 lb". This is not a new bug class — it is the existing one covering one more field — and #453 owns the fix (diff target-vs-target, or drop never-carried-forward fields from the pending card, or render them neutral). Noted on #453 rather than gated here, because gating would have meant shipping a `Target` that still could not see sets.

Kit **482** green on Linux (+15 regression tests, was 467), CLI 26 green. swift-reviewer found the suppression bug, the multi-block miscount, the cross-routine set comparison, and the inverted-band case; all four are fixed above and pinned by tests, including one that pins assistance's position ahead of reps (a mutation of that ordering passed the whole suite before).

**Known dead surface, deliberately:** `changedFields`, `Field`, `value(for:)` and `repsUpper`'s band role have no production caller yet — the Today ledger is their first, and it will be the thing that proves the semantics on a real screen rather than only in unit tests.

## 2026-07-27 — Today's pending card is a LEDGER: target beside prev, no delta printed

**Decision:** the pending card's one-line diff summary is replaced by a two-column table. Columns are `target` and `prev`; rows are the exercises that MOVED on a multi-exercise routine, and the METRICS that moved on a one-exercise routine (the card title already names the only exercise, so the rows key on what actually varies). Each cell carries the whole prescription — `3×10 @ 30 lb` beside `3×8 @ 25 lb` — with the `target` column dimming the tokens that held and lighting the one that moved, and `prev` flat throughout at a single brightness. **No delta is printed anywhere.** Movers only, capped at four rows, then a `QuietKey` reading "3 more changes" reveals the rest and collapses again. Nothing in the table is green: `target` moved is `textPrimary`, `target` held is `textSecondary`, all of `prev` is `textFaint`.

**Reason:** Dave's build-126 device report. The card collapsed a whole routine into one signed number, and on a run that number read as a *value* rather than a difference — "I read −3:42/mi as an impossible negative target pace". It was really the gap between a static 12:00 /mi pace target and a 15:42 /mi run, painted in progress green before the workout started (#453). Two columns hand the arithmetic to the reader, which makes a signed number unrepresentable rather than merely corrected — the misread cannot recur because the shape that produced it is gone.

**How the emphasis works without substring math (the load-bearing piece).** Lighting a token inside a finished string means computing character ranges and keeping them correct through unit changes, rep ranges and a negative assist load. New Kit type `PrescriptionRun` (text + an optional `RoutineDiff.Field`) makes it a lookup instead: `Prescription.blockRuns` renders a `Target`/`Prior` into tagged runs, `changedFields` returns `.metric(.weight)`, and the run carrying that field is the one drawn bright. Both columns build through the same function, so an unmoved prescription renders identically on each side and the token that differs is the only thing that moves — a test pins that property. Separators ("×", " @ ") carry no field and are never emphasized.

**Assistance renders as a NEGATIVE load** (`−25 lb`), in whichever unit the app is set to. It is stored as a positive number that helps, with an improvement direction of `.down`, which makes it the odd one out among loads; shown signed it rejoins weight on a single axis where a bigger number is always the harder session, and the progression from assisted to weighted becomes one continuous line (−35, −25, 0, +10). Scoped to the ledger and NOT to `WorkoutMetric.displayText` (Dave's call, weighed against 27 call sites): that backs the metric wheels and ± steppers, whose stored range is 0...500 and whose increment would then appear to move toward zero. Accepted cost: the ledger says "−25 lb" where the exercise sheet still says "25 Assist". The same renderer fixes a latent bug on the way past — the previous formatter appended a load by reading the WEIGHT column, but an assisted profile stores its load in the extras bag, so an assisted exercise would have rendered a bare "3×6" with nothing after it.

**Two laws bent on purpose, both noted in the code so they are not "fixed" back.** (1) The row label **WRAPS rather than truncating**, departing from the rail's "names stay single-line" rule: here the name is the row's KEY, saying which of five exercises the two numbers beside it belong to, so a clipped name makes the row unreadable rather than merely abbreviated (`.firstTextBaseline` row alignment keeps a two-line name's numbers beside its first line). (2) **An all-unchanged card SAYS "same as last time"** rather than rendering nothing, reversing the 2026-07-23 "unchanged emits nothing" call for this surface only. That call killed "=" and "n =" because they were noise *beside real movement*; an empty ledger is a different problem, since the region simply vanishes and a card showing name, meta and Start reads as "no information" rather than "you are repeating a session on purpose". Scope is the view alone — `RoutineDiff.summary` still returns empty for all-unchanged, so Kit is untouched and the finish recap tally keeps its behaviour. Never-performed keeps `first time · sets the baseline` in accent.

**The ledger sits OUTSIDE the card's `NavigationLink`,** a sibling of Start, because its expander is a tap target: nested inside the navigating region it would race the push, which is this card's documented silent-dead-tap class. Expansion state is `@State expandedLedgers: Set<UUID>` keyed by `Routine.uuid`, never `persistentModelID`, like every other routine-keyed state here. At `dynamicTypeSize.isAccessibilitySize` the grid reflows to stacked rows rather than truncating (#164's reflow-don't-cap, applied to a table), and each row announces as one sentence with spoken separators, since the raw cell reads badly aloud.

Deleted as dead on the way: `TodayView.targetText` (it populated `DiffLine.target`, which nothing read), `DiffLine`, `diffLines`, `diffSummaryText`, `color(for:)`. `RoutineDiff.summary` stays — the committed card's net chip uses it. Copy-reviewer changed "3 more diffs" to "3 more changes": the working path speaks plain training English and the git layer is a second reading the app never leans on.

Kit 500 green on Linux (+18 `PrescriptionTests`), CLI 26 green.

⚠️ **Device pass, and this one is mostly invisible to CI.** The likeliest thing to be wrong is column fit: two value columns plus a wrapping name on a 393 pt phone. Also wanted — the expander at four-plus movers, the accessibility-size reflow, VoiceOver reading a row, a superset routine (every member carries the group's set count), and the Run card that started this.

## 2026-07-27 — The ledger's row test is `movedFields`, not `changedFields` (swift-reviewer round)

**Decision:** a new Kit `RoutineDiff.movedFields(target:prior:profile:)` decides which rows a target-beside-actual display may draw, and it is deliberately narrower than `changedFields` on two counts. **Both sides must carry a value** — a missing prior is absence of evidence, not movement. **Only the profile's own fields participate** — a load column stranded by a profile change cannot produce a row. Alongside it: Today's `Prior.sets` now reports the rounds the block was PLANNED for (`sortedSetLogs`), not the rounds finished; never-performed exercises get a row with an em-dash `prev` instead of being skipped; `Prescription.text` renders reps as the bare target on BOTH paths; the ledger's per-row accessibility label rides the label CELL; empty cells render "—"; and `ForEach` ids carry position when a uuid is absent.

**Reason:** swift-reviewer on the ledger commit, which named the root cause exactly: the ledger decides *whether a row exists* from a directionless "what is not the same" answer, while `prior(for:)` deliberately feeds it nils and shortfalls that the old `delta` consumer was structurally immune to (`delta` required both sides non-nil). Four defects fell out of that one composition, all reproduced before fixing:

- **A cross-routine exercise drew a spurious, misaligned row.** Bench Press in both "Push A" and "Full Body": staging Full Body with byte-identical targets set `prior.sets` to nil (the same-routine gate), which `changedFields` reports as changed. The row rendered `3×8 @ 135 lb` against `8 @ 135 lb` — different token counts, the `3` lit as the thing that moved, nothing having moved. Same for any prior from an ad-hoc session. **Fixed by the both-sides rule.**
- **"Same as last time" was printed over a never-performed exercise.** Squat and Bench unchanged plus a Face Pull added yesterday: the new entry was skipped for having no prior, `isFirstTime` was false because the others had one, and the card asserted nothing had changed — over the one thing on it worth knowing. **Fixed by giving a new exercise its own row.**
- **A short session repainted the next card as movers.** Stopping one set short on five exercises made all five movers with the set count lit, purely from last week's shortfall. `RoutineDiff` already documents this artifact and demotes `.sets` to LAST in `diffPriority` for it, but a row test has no priority list to hide behind. **Fixed by comparing planned rounds to planned rounds** — the two sides of a set comparison have to be the same kind of number.
- **A rep RANGE collapsed to its lower bound on one path only.** 3×8–10 read "8–10" in a two-exercise routine and "8 reps" in a one-exercise one, from the same data. **Fixed by routing both paths through `Prescription.text`**, which also kills the "reps · 8 reps" stutter.

Also from the same review: a modifier on a `GridRow` applies to each CELL rather than the row (Apple documents this), so the per-row accessibility label would have made every row announce itself three times — it now rides the label cell with the value cells hidden, working with that semantic rather than against it. `.minimumScaleFactor` covers the sizes between xLarge and the accessibility reflow, where a truncated number in a comparison is a WRONG number.

**Process note, and it is the real lesson:** every one of these lived in the composition between two individually-correct pieces, and all of them were unreachable from any test target because the policy sat in private methods on a `View`. Lifting the row test into Kit made them Linux-testable in the suite that already pins the column-alignment property. Kit 505 green (+5 `movedFields` regressions, +1 reps-parity), CLI 26 green.

---

## 2026-07-28 — The row entrance flash is a leading GUTTER MARK on the row background, not an inset ring overlay

**Decision (Dave, from a design study).** `RowEntranceFlash` — the "here is the thing you just made" beat on the Routines / Exercises / Kit catalogs — stops drawing a rounded accent ring around the row and instead paints a 3 pt accent capsule in the row's LEADING MARGIN, 5 pt in from the screen edge, growing from its centre, holding, then fading. It moves from `.overlay` to `.listRowBackground`. Wash-plus-mark and a wash alone were both prototyped and rejected; the mark alone won.

**Reason.** The ring was `RoutineCard`'s choreography at row scale, and it stopped working the moment the catalogs went cardless (2026-07-25). Three faults, one cause:

- A rounded stroke traces a boundary **no other row in the list has**. Nothing else there is drawn *around* content — the separators and the 16 pt content column are the list's only structure — so the ring read as a state badge on a selected row rather than a pointer at a new one.
- `.padding(.horizontal, -6)` was the tell: an **overlay guessing at bounds it cannot see**. A row background gets the row's true full-bleed bounds for free, which is exactly what the negative padding was approximating.
- `.padding(.vertical, 2)` put the stroke ~2 pt off the text on a one-line row, so it read as a squeeze. The margin the mark now occupies is the one strip of a row that never holds content, so a bare "anytime" routine and a three-pill one get the identical mark with no per-surface constant.

Two things came free and are worth keeping in mind before anyone moves it back: a row background sits **under** the swipe actions, so a flash caught mid-swipe can no longer fight `swipeAdd`/`swipeDelete`; and the mark is diff-gutter grammar, which this app already speaks (green is data and creation, purple is merged).

**Reduce Motion** is still NOT a gate — the flash answers "which row landed", the same call the card ring made. Only the vertical bloom drops there (`@Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion)` seeds `bloom = 1`); the mark still appears and fades.

**One timing fact, and it is a trap.** The flash lives in a view that only exists while `newlyAdded == id`, so **clearing that identity unmounts the mark mid-fade**. The old ring finished at ~1.54 s from the add and the routines arm held for 1.96 s, so the mismatch never showed; the new beat runs 2.08 s from mount and would have been cut off. `RowEntranceFlash.totalDuration` now publishes the figure and both arms of `CatalogScopeView`'s arrival task sleep on it instead of on hand-tuned literals. The four beat constants are seconds, feeding both the sleeps and the curves, so a retune cannot leave an animation longer than the sleep waiting on it.

**Validation.** Invisible to CI — the smoke flows assert the row exists, not what colour its ground went. Device pass required.

---

## 2026-07-29 — Routine detail, header redesign settled as a prototype — Design decided before implementation, in a reviewable artifact rather than a branch of Swift

**Decision.** Dave's seven-point brief for routine detail was worked as an interactive prototype over five rounds, then BUILT in the same PR. ⚠️ **The prototype was NOT committed** (Dave: "you shouldn't be committing your prototypes") — it was scratch, and this entry is the record of what it settled.

**The settled header.** The estimate leads a 100 pt column on the LEFT (`~30 min` over `20 sets`, with the routine's muscle groups beneath it as inert data tags). A three-row spec table takes the right: `SCHEDULE`, `PAUSES`, `KIT`, mono caps label left, hairline under each row, value hard right, each row a door.

**Rest and transition merged into one `PAUSES` row**, each number carrying its own noun on its own line (`45s between sets` / `15s between exercises`) and one sheet behind both. Two reasons beyond tidiness: `TRANSITION` was the longest label in the block and was setting the left edge for every value, so merging made the column read even; and three rows plus a left column took the worst-case label-to-value gap from 267 pt to 121 and the first exercise row from 215 pt to 161.

**That gap is the thing the round was really about** (Dave: "too much horizontal eye movement between the label and the value"). Eight arrangements were built and measured. The finding: **travel is set by where the label starts, not by how wide the table is** — narrowing to 62% only reached 111, while a left column reached 121 with three rows and no narrowing. An earlier round claimed right-shifting was the answer at 61 pt; that measured a variant whose value column was already inset 60 pt, so the claim was wrong and is retracted here.

**Kit names are data tags, not a `·`-separated run.** The middle dot floated at mid-height with nothing to sit on and read as debris. Tags remove the separator rather than replacing it: the boundary IS the separator, the amber gets a shape to live in instead of being a lone coloured word, and a kit of five does not become a chain of dots. ⚠️ The tags are **soft ground plus a ring** — `CardTagCapsule`'s fill with a 1 pt `borderStrong` stroke at r6 — because they are DOORS and the muscle groups beside them are not. That is the surface law's "shape carries what taps" applied at half strength: full control shape (r11 + stroke) read as two buttons in a data row, and bare soft fill made them indistinguishable from the inert muscle tags. ⚠️ Consequence: the kit row carries **no trailing chevron**, since a row-level door plus per-tag doors would be nested tap targets, so the three rows do not end identically.

**Also settled:** the whole header scrolls and the name hands off to the centre of the top bar; exercise names wrap to two lines; each row prints Today's `target` beside `prev` with no delta (`DiffLedger`'s grammar, one run per line so a 58 pt column fits beside the rail); those column headers pin above the list; the `EQUIPMENT` section label is gone; notes leave this region entirely; and Start plays the welcome key's ignition.

**Only `target` and `prev` pin** (Dave, asked and answered at the end of the round). The opening brief asked for the metadata to stick when it hits the top with the exercises scrolling behind it; the settled design holds only the column headers, and the estimate and table scroll away with the title. Nothing in this header is consulted mid-list, so pinning it would spend list height on facts already read. The brief's sticky-metadata ask is therefore **retired, not deferred**.

**Three implementation constraints, all found while prototyping.** (1) `RailLayout` assumes one `railRowHeight` and the drag, ring and drop targets all read it, so a wrapping name needs either a raised uniform height (cheap, `RailArrangement` untouched) or per-row heights (honest, much larger). The two-column ledger helps: every row is a two-line block regardless of name length. (2) `RailGlyph` takes `dotY: rowHeight / 2`, correct only while a row is one line — it becomes the first line's centre, both halves `@ScaledMetric`, and the superset loop's quarter-curves follow for free. (3) SwiftUI cannot put a padded tag inside a wrapping `Text` run (already documented for `KitTag`), which is why an inline-token treatment in the header would have to be links through a URL scheme.

**Two knock-ons outside this screen.** `prev` already exists as `ledger(for:)` in `TodayView` and wants moving beside `RoutineCardContent` so both surfaces read one producer. And lifting the ignition out of `IntroView` into `StartFlashButton` changes **Today's Start too**, since both call it.

**⚠️ A latent copy fault found while writing this round's sheet copy, and it is SHIPPED.** `EquipmentLibrary.activeKitPhrase` returns `"the \(name) kit"`. That construction assumes the kit's name is ADJECTIVE-SHAPED, which is true of exactly one name: `main`, the default. It reads fine for "the main kit" and "the travel kit" and breaks for anything else a user might type ("the Dave's stuff kit", "the Home Gym kit"). The helper was validated against the one name that flatters it.

It is invisible today for the same reason the `KitTag` fault was: `activeNamePhrase` only returns a NAME once more than one real kit exists, so with a single kit every caller reads "your kit" and the construction never renders. One shipped call site: `RoutineTemplateDetailScreen`'s onboarding blurb ("Marking what you have in …").

The fix is the law's own preferred path rather than a new phrase: restructure so the sentence ENDS on the name and let `KitNamePhrase`/`KitTag` mark it, which is what that law says to do wherever the tag can go. `activeKitPhrase` then wants narrowing or retiring, because its premise — that "the <name> kit" reads — is the thing that turned out to be false. NOT fixed in this PR: it is app code, it changes a shipped screen's copy, and the rewording is a call to make rather than assume.

**Carry-over to other surfaces.** Assessed at the end of the round; four things generalize and one is a straight conflict.

⚠️ **The one conflict: tappable amber's shape — and it is smaller than it looks.** `RoutineEquipmentTags.tag` (`Views/Components/RoutineCardContent.swift`) implements design-review 2026-07-23 law #3: a tappable amber piece wears the CONTROL shape (r11 + stroke + trailing chevron) while inert card tags stay soft r6. That size delta is precisely what Dave opened this round objecting to ("imbalanced and accidental"). The settled answer keeps the law's INTENT (shape carries what taps) and moves the delta from radius-plus-chevron to **stroke only**, at r6, so both tags are the same size.

**The audit's useful finding is the call-site count: `interactive: true` renders at exactly ONE place**, `RoutineDetailView`'s header. The other two `RoutineEquipmentTags` call sites (Today's pending card, `RoutineCardContent`) are inert and unaffected. So "amend the law app-wide" and "scope it to the routine header" are the SAME edit in practice — there is no second surface to migrate, and no risk of two amber grammars coexisting. What needs doing is the LAW TEXT in app-surfaces.md, which would otherwise keep pointing future work at a treatment nothing renders. That is the whole cost of the conflict.

**Should carry — same problem, same fix.** (1) **A `·` between NAMES is out; a `·` between FACTS stays.** Scope it that precisely or someone strips every dot in the app, since the fact-line joins (`RoutineCardContent`, `HistoryView`, `TodayView`'s committed subtitle, `ShareImportSheet`, `SessionOverviewSheet`) are all legitimate. The name-joining sites that share this round's fault: `RoutineTemplateDetailScreen` (muscle group names), `CatalogDetailViews` (metric labels), `EquipmentResolveSheet` (equipment names). (2) **The scroll + name hand-off** belongs to the whole pushed-detail family, not this screen: `RoutineTemplateDetailScreen` and both `CatalogDetailViews` screens already share the "clear the chrome title, lead the body with a large wrapping header" pattern, so if only routine detail's name flies into the bar the family splits. (3) **Rest and transition merging** has to reach `RoutineSettingsScreen`, which still carries them as two sections (`BETWEEN SETS`, `BETWEEN EXERCISES`); one tap apart, the same two settings would otherwise have two different mental models. (4) The two already noted above: `ledger(for:)` moving beside `RoutineCardContent`, and the ignition reaching Today's Start.

**A divergence to name rather than fix.** `TodayView` holds an explicit law: fact captions reflow to two lines but **names stay single-line**, because "a truncated name is recoverable one tap away, a truncated date isn't". Routine detail now wraps exercise names to two lines. That is defensible — in the rail the name IS the row's content, not a label on a card you are about to open — but it should be written down as a scoped exception, not left to look like drift.

**Deliberately NOT carried.** The estimate-left split and the three-row table are shaped by this routine's particular facts; exercise and equipment detail carry different ones. The spec-table anatomy is a reusable component candidate, but only on the repo's own terms: extract it when a SECOND surface adopts it, not before.

**Validation.** Built and CI-green (app, Kit and CLI). ⚠️ **Nothing here is device-verified, and the riskiest part is invisible to CI**: `ui-test` is skipped on branch builds, and the rail's drag, ring and drop targets all read `railRowHeight`, which this change moves 52 → 76. The nav-bar swap and the sticky column band are equally device-only. Poke the rail first.

**Two faults found while building, both worth remembering.** `PrescriptionRun`s are FRAGMENTS of one line, not lines — the first cut rendered one per line, which breaks `3×10` into three rows; they compose into a concatenated `Text` with ink per fragment, as `DiffLedger` already did. And the ledger index was first read per ROW, which walks session history once per exercise on every render; it is read once in `railList` and threaded down.

---

## 2026-07-29 — Routine detail, device pass: five layout faults, four of them one bug each

**Decision.** Dave shipped build 156 to his phone, looked at routine detail, and named five things. Every one is a layout fault the simulator-free branch build could not have shown, and each has a single root cause. They are recorded together because the SHAPE of the mistakes repeats: in four of the five, something was given the space and the other thing got the remainder.

**1. The spec table forced ~90 pt of dead air between each label and its value** (Dave: "seems we are forcing too much space between the table labels and values"). The label carried `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)`, which drove every value to the far right edge — reintroducing, inside the table, the exact horizontal eye travel the left-column arrangement exists to remove. The label is a fixed COLUMN now (`labelWidth`, sized for `SCHEDULE`, `@ScaledMetric`) with the value beside it and the slack at the trailing edge where the chevron is.

**2. Same greedy label, second symptom: the values were compressed to whatever was left**, so `45s between sets` ellipsized to `45s between…` and the kit tags stacked one per line. ⚠️ The nouns and the tag names ARE this table's disambiguation — the merged `PAUSES` row is only legible because each number says what it is — so they are the one thing that row cannot afford to truncate.

**3. A second, independent way to stack those tags, worth knowing before writing another row like this.** `FlowLayout.sizeThatFits` returns `proposal.width ?? 0`, so an `HStack` reads it as infinitely flexible. A `Spacer` is too, and a stack splits its remainder EVENLY between two equally flexible children — so pairing the two halves the tags' room whatever the label does. The value takes `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` and there is no trailing spacer.

**4. The rail nodes did not sit on the first line of the exercise names.** The row was a plain `HStack`, which centres vertically: the glyph kept its top edge at the full 76 pt row height while the name floated to the middle of whatever height IT had. The node was drawn at a fixed y from the glyph's top and the name was not, so the two only agreed by accident — and disagreed by a different amount for a one-line name than a two-line one. The row is `.top`-aligned, the text carries its own `textTopInset`, and the text pieces share a `firstTextBaseline` so the ledger's first line lands on the name's.

⚠️ **Two follow-ons from that one.** The offset moved OUT of `ExerciseRailRow` and onto the screen: `supersetLandingFX` and `landingParams` place the landing band and spark at `railRowHeight / 2`, the old mid-row assumption, so moving the node inside the row had silently left the animation anchored where the node used to be. One number, one owner. And the node's y is read from `UIFont.preferredFont(forTextStyle: .body).lineHeight` rather than stored as a scaled constant — a `@ScaledMetric` scales a number guessed at one size, and 2 pt out is visible on a 10 pt dot. It is a pure read, never a write during layout, so it stays clear of the morph law.

**5. The ledger cells broke wherever the glyphs ran out.** `3×8 @` / `10 lb` stranded the separator at the end of a line, and the two columns broke at different points on the same row (`3×9–14` / `@ 35 lb` beside `3×10 @` / `35 lb`), so the pair could not be read across — which is the entire job of printing them side by side. The break is CHOSEN now: the fragments split at the load separator, which the line break replaces. Both columns break at the same place on every row. The one shape with nothing to split at is a cardio block (`5× 1000 m`); it scales a hair rather than wrapping into a shape nothing else has.

**One thing found while fixing, not reported.** The `target` column heading sat 5 pt right of the column it names — the header row spaced its labels at 8 while the row spaces its run columns at 13. On right-aligned mono columns that is plainly visible. ⚠️ A heading and the thing it heads have to share a spacing constant or they drift the moment either is tuned.

**Validation.** Parse-checked and CI-green. Device-only, again: all five are layout, `ui-test` is skipped on branch builds, and the rail's drag/ring/drop targets read the same geometry the node offset now travels with.

**Addendum, same day — the two header columns get a drawn gutter.** Dave, looking at the fixed layout: "think we need some way of making sure the two columns of that top area are clear". A 1 pt `Theme.border` rule now sits in the gutter, spanning the taller column.

It is the SAME hairline the spec table already draws between its rows, turned on its side, so the three horizontal rules start on a vertical one and the right column reads as a table rather than as text that happens to be right of other text. No new weight, colour or idea enters the screen.

⚠️ **The alternative — a filled ground behind one column — costs more than it gives here, and the reason generalizes.** A panel in a cardless screen reads as a card you can open, and neither column is one. And the left column's muscle tags already wear `surfaceRaised` over `background`: put them on `surface` and the tag flattens into its own ground and stops looking like a tag. **A soft data tag needs the page's ground under it; giving its container a fill spends the contrast the tag is made of.**

Same round: the pause NOUN wraps instead of truncating. It is what tells the two pauses apart under one label, so at an accessibility text size where it stops fitting on one line, a second line is the answer and "between exercis…" is not — the same rule as the fault above, applied where the fault would return.

**Addendum 2, same day — the Pauses tray shipped with no header padding.** Dave's second device shot: the title flush against the sheet's left edge and "Done" running off the right.

⚠️ **`SheetHeader` carries no horizontal padding of its own and never has** — every tray supplies it, at 18, because the scrolling content beneath has to be full-bleed so rows clip at the sheet's edges rather than at a padded inset. `PausesTray` was written new in the redesign round and omitted it; its content also sat at 16 where the family uses 18. Both are 18 now.

Worth naming as a class rather than a one-off: **a shared header that expects its caller to pad it will eventually meet a caller that doesn't**, and nothing catches it — it compiles, it renders, and only a device shot shows the title touching the glass. A sweep of every `SheetHeader` call site found this to be the only one, so the component keeps its contract; if a second appears, the padding belongs inside `SheetHeader` and the full-bleed trays take a flag instead.

---

## 2026-07-30 — Routine detail, round 2: honest prev, direction ink, content-sized rail rows — Picked by Dave from an options artifact, built and merged in one round

**Decision.** Dave asked for a second opinion on the rebuilt routine detail screen ("I just redesigned this with Opus 5 … I want to see how you'd improve it"). Eight questions went to him as an interactive artifact — radio groups with recommendations, write-ins, and a copy-back button — and his picks came back as one paste. Everything below shipped together. The survey pattern worked well enough to note: design options as a form beat design options as prose, and the copy-back text was the whole spec.

**1. The prev-source bug report: root-caused, then UPHELD as designed — the source does not change.** Dave, the day before: "Prev appears to be showing what the targets were the last time for each exercise, not the actual." The mechanism is real and worth recording. The pipeline reads actual FIELDS all along (the set screen writes real actuals, the watch syncs real actuals); three summarization rules collapse them back into the plan. (a) The set count is the PLANNED rounds — 2026-07-29's deliberate diff-stability choice. (b) Reps come from ONE set — the top by weight, and ⚠️ **Swift's `max(by:)` resolves an all-equal tie to the FIRST element** (verified against the toolchain with the exact comparator), so every bodyweight or constant-load block reports set 1, the set most likely to hit the prescription. (c) Completion prefills actual := target, so the one set consulted almost always echoes the plan. 10 · 9 · 8 push-ups against a 3×10 plan print "3×10".

An honest-summary rework was built (completed sets, top-set ties→last, performed rep bands "8–10" through a `Prior.repsUpper`) and then REVERTED in the same round on Dave's explicit call: **"don't change the source of the values for the prev column."** The trade he picked, knowing the mechanism: the plan-stable summary keeps a short week from repainting Today's movers, and that quiet is worth more than per-set fidelity in a 58 pt cell. Two durable residues: `RoutineDiff.Prior.sets`'s Kit doc — which promised "sets actually COMPLETED" while the LEDGER producer never passed that — now documents what each producer actually does and why (the DOC was the thing that lied; ⚠️ scoped to the ledger on swift-reviewer's catch, because the finish tally legitimately DOES pass completed sets — both of its sides are actuals, so completed-vs-completed is its correct comparison and must not be "fixed" to planned), and `RoutineLedger.prior` carries the full mechanism inline so the same observation doesn't get re-litigated. ⚠️ The direction inks below therefore compare against this SAME plan-stable prior the prev column prints — the marks describe the two numbers actually sitting beside each other, whatever those numbers' provenance.

**2. Target tokens wear DIRECTION: increases green, decreases a gentle brick.** Dave's write-in for the steady-state-echo question, with the load-bearing parenthetical "(decrease is not a problem)". `RoutineDiff.movedDirections` returns up/down per moved field on each metric's own progress axis (`improves`): a faster pace and a lighter assistance stack are both `.up`, and both PRINT that way (pace falls; assistance renders signed). Neutral settings (resistance, incline…) stay directionless and keep plain bright. Both ledger surfaces (`ExerciseRailRow`, `DiffLedger`) share the rule by hand-kept twin `ink` functions. ⚠️ **The anti-shame law is scoped, not repealed**: it governs how a PERFORMANCE renders; these inks mark how the PLAN moved against it, always beside the printed prev, never a bare signed number. `DiffLedger`'s "nothing here is green" doc is superseded — the #453 misread was a signed number in progress green; a colored token beside its comparand cannot re-create it.

**3. Two new palette entries, and a finding.** `BrandPalette.increaseInk` (light 0x0E7A3D · dark 0x46D17C) and `decreaseInk` (light 0x9E4E44 · dark 0xE09084), HC variants included, ~5:1+ on their worst grounds. ⚠️ The finding: **plain `accentLight` (0x17914B) measures 3.65:1 on light `surface`** — fine as a fill or large glyph, under the AA floor as caption text, which is why `increaseInk` exists instead of reusing `accent` (the `selectedInk` story, third verse). The brick is deliberately far from `destructive` in saturation: a deload is a choice, not an alarm.

**4. Rail rows size to their CONTENT — the honest fix 2026-07-29 named, built.** `RailLayout.build(rowHeights: [[Double]])` is the primary Kit API now (uniform `groupSizes` + `RailMetrics` overloads remain, delegating); `RailDrag`/`RailRing` take the same shape, and `previewPositions` already read per-row heights internally so the hole a drag opens is the dragged row's own height. App side: heights are COMPUTED from UIFont metrics (name lines measured via `boundingRect` at the name column's actual width, ledger lines from `printedLines`, seated on the ascender difference), floored at the proven 52, with the width from ⚠️ a PURE `GeometryReader` read — never measured back from layout, which would be a state write and the morph law. Heights are content-derived, never membership-derived, so ring join/leave still moves no row (#87). The add row keeps 76. The drag preview, drop targets, ring spans, and landing FX all read the one computed layout.

**5. The rest of the picks.** PAUSES is two side-by-side noun-under-number cells (the one-line phrase wrapped mid-phrase at DEFAULT type size — the wrap-not-truncate rule firing where it was never meant to; stacking by design means accessibility sizes wrap within a cell instead of changing the row's shape). Muscle tags cap at two + "+N" (assistive tech gets the full list via label). The pinned TARGET/PREV band draws a hairline shelf — the spec table's rule, third job — so scrolled names clip against a visible edge. The empty-prev placeholder is a bare "—" everywhere ("new" retired; the placeholder glyph, not a word doing a caption's job). The rows stack's leading padding went 20 → 12 (Dave: "the left rail should be further to the left") — the glyph column is structure, so it sits nearer the edge and the name column gains the width. The TARGET/PREV headings and the run columns now share ONE `@ScaledMetric` (the 2026-07-29 drift lesson applied structurally). Declined from my own recommendations, by Dave: recency in the header (left out), hiding PREV before history exists (dash instead).

**Validation.** Kit 532 + CLI 26 green locally; app files parse-checked; CI is the app verifier. ⚠️ Device-only, and it is most of the risk: per-row heights move every rail gesture's geometry (drag, ring, drop, landing FX), and the height computation's UIFont measurement has to agree with SwiftUI's wrap decision (measured 2 pt narrow so boundary names round UP to two lines — over-measuring costs air, under-measuring clips). Poke the rail first, then the pauses row at accessibility sizes, then a routine with >2 muscle groups, and a DURATION exercise (clock glyph) whose name sits near the wrap boundary — the glyph's width is an estimate in the height computation (swift-reviewer), and an under-measure draws the name's second line over the next row.

## 2026-07-31 — The exercise editor could not put its keyboard away, and the fix is the law the rest of the app already had

**Decision.** `ExerciseEditorView`'s form gets the app's standard keyboard exits: `.scrollDismissesKeyboard(.immediately)` on its `ScrollView`, a tap on the form's ground, `.submitLabel(.done)` on the two single-line fields, and an explicit focus clear on every route that opens something over the form (the shared `addChip` push, both defaults wheels, the Cancel/discard path). Focus is a `@FocusState` over a three-case `Field` enum (name · notes · video).

**Reason.** Dave, creating a custom exercise from the picker during an ad-hoc session: "I can't seem to make the keyboard hide. The name input seems to remain focused even after I click out of it." He was right, and there was no way to be wrong about it — the sheet had **no dismissal at all**. A plain SwiftUI `ScrollView` does not dismiss the keyboard on scroll; that is why `.scrollDismissesKeyboard` is declared explicitly on five other scrolling surfaces in this app (the two catalog detail screens, `CatalogScopeView`, `SheetPickList`, `RoutineTemplateDetailScreen`, `OperatorTray`). This form — the biggest one in the app, and the one where the keyboard covers half the controls you next need — was simply missed. Tapping a chip never dismissed it either, because tapping a `Button` does not resign first responder on iOS; that part is standard platform behaviour, not a bug, but with nothing else on offer it left the keyboard up until the sheet closed.

**⚠️ The ground tap is a layer BEHIND the form, never an `.onTapGesture` on the content stack.** Both reach the same taps in the good case — an ancestor's tap gesture yields to any control the touch actually lands on, so chips toggle either way — but they fail differently, and only one failure is survivable. An ancestor gesture RACES the text field for the tap that focuses it; lose that race and the keyboard blinks shut on the way IN, which is a worse bug than the one being fixed. A background layer can only ever receive a touch nothing in front of it took: no regression is possible, and the price is paid in reach. Worst-case-acceptable beat best-case-better, on a change that cannot be validated from a Linux session.

**⚠️ And the price is real: the ground is the SECOND exit, never the first.** Sitting behind everything means everything hit-testable takes the tap first — not just captions, but section labels, each field's own filled chrome including its 14/11 pt padding ring, and the defaults card. What is actually left is the 18 pt side margins, the 24 pt bands between sections, and the header. Above the keyboard on a full sheet that is two thin strips and a couple of bands, which is why `.scrollDismissesKeyboard` is the load-bearing fix and this is the courtesy. The header needed its own copy of the layer: it is a SIBLING of the `ScrollView`, so neither of the scroll's exits reached ~90 pt of the most conspicuous blank space in the sheet, sitting directly above the field that holds the keyboard (swift-reviewer).

**Save clears focus too, and it is the exit that most needed it** (swift-reviewer, on Dave's exact path). Every other route was covered; `save()` was not, and `save()` is the one that HANDS OFF — on the picker path `onCreated` runs `onPick`, and `ExercisePickerView` answers by presenting `ExerciseConfigSheet`. A standing keyboard would have followed the editor's dismissal straight into a brand-new sheet, which is the same fault the wheels and pushes were fixed for, on the route where the next surface is a whole sheet.

**Not widened.** `RoutineDetailView`'s routine-settings screen has the same missing `.scrollDismissesKeyboard` over its name and notes fields. Same bug class, different surface, not what was reported — filed here rather than fixed in this diff.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; nothing in Kit or CLI moved. ⚠️ Device pass: the keyboard is invisible to XCUITest and taps there dispatch through accessibility, bypassing exactly this gesture layer (testing.md), so a green suite proves nothing about it. Type a name, then (1) scroll, (2) tap the SIDE MARGIN beside a chip and the blank header — the two places the ground actually reaches, so don't conclude "ground tap works" from one lucky tap, (3) press Return, (4) tap "+ Add" under MUSCLE GROUPS — the pick list must not arrive under a standing keyboard, (5) Save from the picker path and check `ExerciseConfigSheet` does not arrive under one either, (6) tap the name field itself and confirm it focuses on ONE tap, and (7) Cancel a dirty draft: the focus clear and the dialog present in ONE SwiftUI transaction, so if the discard dialog ever arrives over a standing keyboard, defer the presentation a runloop.

## 2026-07-31 — Keyboard dismissal is a shared law, not a per-form fix: `keyboardGround` promoted, routine settings joins

**Decision.** The ground layer that #489 built inside `ExerciseEditorView` moves to `PlusPlus/Views/Components/KeyboardGround.swift` as `View.keyboardGround(clearing:)`, and `RoutineSettingsScreen` (`RoutineDetailView.swift`) gets the full treatment: `@FocusState` over a two-case `Field` (name · notes), `.scrollDismissesKeyboard(.immediately)`, the ground, and a focus clear on every route that opens something over the page (the schedule tray, both metric wheels, the delete alert) or leaves it (`onBack`, and `onDisappear` for the swipe-back that never reaches `onBack`).

**Reason.** #489 noted this surface as the same defect on another screen and deliberately left it; Dave asked for it. Two fields, one of which grows to eight lines, under a keyboard covering half a pushed page, with no way to put it away — the same bug, and the second occurrence is what makes it a law rather than an incident. The component file carries the reasoning so the next form gets it right by reading one doc comment: **`.scrollDismissesKeyboard` is the load-bearing exit and is non-negotiable on any scrolling surface holding a field; the ground tap is always the SECOND exit; and it goes BEHIND the content, never as an `.onTapGesture` on the stack** (an ancestor gesture races the field's own focus tap, and losing that race blinks the keyboard shut on the way in — worse than no dismissal). It also records the two traps #489 paid for: the catchment is thinner than "the gaps" (labels, captions, field chrome and card backgrounds all take the tap first), and a header that is a SIBLING of the scroll needs its own copy.

**The name field keeps `onSubmit`.** `RoutineSettingsScreen`'s Return-commits behaviour is load-bearing — it is where #189's duplicate-name guard gets its chance to veto — so `.focused` was added beside it, not in place of it. This is the surface that makes the app-wide "Return never commits" phrasing false; the honest rule is that Return never NAVIGATES.

**Not done.** `pushedScreenChrome`'s header band has no ground of its own. Unlike `SheetHeader`, that chrome is shared by every pushed screen in the app, so giving it one is a change to all of them and wants its own round.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier. ⚠️ Device pass, same blind spot as #489 (XCUITest taps bypass the gesture layer): on routine settings, type in NAME, then scroll · tap the side margin · press Return (it must still commit the rename and surface the duplicate warning) · open the schedule tray and both pause wheels · swipe back mid-edit and confirm the rename still committed. Then, on BOTH surfaces, type 10+ lines into NOTES and drag INSIDE the field: the keyboard must stay up. `.scrollDismissesKeyboard` is an environment value and reached that field's own internal scroll — `.never` on the two notes fields is what carves them out, and it is a hazard #489 shipped unnoticed (swift-reviewer).

## 2026-08-02 — The GitHub wizard authorizes FIRST, so its remaining steps can be checked instead of trusted (#509, Q18-A)

**Decision.** `GitHubSyncTray`'s three steps are reordered to **authorize → create repo → install**, and the install step verifies itself live: with the device's token in hand, `GitHubSyncCoordinator.installedRepository()` asks GitHub which repo the Sync App is installed on, the step prints "installed on owner/repo" with a green check, and Finish is disabled until that answer arrives. It re-asks on every `scenePhase == .active` — the install opens externally on purpose, so returning to the app IS the moment the answer can have changed — plus a "Check again" key for the states where it can't. `connect()` is deleted and replaced by `authorize()` (device flow, save the token, stop) and `finishConnecting()` (bootstrap and adopt); `hasToken` names the state between them.

**Reason.** The old order made the two cheap steps honor-system and put the only real verification after the expensive one: whoever missed the install found out last, via a generic error, having already paid the full device-flow round trip. Authorizing first is what makes the rest checkable at all — a user-to-server token can list the App's installations, so "did you install it" stops being a question we ask the user and becomes one we ask GitHub. Dave picked this over the keep-the-order variant (Q18-B, poll on return) because it removes the guess rather than deferring it.

**Repo creation stays honor-system, deliberately.** The Contents-only App can only ever see repos it is installed on, so an uninstalled repo is invisible to us. The install check covers both anyway — you cannot install on a repo that doesn't exist — which is why step 3 gates Finish and step 2 still says "Done? Continue".

**Where you land is decided by the TOKEN, not by which door opened the tray.** A device holding one is past step 1 whatever brought it here (the post-install bounce, a reconnect, a wizard abandoned halfway), and one request tells it which of the remaining states it is actually in. Without a token there is exactly one thing to do.

**⚠️ Authorizing does NOT clear the fault, and that nearly shipped inverted** (swift-reviewer). The old `connect()` cleared it after `bootstrap()` + `connection = .connected`, one atomic success. The reorder moved `clearFault()` to the end of step 1 — four user actions before a connection exists — and `clearFault()` PERSISTS `connectionFaulted = false`. The commonest repair path is: token expires, tap Today's advisory, authorize, then close the tray believing you're done. That would have left `faulted` false, `connection` disconnected, and a live token in the Keychain: Today's card gone, the drawer row painted the same gray as a never-connected install, and nothing able to re-raise either, since `sync()` guards on `isConnected`. Starting the repair would have silenced every signal that the repair was needed — strictly worse than the bug above it. `finishConnecting()` clears the fault, because that is where the connection begins. A token is not a connection, and `hasToken` (now a stored, observed property rather than a computed Keychain read that `@Observable` could not see) is what names the state between them.

**Two more the same review caught in the reorder.** The in-app browser used to close off `sync.isConnected`, which the old `connect()` set at the end of the device flow; `authorize()` deliberately doesn't, so the Safari sheet would have stayed up over GitHub's "device activated" page while the wizard advanced invisibly beneath it — and `SafariView` sets no delegate, so its own Done button does nothing. `startAuthorize()` closes it now. And a 401 from the install check was reported as "Couldn't reach GitHub", which left Finish permanently disabled under a "Check again" that could only fail identically; a dead token now drops itself and seats step 1.

**A stuck fault now has an exit** (swift-reviewer). Disconnect used to live only in the connected branch, so `faulted` was a one-way door: it is cleared by a successful connect or by `disconnect()`, and while faulted the tray shows the wizard, which offered neither. Someone who deleted their repo or changed their mind was left with a red drawer row and an amber card on Today saying "reconnect", with no way to say no. `abandonKey` ("Stop syncing") appears once there is something to forget, so a first-time wizard never carries it.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; nothing in Kit or CLI moved. ⚠️ Device pass: no XCUITest covers the wizard, and the whole feature is a live network round trip. Walk it on a fresh install — authorize, then WITHOUT installing anything tap Continue to step 3 and confirm it says "Not installed yet" and Finish is dead; install; background and return; the check must fire on its own and name the repo. Then the abandoned-wizard path (authorize, close the tray, reopen it — it must land on step 3, not step 1) and "Stop syncing".

## 2026-08-02 — A broken sync says so on Today, gated on a backup that actually existed (#509, Q19-A)

**Decision.** Today's rail carries an amber advisory entry when GitHub sync has broken — "GitHub sync stopped · nothing lost · syncs when you reconnect" — tapping it presents the sync tray. It sits directly under the anytime card, sharing that row's gate, and it is shown only when `GitHubSyncCoordinator.isBackupBroken`.

**Reason.** A broken sync used to live ONLY as a red dot in the drawer — a surface you might not open for weeks while local edits piled up un-backed-up, which is the one thing a backup failing quietly must not do.

**`everSynced`, not `lastSyncedAt`, and that took two review rounds.** The first cut used the drawer row's predicate (`disconnected && faulted`), which is set by ANY failed connect attempt: a dropped wifi during the device flow, an expired code, a Cancel on github.com. Someone who tried once, failed, and never came back would have got a permanent card promising their workouts were safe in a backup that never existed. The second cut gated on `lastSyncedAt != nil` — honest evidence, destroyed at the worst possible moment: the commonest way a live sync breaks is an expired or revoked token, and that path calls `clearCoordinate()`, which deletes the last-synced key in the same breath as `setFault()` records the fault. The advisory would have shown for the rest of that process and then vanished forever at the next launch, exactly while the un-pushed edits accumulated. `GitHubSyncSettings.everSynced` is written by the first successful pass and cleared only by a deliberate `disconnect()`; the coordinator's `init` backfills it for installs that predate the flag from `lastSyncedAt` ALONE. ⚠️ A saved coordinate does NOT qualify (swift-reviewer): `bootstrap()` writes it at connect time, strictly before the first pass, so an install that connected on a flaky network and never completed a sync would have backfilled to true and then claimed "nothing lost" about a repo that never received a byte — the same false claim one layer down. `PlusPlusTests/GitHubBackupBrokenTests.swift` pins eight states; the relaunch case is red against the second cut, and `coordinateWithoutASyncDoesNotBackfill` is red against the third.

**Presentation is DIRECT, not via the drawer.** Routing the tap through `RevealController` needed a second notification receiver inside `RevealSurface` and a 360 ms sleep to clear the drawer's spring — a magic number Reduce Motion falsifies, since a reduced open finishes early — plus a cross-layer write into that view's own presentation state. One receiver on `RootTabView`, one sheet, and the user lands where the card promised. The notification is `plusplusOpenSyncTray`; do not add the second listener back.

**One `.sheet(item:)`, never two `.sheet(isPresented:)`** (swift-reviewer). A view presents one sheet, so a second request arriving while the first is up is silently dropped AND latches its flag true, after which every later request is a no-op and the post-install auto-return is dead until relaunch. That ordering is the likely repair path, not a corner: re-installing the Sync App is how a broken sync gets fixed, `openInstall()` leaves the app so the Setup URL can bounce back, and the bounce lands while the tray opened from Today is still on screen.

**Placement is structural, not incidental.** The advisory is second, never first, because first is the landing slot the opening `scrollTo` seats under the band, and taking that over on every open until someone fixes a token is what an alarm does — design-grammar's amber law says this isn't one. But "second" was only true when the anytime entry rendered, which is conditional; sharing its `!setupActive || equipmentStepDone` gate makes the invariant hold by construction. Cost: a fresh install still on setup step 1 sees nothing, which is the right trade on the surface teaching them the app.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier; the six-state predicate has unit tests. ⚠️ Device pass: revoke the PlusPlus Sync App on github.com, foreground the app, confirm the card appears on Today, RELAUNCH and confirm it is still there (the whole point of `everSynced`), then tap it and confirm the tray arrives directly with no drawer slide — with Reduce Motion ON as well as off.

## 2026-08-02 — The calendar ask moves to the first fixed-weekday schedule (#509, Q20-B)

**Decision.** The offer to put scheduled workouts in the calendar now appears inline in `ScheduleEditor`, the first time a routine gets a non-empty fixed-weekday schedule. The drawer's toggle stays as the second door, and the offer names it.

**Reason.** Health had already moved to a contextual ask at the moment of value; calendar permission still fired off a toggle three levels into the drawer. Dave closed the asymmetry (Q20-B over the leave-as-is recommendation). Fixed weekdays are the only schedule shape that becomes a calendar event — `WorkoutCalendarPlan` builds a weekly recurrence rule — so "every few days" never sees it.

**INLINE, not a sheet, and that is the whole design.** `persistSchedule()` runs on every day-chip tap, so a modal would land on the FIRST day someone picked and interrupt them picking the other two. It would also be a sheet over a sheet, since both hosts present the editor modally, and resolving it on the editor's way out would be dismiss-then-present in one transaction — the presentation-drop class `HealthStartPrimer` documents.

**Four gates, each removing a way to lie.** `SetupState.calendarPrimerShown` keeps it to once ever — the app's own memory, because `EKEventStore.authorizationStatus` answers "did iOS decide", never "did PlusPlus already offer", and the two come apart the moment someone taps Not now. `CalendarSyncCoordinator.isEnabled` hides it when the feature is already on. `canRequestAccess` hides it from anyone iOS has already answered, so the key can never do nothing when tapped. And it needs a non-empty day set, since an empty one persists as `.unscheduled`. It is off under `--uitest-reset` with the tips and entrance flashes: the calendar IS available on a simulator (unlike HealthKit, which `HealthStartGate` gets its test exemption from for free), so the offer would otherwise render inside the onboarding smoke flow one tap from an OS dialog XCUITest does not own.

**Validation.** App target, so CI is the verifier. ⚠️ Device pass: on a device that has never been asked, schedule a routine on fixed weekdays and confirm the card appears after the first chip and does not interrupt picking the rest; take it, and check the "++ Workouts" calendar gets the event; then confirm it never appears again. Separately, on a device that has DENIED calendar access in iOS Settings, confirm it does not appear at all.

## 2026-08-02 — The catalog leads with front matter, and it PREPENDS rather than replacing the list

**Decision.** A catalog TAB with nothing narrowing it (no query, no facet) renders `CatalogFrontPage` above its sections: one statement of what the scope and the active kit come to, then the scope's own axes as `SelectableChip` runs that write facets. Exercises gets muscle and movement, Kit gets type, Routines gets focus. The search tab is excluded. The whole grouped list still follows, unchanged.

**Reason.** `CatalogScopeView` is asked to do three jobs and serves two. Retrieval is solved (fuzzy search, synonyms, collisions, yours-before-catalog). Narrowing works but assumes you already speak the taxonomy — a chevron chip that opens a tray of words is no help when you do not know the words. Discovery had no surface at all, so the empty state stood in for it, and the empty state is row 1 of 347 alphabetically: on a fresh install the Exercises tab opens on **90/90 Hip Switch**. Kit is the same shape at 102 rows (#251) and Routines at 56. Meanwhile the kit is a precise declared constraint that already knows what it can do, and nothing ever said so out loud.

**The prepend-versus-replace call, which is the part worth keeping.** The design round proposed replacing the list with entry points when the query is empty. Three things in this codebase say no, and only the first is about tests. (1) `FindOrCreateEngineTests.emptyQueryShowsEverything` pins the grouped-whole-list behavior and navigation.md states it as law; prepending leaves both true. (2) **Reorder has nowhere else to live** — routines' `.onMove` is tab-only, empty-query-only, MINE-tier-only by design, because a ranked or narrowed list has no order to write back. Replacing the list on an empty query would have made drag reorder unreachable, and nothing in the proposal noticed. (3) The facet row is the list's ONE pinned header and the missing-equipment disclosure hangs off the sections; a replacement disturbs both. Prepending costs one condition and changes no structure. `kitHint` was already the precedent for empty-query-only content injected before the sections.

**What falls out of prepending, and is the better design anyway.** A chip writes a facet, the facet makes `filters.isEmpty(for:)` false, and the block gives way to the list it just asked for. So the front matter is **the facet row spelled out** — same state, same engine, entered by a door that shows you the vocabulary instead of asking you for it. Nothing new to learn and nothing to dismiss.

**Counts.** Built from a DEDICATED `FindOrCreateEngine.outcome` pass at empty query and no filters, held in `@State`, rebuilt on a key of scope · kit MEMBERSHIP (the whole `Set`, not its count — swapping one piece for another moves every doable number) · catalog sizes. Never derived in `listBody`: a per-render count over the catalog is exactly the cost the per-scope tab counts were retired over (2026-07-25).
