Building it from the ENGINE rather than a parallel pass over the `@Query` arrays is deliberate — the tier rules, the added-template dedup and the kit-doability verdict all already live there, and a second implementation of them is a second answer waiting to disagree with the list two rows below.

**Two smaller calls inside it.** ⚠️ Muscle buckets file under the PRIMARY group only, so the columns sum to the catalog rather than past it; the facet a bucket writes still matches on ANY group, which is the honest asymmetry (the count says what a muscle is FOR, the list then shows everything that touches it). ⚠️ Chips state the axis value's CATALOG total, not its kit-doable subset: a doable count reads "Carry · 0" for a kit with no loadable gear, which is a chip opening a group with a disclosure and nothing above it, and the statement already carries the kit frame. A move carrying no movement pattern is ABSENT from the movement axis rather than bucketed as a null — cardio carries none, most isolation carries none, and an "unclassified" chip would be the catalog's largest and mean nothing.

**Where the math lives.** `PlusPlusKit/Sources/PlusPlusKit/CatalogReach.swift` — pure value math over `ExerciseSimilarityFeatures`, the bag the swap ranker already builds, so there is no second reading of what an exercise requires and the whole thing is Linux-tested (`CatalogReachTests`). `ExerciseFilterState.similarityFeatures` and `CatalogFilterState.resolvedFocus` became internal to serve it, rather than gaining copies.

**Copy.** The frame is what a kit OPENS, never what it lacks: "129 of 347 exercises fit your kit", and bodyweight-only reads as its own sentence ("83 of 347 exercises need no equipment") rather than as a deficit. Nothing is called missing (the anti-shame law), and the kit is named through `EquipmentLibrary.activeNamePhrase` with `KitNamePhrase` carrying the tag when a second kit exists.

**Test note.** `SmokeTests.testExerciseFilterNarrowsAndClears` asserted `"Arm Circles"` after Clear-all to prove the full run was back. That row is now below the fold and invisible to a lazy list, so the assertion became the front matter's RETURN — which is what "unfiltered" now looks like. A second smoke test covers the chip write.

**Review round on the same PR, three fixes and one accepted limit.** ⚠️ `SelectableChip` had never been placed in a `List` row before, and it carries no `.buttonStyle` — inside a `List` that resolves to `.automatic`, and row taps route into default-styled buttons anywhere in the row (the build-12 class `catalogRow` already carries a warning about). The front matter is a ~200 pt row that is mostly NOT chips, so a tap on the statement or on the ragged end of a wrapped chip line would have written a facet nobody chose, and the block being self-dismissing meant the list would then narrow with no visible cause. `.buttonStyle(.plain)` on the chip run, NOT on the component — its two other call sites are in `ScrollView`s and want the automatic press fade. Invisible to CI either way (XCUITest dispatches through accessibility and bypasses hit testing). ⚠️ The routines statement had no empty-kit branch, so a fresh install whose kit is still empty opened the Routines tab on "0 of 56 routines fit your kit" — a subtraction against something the setup step has not asked for yet, which is exactly the read the other two scopes' fallbacks exist to prevent; it now states the catalog. ⚠️ `frontMatterKey` is `nil` off the surfaces that can show the block: it is evaluated per body pass, and the search surface has one per keystroke, so it was allocating a ~100-name `Set` per character for an instance that never renders the block.

**The accepted limit, stated rather than hidden.** The key tracks CARDINALITY, not content, so it cannot see an EDIT — a muscle changed in the exercise editor moves a bucket without moving any count in the key. Hashing the catalog's attributes into the key instead would fault every relationship on every body pass, which is the cost this whole design is arranged to avoid. The answer is a second trigger: `onAppear` rebuilds as well, and every edit route leaves through a pushed detail screen, so popping back to the root re-appears it. Bound: an edit made in a sheet presented directly over the list, with no push in between, holds a stale number until the next appear. The block is also built in `onAppear` rather than inline, so the first visit to each tab per launch renders the list and then inserts the block; both are device-pass riders.

## 2026-08-02 — The similarity ranker becomes a browse structure: NEAR THIS on exercise detail

**Decision.** Exercise detail ends with a **NEAR THIS** cross-reference block: the catalog's nearest moves, kit-doable first, each row naming why it is near, tapping through to that exercise's own detail. Six rows, then a QuietKey expands the rest in place. Rows are `CrossRefRow` in a `crossRefBlock`, the idiom the screen's EQUIPMENT and ROUTINES sections already use (Dave's call from an options round).

**Reason.** `ExerciseSimilarity` is a tested adjacency graph over the whole catalog, and it was being spent on one tray ("Swap for…"). That is a browse structure used as a single feature. Walking from a move you know to its neighbours is how people actually learn a catalog of 347 things, and it is the cheapest discovery affordance in the app because the hard part shipped in July. This is a second READER of `ExerciseFilterState.swapSuggestions(for:in:kit:)`, never a second version of it, so the move that ranks as the best substitute is also the first thing offered here.

**The one new piece of logic, and why it is a classification rather than a string.** `ExerciseSimilarity.reasons(candidate:origin:)` returns `[SimilarityReason]`, strongest first; the app picks the words. That keeps the voice laws on the app side and the logic Linux-tested, the same split the Operator's `ChangeSpec` uses.

⚠️ **The display order deliberately differs from the score's weighting.** `score` leads with muscle because muscle is the spine of substitution — but every candidate in this list already shares a muscle group with the origin (the pool is built that way), so "shares a muscle" is true of every row and therefore says nothing. What separates one row from the next is the movement pattern, then what the move is FOR, then the gear. Mechanic is absent from the vocabulary entirely: compound-vs-isolation earns its weight in the ranking and reads as jargon in a caption. ⚠️ An attribute either side lacks yields NO reason rather than a false one, the same absent-is-unavailable rule `score` renormalizes by (#495). ⚠️ `sharedMuscle` is suppressed when the primary already matched, since it is a weaker restatement of the line above it.

**The caption's second half.** A row the kit cannot do says so, per flag-don't-hide — but `CrossRefRow.meta` is a one-line mono caption sharing its row with the title, so a missing piece is NAMED only when there is exactly one ("needs barbell"); past that it counts, in the app's own countable word ("needs 2 pieces", #507). The exercise's own screen is one tap away and names everything.

**Placement.** LAST, under the routines flow and its "Add to routine…" row, because it is browsing rather than an act on this exercise — and the CreateRow belongs to the ROUTINES section it completes, so the new block could not be inserted between them.

**Known gap, recorded rather than faked.** The model carries no difficulty or progression axis, so "near" can mean sideways but never "easier than" or "next after". Push-Up to One-Arm Push-Up is a real edge the data cannot express, and the ranker cannot infer it — it would have to be an authored attribute on the catalog table. Nothing here pretends otherwise.

**Review round on the same PR: the pool is not small, and the render path is not free.** ⚠️ The first cut capped at six and let a QuietKey expand the rest IN PLACE. The pool is every catalog move sharing ANY muscle group with the origin, which is **167 for a Romanian Deadlift and 248 for a Clean and Press** — and `crossRefBlock` is a plain `VStack` in a `ScrollView`, so nothing there is lazy. That expansion would have built ~248 buttons and 247 dividers in one animated body pass, each row faulting an equipment relationship and rebuilding the kit's name `Set`. The swap tray never had this problem because it caps at 8 and its escape is a PUSH into the real, lazy catalog; this round dropped the cap that made the list safe to render. Bounded at 20 now, with the key's label stating the number it will actually add so the bound is visible rather than silent.

⚠️ **And the pipeline ran on every body pass, on a screen that is now recursively stacked.** `swapSuggestions` walks the whole catalog, reads `muscleGroups` on all 347 (a decode for any user-edited row), and traverses each pool member's equipment three times. Both existing consumers read it only inside a `.sheet` content builder, so this was the first render-path caller. At depth four, one favourite-star tap would have re-run it four times. The rows are prepared once into `@State` on a key (exercise · kit MEMBERSHIP · catalog count) with an `onAppear` rebuild, the same shape the front matter uses, and the kit set is read once per rebuild rather than once per row.

⚠️ **A `navigationDestination`'s SOURCE view is not unmounted when it pushes**, so "starts folded again" was not true on the path that now matters most: pushing a neighbour and popping back returns to the same live `@State`. The fold is re-applied in `onAppear` rather than left to a fresh instance that never arrives.

⚠️ **`.fullBody` is a catch-all, not a claim.** Every cardio move files under it, so for a cardio origin "same muscle" was true of all 122 neighbours — the exact says-nothing failure the display order exists to prevent, arriving through the bucket instead of through the pool. A shared fullBody primary now reports nothing and the pair falls through to what it actually shares: the gear, or the lack of it.

## 2026-08-02 — The Kit catalog is ordered by what a piece would open, not by the alphabet (#251)

**Decision.** With no query, the Kit scope's CATALOG tier orders by marginal unlock count (descending, name as tiebreak) and each row states it: an `opens N` tag replacing the "N exercises" one. MINE rows keep "N exercises". Counts come from a new `CatalogReachCalculator.unlocks`, which `EquipmentDetailScreen`'s existing "+N" add beat now also reads.

**Reason.** #251 filed it as "100 alphabetical rows need grouping or common-first ordering". A hand-curated common-first list is the obvious fix and the wrong one: it goes stale the moment the catalog grows, and it encodes someone's guess about a typical gym rather than a fact about YOUR kit. The app already knows the fact. Every exercise carries its requirements, so for any piece the kit does not hold, the number of exercises it ALONE stands in front of is a pure function over data that has always been there. Ordering by it turns the Kit tab from a checklist into a decision surface, and it is the one thing this app can say that a tracker without per-exercise requirements cannot.

⚠️ **Marginal, and truthful about it.** A piece that is one of TWO things a move is missing scores nothing for that move until the other arrives. The alternative — count every exercise that uses the piece — would overclaim, and a number that overclaims on the surface you use to decide what to buy is worse than no number. This is the rule the "+N" unlock beat has always used (`newlyDoableCount`), which is exactly why that method now calls the shared function instead of counting for itself: a beat saying "+7" over a row promising "opens 5" would train the distrust the count exists to avoid. A Kit test pins the invariant directly — an unlock count equals what the doable count actually gains.

⚠️ **Single pieces only, and nothing pretends otherwise.** A bench and a barbell together are worth more than the sum of their entries. Saying so would make this a solver rather than a fact, so it says nothing.

**Not a sort control.** `SortChip` stays deleted, nothing on screen offers a choice of order, and the reordering is empty-query-only — a query still ranks by score. It is one principle applied to one tier. ⚠️ And the counts arrive as a precomputed `[String: Int]` parameter rather than being computed inside the scoring pass, on the front matter's key; an EMPTY map leaves the incoming order alone, which is what preserves the presented equipment catalog's flat alphabetical run (navigation.md) without a second code path.

**Copy.** The frame is what a piece OPENS. "opens 21" states a gain; anything shaped like "you are missing 218" would break the anti-shame law and smuggle in obligation. A piece that opens nothing today shows NO tag rather than "opens 0" — a zero in a tag reads as a verdict on the piece instead of a fact about the kit.

**Review round on the same PR: the order must hold still while the tags move.** ⚠️ `unlocks` is a function of the kit, so adding ONE piece changes the count of many others — and the Kit tab's leading swipe commits membership in place. A live order would therefore re-sort the whole CATALOG tier under the thumb that just swiped, and put the next swipe on a different piece than the one it was aimed at. That is precisely the complaint that made the PRESENTED equipment catalog one flat alphabetical run (navigation.md, 2026-07-25); the tab's version of it used to be a single row moving to MINE, and ordering by unlocks would have made it the whole tier. Resolved by splitting the two: the ORDER is a snapshot seeded on appear and never re-seeded by a kit change (leaving the tab and returning re-sorts, a moment nobody is mid-gesture in), while the TAGS read a live per-render map — a number printed on a row has to be true when it is read, even while the order it produced is deliberately holding still. The live map also closes the one staleness the rebuild key cannot see, since an equipment-relationship edit moves no count the key watches.

⚠️ **Not on the null kit.** It refuses every add (`setMembership` hard-guards it, and the row has no ADD swipe), so ordering ~100 rows by what they would open, and tagging each one, would be a hundred propositions the surface cannot accept. It falls back to alphabetical through the existing empty-map path, with no second code path.

**Three smaller ones.** The new `rankEquipment` was inserted between `rank` and its doc comment, so `rank`'s contract — including the warning that re-sorting by name would discard a user's routine ordering — ended up attached to the one function that DOES re-sort; both now carry their own, and `rank`'s points at the exception. The tag is sentence case (`Opens 5`) like every sibling data tag, rather than reading "Free weights · opens 5". And it carries a spoken label with the noun: the tag it replaced said "21 exercises" and described itself, where "opens 5" alone does not.

**And the doc budget bound, so the scope narrowed rather than the limit rising.** navigation.md reached 24380 of its 24576 bytes on this round — 196 bytes of headroom, which the next session's first line would have spent, turning `kit-test` red for a docs reason before Swift ever ran. The remedy the budget asks for is a path-scope split, and `today-rail.md` set the precedent the same day: **`catalog-scopes.md`** now holds the scopes/tiers/facets/front-matter laws (9 KB of them), scoped to `CatalogScopeView` and its engine, state and row components rather than to every file under `PlusPlus/Views/**`. navigation.md keeps what it is actually about — where the facet row SITS, the scope control, tab-root chrome, landings — and leaves a pointer. 24380 → 15667, with the new file at 10.5 KB. ⚠️ Neither `today-rail.md` nor `catalog-scopes.md` is named in CLAUDE.md's rules list, which is now two files stale; that list is Dave's to change.

## 2026-08-03 — The catalog front page and Near this are removed; the Kit's unlock ordering stays

**Decision.** Dave cut two of the browse/discovery round's three surfaces. `CatalogFrontPage` and the exercise-detail **NEAR THIS** section are gone, along with the Kit code each needed and nothing else uses: `CatalogReach`/`reach`/`canDo`, and `SimilarityReason`/`reasons`. #251's Kit ordering stays, whole.

**Reason.** Dave's call on what the app should be; there is no defect behind it. Recorded here because the entries above describe two surfaces that no longer exist, and this file is append-only — a reader who finds the 2026-08-02 reasoning needs the next line to say it was undone.

**Not a revert.** #548 landed on top of both and shares code with them, so the removal is surgical rather than two `git revert`s. What earned its place independently and stays: **`KitUnlocks`** (the marginal unlock count, and the invariant test that keeps the Kit row's promise and the equipment screen's "+N" beat agreeing); **`ExerciseFilterState.similarityFeatures`** internal, since both surviving callers need it; **`EquipmentLibrary.genericNamePhrase`**, which `activeKitPhrase` now also reads; **`CatalogScopeView.leadingRows`**, the `some View` boundary extracted for the type-check budget — re-inlining it would walk back toward that budget for nothing; and **`catalog-scopes.md`**, the path-scoped split, which is justified by scope rather than by the size it was triggered at.

⚠️ **`CatalogReachCalculator` is renamed `KitUnlocks`** (`KitUnlocks.byPiece`). A file called `CatalogReach.swift` holding one function about unlocks is named for a concept it no longer contains, and this repo's comments are its record.

**What the removal cost, stated plainly.** The Exercises tab is back to opening on row 1 of 347 alphabetically — 90/90 Hip Switch on a fresh install — and `ExerciseSimilarity` is back to being a tested adjacency graph used only by the "Swap for…" tray. Both are the pre-#546 state, not a regression against it. If browsing is picked up again, the reasoning in the 2026-08-02 entries still holds and the prepend-vs-replace analysis is the part worth re-reading first.

⚠️ **The coverage-map prototypes are stale.** Two of the four directions put the map on the Exercises front page, which no longer exists. Any future round starts by re-choosing a home, and the drawer tray is the remaining candidate.

**One bug found by the removal's review, fixed here rather than left on main.** ⚠️ `equipmentOpensCounts` was missing the `mode.isTab, !isSearchSurface` guard its sibling `seedEquipmentOrder` carries, and `listBody` is shared by the tab, presented and picker mounts — so `Opens N` was replacing the "N exercises" capsule on the PRESENTED equipment catalog (onboarding step 1, the drawer's "Edit your kit", the picker's filter escape) and on SEARCH. Both are wrong for the same reason the presented catalog is a flat alphabetical run at all: it is the ADD surface, where what a piece is FOR is the useful property and a number that moves on every quick-add is exactly the churn that flat run exists to avoid — and on search the tag had nothing behind it, since that surface never orders by unlocks. Three places already said so and disagreed with the code: the call site's own comment, the device-pass rider, and this file's framing of the tag as the CATALOG TIER's. Shipped in #548 and inherited by the removal; the guard now matches its sibling, which also takes a full catalog pass off a surface with a per-keystroke body.
